## XVII A Famous Victory / XVII 一场著名的胜利

===Original===
It was a great day for baseball when the far-famed Calumet club came to Catalpa to play the home nine. The visitors arrived by the evening train and were met at the station by the greater part of the Catalpa club, who escorted their friends to the hotel in which quarters had been engaged. To say that the strangers were objects of curiosity to the youths and lassies of the town would only faintly describe the enthusiasm with which they were received by the people of Catalpa. The morrow was to witness the final game of the struggle, already made sufficiently notable by the narrowness of the margin left for the two contestants, and by the notoriety given to it by the treachery of Ben Burton, now town-talk, but (thanks to the discretion of the players) not known outside of Catalpa.

So high ran the excitement that there were many sleepless youngsters in Catalpa, that night, although the seasoned veterans who were the actors in the drama slept as soundly as though the next day would not dawn, big with the fate of rival baseball clubs. Tom Selby, as his father reported, arose at frequent intervals through the night, looked out on the cloudless sky across which the harvest moon was riding, and went back to his bed with a deep-drawn sigh of satisfaction at the prospect of another fine day for the great match.

It was a beautiful day that lighted up the valley of Stone River; and the mellow October sun flooded the scene with splendor, when the crowds began to flow towards the Agricultural Fair Grounds, now refurbished with great care, and decorated with every available bit of bunting in the place. An enormous throng greeted the sight of the players as they entered the enclosure and made their way directly to the officers’ old rooms, now set apart for the use of the members of the two nines. Special trains had been run on the two railroads entering the town, and from the country round about came long lines of farm-wagons filled with rustic belles and beaux, stalwart young fellows from the rural districts, elder people from outlying villages, and small boys who had heard from afar the news of the great event that was about to happen, and had trudged into town from distant homes, carrying their frugal luncheons with them﻿—all bound to see the sport.

===Chinese===
声名远播的卡柳梅特俱乐部（Calumet club）来到卡塔帕（Catalpa），与本地九人队交锋，这对棒球而言是个盛大的日子。客队乘傍晚的火车抵达，卡塔帕俱乐部的大部人马到车站迎接，把这些朋友护送到早已订好房间的旅馆。若说这些外乡人不过是镇上少男少女们好奇的对象，那还远远不足以形容卡塔帕人迎接他们的热情。翌日将见证这场较量的最后一战——留给两支参赛队伍的余地已如此之窄，加之本·伯顿（Ben Burton）的叛卖行径又为它平添了恶名，此事早已成了镇上街谈巷议的话题，只是（多亏球员们口风紧）还没有传出卡塔帕之外。

当晚，群情激动之下，卡塔帕有许多孩子兴奋得睡不着觉；而那些久经沙场、真正登台唱主角的老将们却睡得香甜，仿佛明天根本不会到来——尽管那个日子满载着两家敌对棒球俱乐部的命运。据汤姆·塞尔比（Tom Selby）的父亲说，汤姆夜里屡屡起身，望着万里无云的夜空和空中高悬的收获月，然后回到床上，深深舒一口气，为大赛又将迎来一个大晴天而心满意足。

照亮斯通河（Stone River）山谷的，是一个大好的晴天；十月醇美的阳光把满目景象洒照得灿烂辉煌，人群开始朝农业博览会场地涌去——场地如今已被精心修葺一新，镇上找得到的小旗子全被拿来做了装点。球员们走进围场、径直前往场地职员们的旧屋（如今辟作两支九人队队员的专用房间）时，一大群人迎着他们的身影张望。进出本镇的两条铁路都加开了专列，四乡则驶来一长列一长列的农用马车，车上坐满乡下的俊俏姑娘和小伙子、乡间来的结实青年、周边村庄的长者，还有那些从老远听说了即将举行的盛事、揣着简便的午饭从遥远的家中一路步行进镇的小男孩们——全都一心要来看这场比赛。

===Original===
There was Judge Howell’s carriage, you may be sure, with the Judge, his pretty daughter, and his prim sister, eager for the sight, even Miss Anstress grimly admitting, as if under great mental pressure, that she did hope that the Catalpas would beat and so have done with what she thought a long and very unnecessary contest for the championship of the State. There, too, was old Rough and Ready, alert and spry as a lad of nineteen, making himself very busy trimming the flags, inspecting the grounds, and running of errands for the players, conscious that but for him the game could not go on. There was a great and tumultuous cheer when the two nines, clad in their uniforms, finally emerged from the unpainted little buildings near the judges’ stand in which they had made ready for the game. Hank Jackson, with what some thought was unparalleled impudence, under the circumstances, but which may have been prompted by a spasm of repentance, stood up on his seat and proposed “three rousing cheers for the Catalpa nine” as that famous organization filed into the Diamond Field. Whereupon, Mr. Heaton, fixing his fond paternal eye on his son, now wearing the uniform of the home club, waved his tall hat and asked for three cheers for the visitors, and these were given with a will.

===Chinese===
你尽可以相信，豪厄尔法官（Judge Howell）的马车也到了，车上坐着法官、他漂亮的女儿和他古板拘谨的妹妹，都一心想看这场比赛；就连安斯特丽丝小姐（Miss Anstress）也板着脸承认——仿佛承受着巨大的思想压力——她确实希望卡塔帕队能赢，好早点结束这场在她看来旷日持久且大可不必的州冠军争夺战。「老硬汉」（Rough and Ready）也在那儿，机敏矫健得像个十九岁的小伙子，忙不迭地修整旗子、检查场地、替球员们跑腿，深信若没有他，这比赛就办不成。当两支九人队身穿队服、终于从裁判台旁那几间未上漆的小屋里走出时（他们就是在那儿更衣备赛的），场中爆发出一阵惊天动地的欢呼。汉克·杰克逊（Hank Jackson）站到座位上，提议「为卡塔帕九人队三呼喝彩」——在那样的情形下，有人觉得这脸皮厚得无以复加，不过这也可能是一时悔悟使然——此时那支赫赫有名的队伍正鱼贯步入内场。紧接着，希顿先生（Mr. Heaton）把他慈父般的目光落在身穿主队队服的儿子身上，挥起那顶大礼帽，请众人为客队三呼喝彩，大家也痛痛快快地喊了三嗓子。

===Original===
“Ah!” sighed Alice, as the Catalpas lost the toss and went to the bat at the direction of their antagonists, “that is a bad sign; but I have made up my mind not to notice any more signs, good, bad, or indifferent.”

“A sensible conclusion, child,” said the aunt. “I have heard that baseball players are as superstitious as sailors, and that is one reason why I think that the game must be debasing to the morals of the players.”

Alice laughed loud and long at this, and even the Judge relaxed his face into a smile as he heard the sage observation of the elderly lady before him.

“Pay attention, Alice,” said her friend Ida, “there goes that handsome Larry to the bat!”

===Chinese===
「啊！」卡塔帕队掷币猜输、按对方的裁定率先上场击球时，爱丽丝（Alice）叹道，「这是个凶兆；不过我已下定决心，什么兆头都不再理会了，管它是吉是凶还是不好不坏。」

「这才是明理的结论，孩子。」姑母说。「我听说棒球球员跟水手一样迷信，这也是我认为这项比赛必然败坏球员道德的原因之一。」

爱丽丝听了大笑不止，就连法官听见眼前这位老太太的高论，绷着的脸也松开成了微笑。

「留神，爱丽丝。」她的朋友艾达（Ida）说，「那位英俊的拉里（Larry）上场击球了！」

===Original===
But it was needless to direct attention to the player. Every eye was fixed on the favorite as he lifted his bat jauntily and took his position with a knowing smile to Sam Morse, the Calumets’ pitcher, as if in recognition of their former contests. But Larry, and Sam Morrison, who succeeded him, failed to hit the ball safely. And Neddie Ellis, who came next to the bat, secured his base only by an error on the part of Captain Ayres, at first base. There was then a chance for the Catalpas to score, but this was destroyed by Charlie King’s going out on a fly. Equally unsuccessful were the Calumets, who now came to the bat with high hopes. Darius Ayres hit a fly to John Brubaker, in the right field, and that vigorous young man neatly captured the ball amid the plaudits of his fellow townsmen, who were plainly glad of the least occasion for hilarity. Sam Morse was retired at first base, and John Handy hit a skyscraper to Neddie Ellis, ending the first inning without a run.

===Chinese===
不过，根本用不着提醒大家去注意这位球员。这位众人的宠儿轻快地举起球棒，朝卡柳梅特队的投手萨姆·莫尔斯（Sam Morse）会心一笑，摆好了击球姿势，仿佛在追认两人昔日交锋的旧谊，此时全场每一双眼睛都盯在他身上。然而拉里，以及随后上场的萨姆·莫里森（Sam Morrison），都没能击出安全的安打。再接下来上场的内迪·埃利斯（Neddie Ellis），也只是靠一垒的艾尔斯队长（Captain Ayres）一次失误才上了垒。这时卡塔帕队本有得分之机，却因查利·金（Charlie King）击出高飞球被接杀而化为泡影。卡柳梅特队满怀希望上场击球，同样一无所获。达赖厄斯·艾尔斯（Darius Ayres）击出一记高飞球飞向右野的约翰·布鲁贝克（John Brubaker），这位精壮的小伙子利落地把球接住，赢得同城乡亲们的喝彩——他们显然巴不得有个由头热闹一番。萨姆·莫尔斯被杀出局于一垒，约翰·汉迪（John Handy）朝内迪·埃利斯击出一记冲天高球，第一局就此结束，双方均无一分。

===Original===
Again both clubs, watching each other with rigid scrutiny, failed to score a run. Each of the nines played a model fielding game and the result was that not a player reached first base in safety. For the Catalpas, Hart Stirling struck out; John Brubaker hit a slow ball to Jamie Kennedy who fielded him out at first base, and Hiram Porter went out on a fly to James McWilliams.

The Calumets were retired with equal precision and celerity, Rob Peabody being thrown out at first base by Albert Heaton, Tom Shoff meeting his fate at the same point at the hands of Hart Stirling, while Glenn Otto failed to hit the ball, although he made three mighty strokes at it.

The third inning began without a run to the credit of either club, and it ended in like manner. The Catalpas went to work with a will that promised to achieve something for their success, but they were forced to yield to the strong fielding game played by the visitors. Al Heaton made his first appearance at the bat, and a little rustle of applause ran around the crowded seats as he stepped lightly to his position. He had been “a little shaky,” as he expressed it confidentially to his friend Larry, but the welcome he received from the spectators gave him a bracing of the muscles, and he hit a hard ball to the right field, where it was captured neatly by Rob Peabody. “The Lily” next tried his best to hit the ball, but he could not send it out of the diamond, and, as Deputy Sheriff Wheeler remarked, “he died at first base.” Larry Boyne fared no better than his predecessors, as he hit up a very easy fly which fell to the lot of Shoff. It was the work of a few minutes to dispose of the Calumets. Jamie Kennedy struck out; Charlie Webb was retired at first base, after hitting a hot ball to Hart Stirling, and McWilliams went down before the deceptive curves of the Catalpas’ pitcher.

===Chinese===
第二局，两队依旧以严苛的目光互相盯防，谁也没能得分。两支九人队的防守都堪称模范，结果竟没有一个球员能安全踏上一垒。卡塔帕队这边，哈特·斯特林（Hart Stirling）三振出局；约翰·布鲁贝克击出一记慢速地滚球，被杰米·肯尼迪（Jamie Kennedy）接起传杀于一垒；海勒姆·波特（Hiram Porter）击出高飞球，被詹姆斯·麦克威廉斯（James McWilliams）接杀出局。

卡柳梅特队也同样精准利落地被解决掉：罗布·皮博迪（Rob Peabody）被阿尔伯特·希顿（Albert Heaton）传杀于一垒，汤姆·肖夫（Tom Shoff）在同一地点栽在哈特·斯特林手里，格伦·奥托（Glenn Otto）虽然挥了三记猛棒，却连球都没碰着。

第三局开始时双方账上都没有一分，结束时也是如此。卡塔帕队鼓足干劲上场，一副要为胜利干出一番名堂的架势，却被客队坚固的防守逼得无从施展。阿尔·希顿（Al Heaton）头一回上场击球，当他轻快地走向自己的位置时，拥挤的看台四周响起一阵细碎的掌声。他曾私下对朋友拉里说自己「有点发怵」，但观众给他的欢迎使他浑身筋骨为之一振，他击出一记又急又猛的球飞向右野，被罗布·皮博迪利落地接住。「百合」（The Lily）接着奋力挥棒，却无法把球打出内场，正如惠勒副治安官（Deputy Sheriff Wheeler）所说，「他死在一垒上」。拉里·博因（Larry Boyne）也不比前几位走运，他击出一记极易接杀的高飞球，落进了肖夫的手心。打发卡柳梅特队出场只花了几分钟工夫：杰米·肯尼迪三振出局；查利·韦伯（Charlie Webb）击出一记烫手的急球飞向哈特·斯特林后，被杀出局于一垒；麦克威廉斯则败倒在卡塔帕队投手变幻莫测的弧线球下。

===Original===
“Three innings and not a run yet!” was the exclamation of Miss Ida Boardman. “Why, both clubs seem to be watching each other as a cat would watch a mouse! I wonder if either will score a run in this game? If they don’t, I shall feel as if my time was wasted, shan’t you, Alice?”

But Miss Alice, with a demure glance at her aunt, who beheld the field with a listless manner, declared that the playing was simply splendid, and she pitied anybody who could not appreciate the wonderful fielding of the two clubs. She wished victory for the home nine; but she could not withhold her generous praise for the fine playing of the visitors.

===Chinese===
「三局了，还一分没得！」艾达·博德曼小姐（Miss Ida Boardman）叫了起来。「哎呀，这两支队简直像猫盯着耗子似的互相盯着！我倒要看看，这场球里还有没有人能得上一分？要是再不得分，我就觉得我这工夫算白费了，你不觉得吗，爱丽丝？」

爱丽丝小姐却端庄地瞥了一眼懒洋洋望着球场的姑母，宣称这场球打得实在精彩，谁要是不懂得欣赏两队出神入化的防守，她真要替那人惋惜。她盼着主队获胜，但对客队的漂亮球艺，她也毫不吝惜慷慨的赞美。

===Original===
When Sam Morrison went to the bat for the Catalpas, there was on his face a look of determination that indicated mischief, as his admirers said among themselves. “The Lily” said, “It is high time that something was done, and we must be the first to send a man across the plate.” Sam hit a difficult grounder to Handy, who allowed the base runner to reach the first bag in safety, by making a poor throw to Ayres, after accomplishing a first-rate stop, at third base. Neddie Ellis made his first base hit of the game, and this advanced Morrison to third base.

The next two strikers, Charlie King and Hart Stirling, threw a gloom over the spirits of the Catalpas and their allies sitting in rapt silence in the benches around, by going out at first base. As John Brubaker, the redoubtable, handled his bat in this inning, the attention of the spectators was fixed on him when he took his position. The eyes of Sam Morrison and Neddie Ellis were also riveted on John; the former was on third base, and Neddie had succeeded in reaching the second bag in safety. Anxiously did they wait to be sent around homewards. John hit a ball over the head of Tom Shoff which secured him two bases and his club the same number of runs, as Morrison and Neddie finished the circuit of the bases on this timely hit of the right fielder of the home nine. A great roar of applause went up from the assemblage, and the moisture gathered in the eyes of some of the more impressionable of the fair ones among the spectators. It was an auspicious moment for the Catalpas. The spirits of the onlookers were slightly dampened, however, by Captain Hiram’s being put out, which ended this half of the inning.

===Chinese===
轮到萨姆·莫里森为卡塔帕队上场击球时，他脸上那副坚决的神色透着一股不怀好意——他的崇拜者们私下这么议论。「百合」说：「早该有点动静了，送人踏过本垒的必须是我们先来。」萨姆朝汉迪击出一记刁钻的地滚球，汉迪在三垒边完成了一次一流的扑接，随后传给艾尔斯的球却失了准头，让跑垒员安然踏上一垒。内迪·埃利斯击出本场自己的第一支安打，把莫里森送上了三垒。

接下来的两名击球员查利·金和哈特·斯特林双双被杀于一垒，让卡塔帕队以及屏息默坐在四周看台上的拥护者们心头一沉。轮到威名赫赫的约翰·布鲁贝克在本局执棒时，他一站上击球位置，观众的目光便都聚在他身上。萨姆·莫里森和内迪·埃利斯的眼睛也紧盯着约翰：前者在三垒上，内迪也已安然上到二垒。两人焦灼地等着被送绕垒回本。约翰击出一记越过汤姆·肖夫头顶的球，为自己挣得两个垒，也为俱乐部挣得同样数目的两分——随着主队右野手这记及时安打，莫里森和内迪一口气跑完了绕垒全程。全场爆发出雷鸣般的掌声，观众中几位心软易感的女士眼里都湿润了。这对卡塔帕队是个吉利的时刻。不过，海勒姆队长的被封杀出局让旁观者的兴致稍稍受挫，这半局也随之结束。

===Original===
Nor was the scoring of runs to be confined to one club. The Calumets, in their half of the inning, also “broke the ice,” as Rob Peabody expressed it to Shoff. Captain Darius hit the first ball pitched and it yielded him a base hit. Sam Morse struck up an easy fly which fell before the skillful fielding of Sam Morrison. Next to the bat came John Handy, who imitated the example of John Brubaker, sending home his captain on a two-base hit. Rob Peabody took his base on called balls, but was put out by a neat double play. Tom Shoff hit a ball to Al Heaton who threw it to Stirling, who put out Peabody and then threw it to first base in time to head off Thomas; and the fourth inning was closed with the Catalpas two to one for their competitors. Whereat there was a thundering round of applause from the partial spectators.

Inspired by this token of their success, the sons of Catalpa went cheerily to the bat and began what proved to be a fruitless attempt to increase the lead of their club. Albert Heaton, their first striker, made a base hit and reached second base on a bad throw by Charlie Webb, but he was left there, as “The Lily,” Larry Boyne, and Sam Morrison were all retired at first base. Here the Calumets played a first-rate game and ran the bases in fine style, taking advantage of two errors committed by their opponents, which allowed them to score the single run needed to put them on even terms. Glenn Otto, the first striker, went out on a fly to Larry Boyne. The next man to the bat was Jamie Kennedy, who hit a line ball to Sam Morrison, who fumbled it and allowed the base runner to reach the first bag safely. Kennedy then succeeded in reaching the second base by a passed ball, and was sent across the home plate by Charlie Webb, who struck the ball for a base hit. McWilliams went out on a foul fly to “The Lily,” and Darius Ayres ended the inning, being fielded out at first base.

===Chinese===
得分也不独归一队。卡柳梅特队在自己的半局里同样「打破了坚冰」——罗布·皮博迪对肖夫如此形容。达赖厄斯队长击中投来的头一记球，得了一支安打。萨姆·莫尔斯击出一记轻易的高飞球，落进萨姆·莫里森娴熟的防守之手。接下来上场击球的是约翰·汉迪，他效法约翰·布鲁贝克的榜样，以一支二垒安打把队长送回了本垒。罗布·皮博迪靠坏球保送上了一垒，却在一记干净利落的双杀中被杀出局。汤姆·肖夫把球击向阿尔·希顿，希顿传给斯特林，斯特林封杀皮博迪，随即又及时把球传往一垒，赶在托马斯之前把他堵杀出局；第四局就此结束，卡塔帕队两分，对手一分。偏袒主队的观众闻声爆出一阵雷霆般的掌声。

受这一成功之兆的鼓舞，卡塔帕的子弟们欢欢喜喜地执棒上阵，开始了一番为本队扩大领先的尝试，结果却证明是徒劳。他们的第一位击球员阿尔伯特·希顿击出安打，又借查利·韦伯一次传坏球上了二垒，却被晾在了那里——「百合」、拉里·博因和萨姆·莫里森接连被杀出局于一垒。这一局里卡柳梅特队打得极为出色，跑垒跑得漂亮，利用对手的两次失误，拿下了把比分拉平所必需的那一分。他们的第一位击球员格伦·奥托击出高飞球，被拉里·博因接杀。下一个上场击球的是杰米·肯尼迪，他击出一记平直球飞向萨姆·莫里森，莫里森接球失手，让跑垒员安然上了一垒。肯尼迪又借一次捕手漏接上了二垒，随后查利·韦伯击出安打，把他送过了本垒。麦克威廉斯击出界外高飞球被「百合」接杀，达赖厄斯·艾尔斯被防守传杀于一垒，这一局到此结束。

===Original===
In the sixth inning, the Catalpas once more took the lead. Neddie Ellis led off with a base hit and was followed by Charlie King, who secured his base by an error on the part of Glenn Otto. Hart Stirling went out on a fly to Rob Peabody and was followed at the bat by John Brubaker, who hit safely and so sent in Neddie Ellis amidst the cheers of the excited spectators, now fairly alive with enthusiasm. Hiram Porter was thrown out at first base, and Al Heaton hit a long fly to McWilliams, which the latter deftly captured, and the crowd, apparently anxious to seem impartial, loudly applauded the catch.

The Calumets failed to tally one in their half of this inning. Sam Morrison made a base hit and Peabody went to first on a trifling error by Captain Porter, but Handy, Shoff and Glenn Otto were retired in quick succession, the first-named at first base and the other two on high flies to the outfielders.

===Chinese===
第六局，卡塔帕队再次取得领先。内迪·埃利斯以一支安打开局，随后是查利·金，他借格伦·奥托的一次失误上了垒。哈特·斯特林击出高飞球被罗布·皮博迪接杀，接着上场击球的是约翰·布鲁贝克，他击出安全安打，把内迪·埃利斯送回了本垒，兴奋的观众齐声欢呼，此刻他们的热情已全然沸腾。海勒姆·波特被传杀于一垒，阿尔·希顿朝麦克威廉斯击出一记长远的高飞球，被后者灵巧地接住，人群显然急于显得不偏不倚，也为这次接球大声喝彩。

卡柳梅特队在他们这半局里一分未得。萨姆·莫里森击出一支安打，皮博迪借波特队长一次微小的失误上了一垒，但汉迪、肖夫和格伦·奥托接连被杀出局——头一位被杀于一垒，后两位击出的高飞球都被外野手接杀。

===Original===
Once more the Catalpas added to their score, the glory of making a home run falling this time to “The Lily.” Coming to the square, he swung his ashen bat over his shoulder, and selecting a “drop ball,” he hit with a will and with all his might, and the sphere flew far over the center fielder’s head, giving the gratified catcher of the home nine the first and only home run of the game. Before the ball could be returned to the diamond, Van Orman had cleared the circuit of the bases, and, as he seated himself breathlessly on the players’ bench, he was greeted with a hearty round of cheers from the excited throng. Cries of “Good for ‘The Lily’ of Catalpa!” burst from the multitude, and Ida Boardman waved her scarf at the bashful William, who detected the compliment from his post on the opposite side of the amphitheater.

“Get up, Bill, and show yourself proud!” cried Neddie Ellis. “You have won an encore.” At this, Bill heaved up his burly form, doffed his cap and grimly bowed to the spectators, who cheered him more wildly than ever.

===Chinese===
卡塔帕队再度添分，这次立下本垒打功勋的是「百合」。他走上击球位置，把白蜡木球棒抡过肩头，瞅准一记「下坠球」，全力以赴、使出浑身力气猛然一击，那白球远远飞越中外野手的头顶，让主队的捕手心满意足地收获了本场比赛第一支也是唯一一支本垒打。球还没能传回内场，范奥曼（Van Orman）已经跑完了绕垒一周，气喘吁吁地在球员席上坐下，激动的人群朝他爆出一阵由衷的欢呼。「好样的，卡塔帕的『百合』！」的喊声从人群中迸出，艾达·博德曼朝那位腼腆的威廉（William）挥动围巾，而他正从环形看台对面自己的位置上察觉到了这份恭维。

「起来呀，比尔，昂首挺胸让大家瞧瞧！」内迪·埃利斯喊道。「你可赢得了一次返场。」闻言，比尔撑起他魁伟的身躯，脱下帽子，一本正经地向观众们鞠了一躬，观众们欢呼得比先前更狂热了。

===Original===
But Larry, who now took his bat to the square, was the cynosure of all eyes. Somehow, the confidence of the great assembly was with him always, even as their affection seemed lavished on peachy-cheeked Neddie Ellis. But Larry failed to win the plaudits that would have readily followed the least pretext for a burst of applause. He made a single hit, but did not score a run, as Sam Morrison, Neddie Ellis and Charlie King were rapidly retired, one after another. In this inning, the Calumets succeeded in keeping themselves within one run of their opponents. Jamie Kennedy made a two-base hit, and, after Charlie Webb and James McWilliams were retired at first base, they scored a run which was achieved by Captain Darius Ayres making a base hit. Sam Morrison ended the inning by going out on a liner to Larry Boyne.

===Chinese===
这时执棒走上击球位置的拉里，成了全场万众瞩目的焦点。不知怎的，广大观众的信心总是寄托在他身上，正如他们的疼爱似乎都倾注在面若桃腮的内迪·埃利斯身上。然而拉里这回没能赢得喝彩——哪怕给他一星半点的由头，掌声都会毫不迟疑地响起。他击出一支一垒安打，却没有得分，因为萨姆·莫里森、内迪·埃利斯和查利·金被接连迅速地杀出局。在这一局里，卡柳梅特队成功把自己与对手的差距保持在一分之内。杰米·肯尼迪击出一支二垒安打，在查利·韦伯和詹姆斯·麦克威廉斯先后被杀出局于一垒之后，达赖厄斯·艾尔斯队长以一支安打为他们拿下一分。萨姆·莫里森击出一记平直球落到拉里·博因手中，被接杀出局，这一局方告结束。

===Original===
The score now stood four to three in favor of the Catalpas, and as “The Lily” sagely remarked, “It’s anybody’s game.” The home club tried every possible maneuver to increase their lead; but all was in vain. The contest was now drawing to a close, and the least bit of luck falling into the hands of the visiting nine would carry them so far ahead that defeat would be inevitable for the Catalpa club. Hart Stirling, John Brubaker, and Hiram Porter, the first three strikers for the home club, went out very quickly in the order named. Then the Calumets came to the bat with high hopes of securing at least the one run needed to bring them up to an even score with their adversaries. But they, too, were doomed to disappointment. John Handy, Rob Peabody, and Tom Shoff were put out in “one-two-three order,” so skillful was the fielding and so accurate the throwing of Larry Boyne, Hart Stirling, and Al Heaton.

===Chinese===
比分现在是四比三，卡塔帕队领先，正如「百合」颇为在理地说的：「鹿死谁手，犹未可知。」主队想尽一切办法扩大领先优势，却都徒劳无功。比赛此时已近尾声，哪怕一丁点运气落入客队手中，都会让他们遥遥领先，卡塔帕队的败局将不可避免。主队的前三位击球员哈特·斯特林、约翰·布鲁贝克和海勒姆·波特，按这个次序很快便先后出局。随后卡柳梅特队满怀希望地上场击球，指望至少拿下把比分追平所需的那一分。但他们也注定要失望。约翰·汉迪、罗布·皮博迪和汤姆·肖夫被「一二三」接连解决——拉里·博因、哈特·斯特林和阿尔·希顿的防守如此娴熟，传球如此精准。

===Original===
“The last inning! The last inning!” cried Miss Alice, gleefully clapping her hands, “and the Catalpas are first at the bat with a lead of one to their credit! Oh, I do hope that Albert will make a run! I know he will! Look at him where he stands! Isn’t he handsome, Aunt Anstress?”

Miss Anstress Howell turned her cool glance in the direction of the Diamond Field, and looking at Albert, said that she was not sure whether a young man could be called good-looking in those singularly ill-fitting and peculiar clothes that ballplayers wore; but she was interested in the game, as a whole, she said, without any special interest in the players as individuals. She took in the performance without any thought for the men who carried it forward. “You are a kind of overseeing providence, Anstress?” said the Judge.

===Chinese===
「最后一局！最后一局！」爱丽丝小姐欢呼着拍手，「卡塔帕队先上场击球，账上还带着一分的领先！哦，我真希望阿尔伯特能得一分！我知道他一定行！瞧他站在那儿的模样！他是不是很英俊，安斯特丽丝姑妈？」

安斯特丽丝·豪厄尔小姐把冷淡的目光转向内场，看了看阿尔伯特，说一个年轻人穿着棒球球员那种出奇不合身又古里古怪的衣服，还能不能称得上英俊，她可拿不准；不过就整体而言，她对这场比赛是有兴趣的——她说——只是对球员个人并无特别的兴趣。她观看着这场比赛，对推动比赛进行的那些人毫无挂怀。「你可真像一位总揽全局的天意啊，安斯特丽丝。」法官说。

===Original===
While they were talking, a murmur, only a murmur, of conversation swept around the crowded enclosure, and everybody seemed to be saying to his neighbor that this was the conclusive and crucial moment in the struggle. All eyes were intent on Al Heaton, and even grown men held their breath, as, with close tension of every nerve, they watched the movements of the players in the field. Tom Selby, attended by his faithful satellite, Mike Costigan, who had a holiday, gazed with admiring eyes at his demigod, Albert Heaton, and so still was the air, now soft and warm and dimmed by the lustrous October haze, that one might have heard a leaf drop, as Bill Van Orman eloquently expressed it, afterwards.

===Chinese===
他们说话的当口，一阵低语——只是低语——掠过拥挤的围场，人人似乎都在对邻座说，这正是这场较量的定局关头。所有的目光都聚在阿尔·希顿身上，就连成年汉子也屏住了呼吸，每根神经都紧紧绷着，盯住场上球员的一举一动。汤姆·塞尔比由他忠实的跟班迈克·科斯蒂根（Mike Costigan）陪着——迈克这天放假——满怀仰慕地凝望着他的半人神阿尔伯特·希顿；四下静得——正如比尔·范奥曼（Bill Van Orman）事后雄辩地形容的那样——十月辉煌的薄雾使空气变得柔暖朦胧，连一片叶子落地都听得见。

===Original===
Albert patiently waited for a good ball, and when he saw one come, at last, he sent the sphere out of the reach of Glenn Otto and placed a base hit to his credit. Next came “The Lily” who hit the very first ball pitched, for two bases, and, with a volley of ah‑h‑h‑s following him, sent in Al Heaton to the home plate. Larry came next in order, and pretty Alice Howell felt a quickening of her pulse and her color glowing as she saw the resolute and sturdy figure of the favorite of the club shouldering his bat and striding to position. Larry made a safe hit to the right field, sending in “The Lily,” and securing his own base. Sam Morrison was put out at first while Larry shot to second base. Then Neddie Ellis went out on a fly to Rob Peabody, and Charlie King ended the inning for the Catalpas, by striking out, leaving Larry on third base, to which he had stolen meanwhile.

===Chinese===
阿尔伯特耐心地等候一记好球，终于等到一记来球时，他把白球击到格伦·奥托够不着的地方，为自己记上了一支安打。接下来是「百合」，他把投来的头一记球就打了出去，击出一支二垒安打，在一串「啊——啊——」的喊声护送下，把阿尔·希顿送回了本垒。按顺序轮到拉里，俊俏的爱丽丝·豪厄尔看见俱乐部这位宠儿扛起球棒、大步走向击球位置的那坚定结实的身影，只觉脉搏加速、双颊发烫。拉里朝右野击出一记安全的安打，把「百合」送回本垒，自己也稳稳占住了垒包。萨姆·莫里森被杀出局于一垒之际，拉里已蹿上了二垒。随后内迪·埃利斯击出高飞球被罗布·皮博迪接杀，查利·金三振出局，卡塔帕队的这一局到此结束——把在此期间已盗上三垒的拉里留在了垒上。

===Original===
The Catalpas now had a lead of three, and the Calumets came to the bat with lugubrious faces. “But I have seen sicker children than this get well,” was Captain Ayres’s philosophical remark, as Glenn Otto went to the bat for the visiting club.

The Catalpas went to the field with an elation which they could hardly conceal, and with a tolerably firm belief in their victory. They handled the ball with a dexterity almost unexampled, even for them, and speedily put a damper on any hopes that the Calumets might have cherished. Glenn Otto went out on a fly to John Brubaker. Jamie Kennedy was thrown out at first base by Hart Stirling, and Charlie Webb ended the game by hitting a hot ball to Larry Boyne who made a lightning throw to first base, before any of the spectators could see what had become of the ball, so swift and agile were his motions.

===Chinese===
卡塔帕队现在领先三分，卡柳梅特队愁眉苦脸地上场击球。「不过，我可见过病得比这更重的孩子又活蹦乱跳了呢。」格伦·奥托替客队走上击球位置时，艾尔斯队长说了这么一句达观的话。

卡塔帕队上场防守时几乎掩不住内心的欢腾，对胜利怀着相当坚定的信念。他们处理球的手法之娴熟，即便对他们自己也近乎空前，很快就浇灭了卡柳梅特队可能还抱有的一切希望。格伦·奥托击出高飞球，被约翰·布鲁贝克接杀。杰米·肯尼迪被哈特·斯特林传杀于一垒，查利·韦伯击出一记烫手的急球飞向拉里·博因，为比赛画上了句号——拉里闪电般把球传向一垒，观众还没看清球的去向，球已到了，他的动作就是这般迅捷灵巧。

===Original===
A great cheer burst forth from the multitude. The umpire superfluously cried “Game” in the midst of a deafening uproar, and, as the two captains advanced towards each other to clasp hands, the Catalpas, relieving their pent-up enthusiasm with a wild yell, swooped down upon Larry Boyne, whose brilliant play had terminated the game, and, seizing him bodily, carried him above their heads, shouting “Hurrah for the ‘Curly-headed Cat!’ ” as they swung around and round the Diamond Field. Men and boys whooped and shouted, women waved handkerchiefs and parasols, and numberless small boys shrilly added to the din. Truly it was a great day for Catalpa.

===Chinese===
人群爆发出巨大的欢呼。裁判在震耳欲聋的喧嚣声中多此一举地喊了声「比赛结束」，两位队长相向趋前握手之际，卡塔帕队员们一声狂吼，宣泄出胸中积压的热情，朝拉里·博因猛扑过去——正是他辉煌的一守为比赛画上了句号——他们把他整个人抱起来，举过头顶，一边绕着内场转了一圈又一圈，一边高喊「为『卷毛猫』三呼万岁！」男人和男孩们又吼又叫，女士们挥舞着手帕和阳伞，数不清的小孩尖声添着热闹。对卡塔帕来说，这真是盛大的日子。

===Original===
For a moment, Alice could not trust herself to speak. And when, with unsteady voice, she responded to her father’s delighted comments, he looked at her with surprise and said,

“Why, Alice, my child, I believe you are crying!”

“For joy, papa,” was all she said. Just then, the lads, still carrying Larry, with flushed face and sparkling eyes, his curly hair ruffled by his unwonted treatment, surged towards the Judge’s carriage. Alice extended her hand, and their eyes met with one swift glance of unspeakable elation. The Judge looked on with benignant approbation, an unusual lump rising in his throat as he regarded with unaffected admiration the young athlete who had carried off the honors of the day.

===Chinese===
有一会儿，爱丽丝不敢让自己开口说话。待她声音发颤地回应父亲喜滋滋的议论时，父亲诧异地看着她，说：

「哎呀，爱丽丝，我的孩子，我看你是哭了！」

「这是喜极而泣，爸爸。」她只说了这一句。就在这时，小伙子们还抬着拉里，朝法官的马车涌来。拉里满脸通红、双眼放光，卷发被这番不寻常的待遇弄得蓬乱。爱丽丝伸出手去，两人的目光相碰，交换了一个难以言喻的欢喜眼神。法官慈祥而赞许地望着这一幕，喉咙里涌起一阵不寻常的哽咽，以毫不掩饰的钦佩注视着这位赢得了当日全部荣耀的年轻球员。

===Original===
“You are to be congratulated very heartily, Mr. Boyne,” he said. “Our club has won a famous victory, and it is a proud thing for you that your associates fix upon you as the noblest warrior of them all.”

With more cheers and congratulations, the assembly slowly dispersed, the booming of an anvil salute falling on their ears as the men, women and children of Catalpa descended the hill to the town. And in the records of that proud community was written this score:—

===Chinese===
「应当向你致以最衷心的祝贺，博因先生。」他说。「我们的俱乐部赢得了一场著名的胜利，而你的伙伴们推举你为他们当中最英勇的战士，这对你可是件值得自豪的事。」

在更多的欢呼和道贺声中，人群缓缓散去，卡塔帕的男男女女和孩子下山回镇时，耳边回荡着铁砧礼炮的轰鸣。在这个自豪的社区的史册上，记下了这样一份比分：——

===Original===
All these things happened years ago. It would be difficult for any inquiring stranger to gather the threads of the narrative herein set forth. Even the name of the Calumet baseball club disappeared from the roll of the League, after that once-famous organization had been reconstructed, merged, and re-reconstructed. The title of the Catalpa Baseball Club has survived time’s changes, but the founders of the club are now sedate upholders of the dignity and credit of their city, with little time or inclination for athletic sports. Their successors cherish with just pride the traditions of the early achievements of the club, and the titles of the original nine are carried with due respect for those who first wore them. The visitor in Catalpa would note many changes in the busy western town from which the famous baseball club went forth to conquer. Judge Howell has left the bench; and he and his daughter Alice have taken to themselves a partner, whose name appears on a signboard bearing the inscription—

> **Howell & Boyne**, Attorneys at Law.

===Chinese===
这一切都是多年前的事了。任何好奇的外乡人都难以把本书所述故事的线索收拢齐全。就连卡柳梅特棒球俱乐部的名字，也在那个曾经显赫一时的组织几经改组、合并、再改组之后，从联盟的名册上消失了。卡塔帕棒球俱乐部的名号历经岁月变迁而犹存，但俱乐部的创始人们如今已是端庄持重的体面人士，支撑着本市的尊严与信誉，既无时间也无兴致顾及体育运动。他们的后继者怀着应有的自豪，珍视俱乐部早年战绩的传统，原班九人队的名号也带着对最初佩戴它们的人的应有敬意而沿用至今。来到卡塔帕的游客会注意到，这座走出著名棒球俱乐部去夺取胜利的繁忙西部城镇已经变了许多。豪厄尔法官已离开法官席；他和女儿爱丽丝给自己添了一位合伙人，此人的名字出现在一块招牌上，招牌上写着——

> **豪厄尔—博因，律师事务所（Howell & Boyne, Attorneys at Law）**。

===Original===
Of a summer afternoon, when the cares of business may be laid down for a while, ’Squire Boyne, as he is called by his fellow-townsmen, may sometimes be found seated in the outer rim of the well-appointed amphitheater of the Catalpa grounds, with other battle-scarred veterans around him, watching the mimic combat in the field below, and telling once more **How our Baseball Club won the Championship**.

===Chinese===
夏日午后，当生意上的操劳可以暂且搁下时，镇民们口中的博因老爷（’Squire Boyne）有时会坐在卡塔帕球场那设施精良的环形看台的外圈，身边围着一群久经战阵、伤痕累累的老将，望着下方场中那演习般的战斗，再一次讲起**我们的棒球俱乐部是怎样赢得冠军的**。

## II A Scrub Game / 第二章 一场杂牌赛

===Original===
Notwithstanding the gloom of the morning, the day came off bright and fine, and by the time the train was due from the West, bringing the Jonesville boys, the weather was perfect. A serene October sky bent over Catalpa, and the bright river flowed rippling toward the Mississippi, its banks red and yellow with autumnal foliage. Crossing the bridge from North Catalpa and from the farming settlements to the north were strings of buggies, lumber-wagons and other vehicles; and not a few sightseers jogged along on horseback, all with their faces set toward the Agricultural Fair Grounds, just above the town and lying to the southward. Catalpa is built on a slope that descends from the rolling prairie to the bank of Stone River. Once out of the town, one reaches a lovely stretch of undulating ground skirted by a dead level plain, admirably adapted for a baseball field. The original use of the Fair Grounds had almost been forgotten when the ball clubs of Catalpa began to practice within the enclosure. The Northern District fair had gone farther North, and the grounds were left to chance comers﻿—a travelling circus, or an occasional amateur racing match.

Today, the blue and white flag of the Catalpas floated proudly from what had once been the Judges’ stand, while the pale green colors of the Jonesvillians hung lazily from a staff driven into the ground to the westward of the track. For more than an hour before the time set for the calling of the game, a steady stream of people poured into the enclosure. The battered and rickety seats had been patched up to bear the weight of those who were willing to pay the small fee exacted for the privilege; but the mass of the spectators were grouped together in the open spaces to the westward and southward of these, and farther around the ring was a thin line of vehicles of various descriptions. Men and women on horseback, young girls crowded into wagon-boxes, and boys ramping around on scrubby mustangs, filled up the background.

It was a pretty sight. And while the crowd waited for the hour to arrive, much scientific baseball gossip drifted about the enclosure. Village lads who had worked hard or had teased with uncommon assiduity to secure the “two bits” needed to gain admission to the grounds, chaffed each other vociferously and exchanged learned comments on the playing and the qualities of the combatants.

“Oh you should have seen John Brubaker play right field that day when the Catalpas sent the Jonesvillers home with a big headache,” said one of these small critics, as he viewed with admiration Brubaker’s stalwart form reclining at ease in the shade of the judges’ stand. “Why he just everlastingly got away with the ball every time one of the Jonesvillers gave him one. Then there was Lew Morris, there’s no player in the Jonesvillers, ’cept it is Larry Boyne, that can catch a ball like Lew, and why the Catalpas keep him in the left field, I don’t know.”

===Chinese===
尽管清晨还是阴沉沉的，这一天到头来却晴朗和煦；等到从西边载着琼斯维尔小伙子们的火车该进站时，天气已经好得不能再好了。十月宁静的天空笼罩着卡塔帕（Catalpa），明亮的河水淙淙流向密西西比河，两岸的秋叶红黄相间。从北卡塔帕和北边各农垦区过桥而来的，是一辆接一辆的单马轻便马车、运木料车和各式车辆；还有不少看热闹的人骑着马缓缓而行，个个都朝着镇子上方偏南的农业博览会场地而去。卡塔帕镇坐落在一片缓坡上，地势从起伏的大草原一直降到斯通河（Stone River）岸边。一出镇子，便是一片宜人的起伏地带，边缘环绕着一马平川的平原，用来做棒球场再合适不过。等到卡塔帕的各棒球俱乐部开始在这围场里练球时，这博览会场原先的用途几乎已被人们忘了个干净。北区农博会早已北迁，场子便留给了各路不速之客——巡回马戏团，或是偶尔来上一场的业余赛马会。

这一天，卡塔帕队蓝白两色的队旗在昔日的赛马裁判台上骄傲地飘扬，琼斯维尔佬浅绿色的旗帜则懒洋洋地挂在跑道西侧一根插进地里的旗杆上。离宣布开赛还有一个多钟头，人流便络绎不绝地涌进围场。破破烂烂、摇摇晃晃的看台座椅已经修补过，好承受那些肯为这特权掏一点小钱的看客的重量；但大多数观众都聚在座椅以西以南的空地上，再往外一圈，环着场地停着稀稀拉拉一列各式车辆。骑马的男男女女、挤在货车车厢里的年轻姑娘，还有骑着矮种马四处撒欢的男孩们，构成了远处的背景。

这真是一幅好看的景象。趁大伙儿等着开赛的钟点，围场里四处飘荡着各种内行的棒球高论。村里的少年们，有的拼命干活、有的缠磨大人缠得格外卖力，才凑足进场所需的那两毛五分钱，此刻正扯着嗓子相互打趣，就交战双方的球艺和本事高谈阔论。

“哦，那天卡塔帕队把琼斯维尔佬打得灰头土脸地回家去，你真该看看约翰·布鲁贝克（John Brubaker）守右外场的样子，”其中一位小小的评论家说道，一边艳羡地望着布鲁贝克那壮硕的身躯正悠然靠在裁判台的荫凉里。“嘿，琼斯维尔佬每回送他一个球，他就准能把球对付得妥妥帖帖。还有卢·莫里斯（Lew Morris），琼斯维尔队里除了拉里·博因（Larry Boyne），谁也接不住卢那样的球，卡塔帕队干嘛把他放在左外场，我可弄不明白。”

===Original===
“Oh you talk too much with your mouth, you, Bill, you,” cried a bigger baseball connoisseur. “What do you know about the game? Why, I saw the Jonesvillians, three years ago, when they first played the old Catalpas, I mean the soldier boys. That was playing, now I tell you. Hurrah! There comes the Nine!”

Pretty Alice Howell, sitting in her father’s carriage and accompanied by the Judge and her severe-looking aunt, Miss Anstress, clapped her hands at the sight, for the two Nines drew near to each other and the game was called. The dignified Judge smiled at the girl’s enthusiasm, but, as he looked around, he saw that multitudes of other young ladies, as well as ladies no longer young﻿—mothers and aged spinsters, watched the preliminaries of the game with absorbing interest.

===Chinese===
“哦，你这张嘴就晓得穷唠叨，说你呢，比尔（Bill），就是你，”一个大块头的棒球行家嚷道。“你懂什么球？告诉你，三年前琼斯维尔佬头一回跟老卡塔帕队——我是说那帮当过兵的小伙子——交手的时候，我可是亲眼瞧见的。那才叫打球哩！好哇！九人队来啦！”

秀丽的爱丽丝·豪厄尔（Alice Howell）坐在父亲的马车上，由法官和神情严肃的姑母安斯特丽丝小姐（Miss Anstress）陪着；见了这光景，她不由拍起手来，因为两支九人队已彼此走近，比赛正式宣布开始。庄重的法官见女儿这般兴高采烈，不由微微一笑；可他环顾四周，发现许许多多别的年轻小姐，连同那些不再年轻的女士们——做母亲的和上了年纪的老小姐们——也都全神贯注地盯着赛前的种种准备。

===Original===
The Jonesville Nine were not so well developed, physically, as the Catalpas. They were mostly farmer’s sons, born and bred on the low prairies to the westward of Stone River. It is a region long famous for its prevailing fever-and-ague epidemic. The sallow faces of some of the Jonesville players suggested quinine and “cholagogue,” just then a favorite specific among the ague-smitten population of Northern Illinois. Nor were the members of the visiting Nine as uniform in size and appearance as the Catalpas. The breadth of chest and vigorous outline of the home nine were not repeated in the forms of the Jonesville boys.

The Catalpas were well chosen with an eye to symmetry and uniformity. They were all brawny and athletic young fellows. As they were mostly men of leisure, they had had plenty of time to practice, and they were apparently ready to give good account of themselves. Chiefly on Al Heaton, the stalwart catcher, did the eyes of the multitude rest with favor. He was a tall, shapely young fellow, with a ruddy and oval face, bright brown eyes, a keen glance, and a sinewy length of limb that gave him preeminence in the field.

The batting game of the Catalpas was better than that of the Jonesvillians, as all previous encounters had shown. But the fielding of the Jonesville boys was far better than that of any other nine with whom they had measured their strength and skill. And Larry Boyne, a fresh-faced and laughing young man from Sugar Grove, but a member of the Jonesville Nine, was the champion catcher of the whole region. So long as the Jonesville Nine held on to Larry, they felt sure of victory. Larry Boyne was a trifle shorter than the average of his comrades. His round and well-poised head was covered with a shock of curly flaxen hair, and his sturdy legs, muscular arms and ample chest gave token of a large stock of reserved power. “That’s the best looking Jonesvillian of them all” was the secret thought of many an observant girl and the open criticism of many a loud-talking spectator.

===Chinese===
论体格，琼斯维尔九人队不及卡塔帕队那么匀称发达。他们多半是庄家汉的儿子，生在斯通河以西的低平草原上，长在斯通河以西的低平草原上。那一带地方久以疟疾流行为名。琼斯维尔队里有几张蜡黄的面孔，一看便知是奎宁和“利胆药水”喂出来的——那时节，这玩意儿正是北伊利诺伊州害寒热病的人们最爱用的灵丹。客队队员的身量长相也不如卡塔帕队那么整齐划一。主队队员那宽阔的胸膛和健壮的体格，在琼斯维尔小伙子身上是找不到的。

卡塔帕队队员都是照着匀称划一的标准精心挑选的，个个都是筋骨结实、体格健壮的年轻人。他们多半是有闲阶层，练球的时间充裕得很，眼下显然已准备好大显身手。众人的目光多半都青睐地落在魁伟的捕手阿尔·希顿（Al Heaton）身上。他是个身材颀长、体形漂亮的年轻人，红润的鹅蛋脸，明亮的棕色眼睛，目光锐利，四肢修长有力，使他在球场上出类拔萃。

以往历次交锋都表明，卡塔帕队的击球比琼斯维尔队强。但琼斯维尔小伙子们的防守功夫，远胜过与他们较量过力气和球艺的任何一支九人队。而拉里·博因——舒格格罗夫（Sugar Grove）来的一个面色红润、满脸笑意的年轻人，却是琼斯维尔九人队的队员——是方圆一带最出色的捕手。只要琼斯维尔队留得住拉里，他们就觉得胜券在握。拉里·博因比队友的平均个子略矮些，圆圆的脑袋端端正正，覆着一头乱蓬蓬的亚麻色鬈发，壮实的双腿、结实的胳膊和宽厚的胸膛，都表明他身上蕴藏着充沛的后劲。“这是琼斯维尔佬里最俊的一个”——这是许多眼光敏锐的姑娘心里的悄悄话，也是许多嗓门响亮的看客嘴上的公开评论。

===Original===
This is the manner of placing the two Clubs:﻿—

Catalpas.

Jonesvilles.

两队的阵容配置如下：——

卡塔帕队。

- **刘易斯·莫里斯（Lewis Morris）**，左野手

- **查利·金（Charlie King）**，投手

- **哈特·斯特林（Hart Stirling）**，二垒手

琼斯维尔队。

===Original===
The Catalpas won the toss and went to the field, with due consideration for the improvement of their chances in the final innings, and the game began with a comfortable feeling pervading the champions of the home nine. The winning of the toss was a good omen, everybody thought.

A buzz of half-suppressed excitement swept over the field as Studley, of the Jonesville Nine, went first to the bat. He sent a low ball to second base which Hart Stirling failed to hold, and Studley got to first base. Larry Boyne followed and sent up a sky-high ball, and Studley, having stolen to second and third base, got safely home, while Larry reached second base. Morrison sent a good right fielder, on which he got halfway around, while Larry, with a rush, made the home run, adding one more to the score of the Jonesvilles. Alice bit her lip with vexation, but some of the more magnanimous of the townspeople commented, under their breath, “Good for the red-cheeked Irishman!”

Great things were expected of Ellis, the champion pitcher of the Jonesvillians, who went next to the bat, and who was reckoned as nearly as good with the bat as with the ball; but he made a poor strike, and, with a long-drawn “Oh‑h‑h!” from the sympathetic friends of the home club, the ball dropped near the home base and the young champion of Jonesville went out on his first. Next, Morrison, in his haste to get to third base, was put out by Will Sprague, and the fortunes of the visitors visibly waned. Wheeler, who went next to the bat, provoked a murmur of approbation from the spectators, who were now warming up to the game, and who admired the handsome proportions and springy movements of the center fielder of the Jonesvillers. He sent a resounding ball safely to the right field, got to first base, but, overrunning the second base, was neatly put out by Hart Stirling, the second baseman of the Catalpas. Thus closed the innings﻿—two runs for the visiting Nine.

===Chinese===
卡塔帕队掷硬币赢了，先上场防守——这是充分考虑了怎样为决胜局多留几分胜算——比赛开始了，主队这班冠军队员个个心里笃定。人人都觉得，先赢掷币是个好兆头。

琼斯维尔九人队的斯塔德利第一个上场击球，全场掠过一阵半是压抑的兴奋的低语。他击出一记飞向二垒的低平球，哈特·斯特林没能接住，斯塔德利上了一垒。接着拉里·博因上场，击出一个直冲云霄的高球，已盗到二垒、三垒的斯塔德利稳稳跑回本垒得分，拉里自己则上了二垒。莫里森击出一记漂亮的右外场球，自己跑过了一半垒程，拉里则一鼓作气跑回本垒，为琼斯维尔队再添一分。爱丽丝懊恼地咬住嘴唇，但镇民中一些气量较大的人低声称赞道：“这个红脸蛋的爱尔兰小子，好样的！”

接下来轮到琼斯维尔佬的王牌投手埃利斯上场击球，人们对他的期望极高——据说他的球棒几乎和他的投球一样厉害；谁知他这一击糟透了，随着主队球迷们一声拖得长长的“哦——！”，球落在本垒附近，琼斯维尔的这位年轻冠军头一次上场击球便出局了。接着，莫里森急于奔向三垒，被威尔·斯普拉格送上出局，客队的气运眼见着低落下去。随后上场击球的惠勒引起了观众的一阵低声喝彩——观众们此刻已经渐入佳境，很欣赏这位琼斯维尔中外野手匀称的身量和弹性十足的步态。他把一记响亮的球稳稳送进右外场，上了一垒，却因冲过了二垒，被卡塔帕队的二垒手哈特·斯特林利落地触杀出局。这一局就此收场——客队得两分。

===Original===
“Not much to brag of,” remarked Bill Van Orman, the big pitcher of the Dean County Nine. “Not much to brag of, and I don’t think that the Jonesvillians are feeling first rate over this. Let them wait until Al Heaton and Charlie King get after them. Then they’ll sing small, I allow.”

“Hush up, you, there goes Lew Morris to the bat for the Catalpas. He’ll show them something. Look at that chist of his! Golly! don’t I remember him, though!” remarked Hank Mitchell.

Lew Morris, tall, handsome and sinewy, deserved the praises lavished upon him, as he stood, modestly but confidently, to open the innings for the Catalpas. But, to the great disappointment of his admirers, he failed to make a hit and was sent to first base on three called balls. Charlie King justified the expectations of his friends by striking a tremendous ball to right field, on which Lew Morris tallied one, but in trying to get to second base, was put out by Studley in excellent style. Hart Stirling followed, making the first quarter, and Will Sprague went to second base on a strong hit to right field, which brought Stirling home. John Brubaker next went to the bat, with an air of serene confidence, but he failed to satisfy the expectations of the onlookers, and went out on a foul tip.

===Chinese===
“没什么好吹的，”迪安县九人队的大个子投手比尔·范奥曼（Bill Van Orman）说。“没什么好吹的，我看琼斯维尔佬对这个也不会太得意。让他们等着阿尔·希顿和查利·金来收拾他们吧。到那时他们就得蔫了，我估摸着。”

“闭嘴吧你，瞧，卢·莫里斯代表卡塔帕队上场击球了。他会让他们见识见识的。瞧他那副胸膛！乖乖！我可太记得这小子了！”汉克·米切尔（Hank Mitchell）说。

卢·莫里斯个子高挑，英俊矫健，站在那儿为卡塔帕队开局击球时，神态谦逊而自信，倒也当得起人们对他的盛赞。可是他未能击中，让崇拜者们大失所望，凭三个坏球被保送上一垒。查利·金不负朋友们的期望，一记势大力沉的球轰向右外场，卢·莫里斯借此得了一分，可他自己想冲二垒时，却被斯塔德利漂亮地触杀出局。哈特·斯特林接着上场，得了一分；威尔·斯普拉格一记有力的右外场安打上了二垒，同时送斯特林回本垒得分。随后约翰·布鲁贝克一脸从容自信地上场击球，却辜负了看客们的期望，一记擦棒球出局了。

===Original===
“Your champions do not seem to be in good condition, today, Alice,” said the Judge, demurely. “I am just beginning to become interested in the game, and I must say that I shouldn’t like to see the Catalpas beaten.”

“Thank you, papa,” said Alice, her eyes sparkling with excitement. “I thought you would get waked up if you once saw the play and realized how much depends on the game today.”

“It’s the championship of the Northern District, is it not, my child?”

“Yes, and if the Catalpas don’t win now, I am afraid﻿—well, I don’t know what I am afraid of. But they will be dreadfully discouraged.”

“So shall I be,” said the Judge, gravely turning his eyes to the stand, where Hiram Porter, the first baseman of the home nine, and an honor man in his class at Ann Arbor, had taken up the bat. Hiram retrieved the failing fortunes of the Catalpas by a powerful ball to center field on which he reached the first base. George Buckner, who followed, sent a high ball which was beautifully caught by Studley, on second base, amidst murmurs of applause, as if the townsmen and townswomen of the Catalpas were half-ashamed to give full expression to their extorted admiration of the visitors’ good play.

===Chinese===
“你们的冠军健儿们今天状态似乎不佳呀，爱丽丝，”法官一本正经地说。“我对这比赛刚刚开始发生兴趣，得说一句，我可不愿意看到卡塔帕队输。”

“谢谢您，爸爸，”爱丽丝说，眼里闪着兴奋的光。“我就知道，只要您看上一场，明白今天的比赛关系有多重大，您就会来劲的。”

“这是北区的冠军赛，对不对，我的孩子？”

“是的。要是卡塔帕队这回赢不了，我怕——唉，我也不知道自己怕什么。反正他们会沮丧得要命的。”

“我也会沮丧的，”法官说，神情严肃地把目光转向击球处，主队一垒手海勒姆·波特——在安娜堡（Ann Arbor）读书时是班里的高材生——已拿起了球棒。海勒姆一记有力的中外场球，上了一垒，为卡塔帕队挽回了颓势。接着上场的乔治·巴克纳击出一个高球，被二垒上的斯塔德利漂亮地接杀，四下响起一阵低低的喝彩，仿佛卡塔帕镇的男男女女被人硬生生逼出了对客队好球的钦佩，又不好意思全喊出来似的。

===Original===
“That was well done, anyway,” remarked Hank Mitchell, “and that winds up the first inning with three outs and three runs to two for the Jonesvillians. Come, you must wake up, Catalpas, or we shall get licked again.”

“Wait until the Catalpas come in on the last innings, and then you’ll see some fun. They are laying low for black ducks, and don’t you forget that. We’ve tried them too many times, Hank, and you know it.” This was Van Orman’s shrewd comment, as the second inning began with Martin, the Jonesville left fielder, at the bat. He should not have made the first base “by rights” as the observant Hank remarked, under his breath, but Charlie King and Hiram Porter fumbled the ball, and he got safely to first. Simpson struck the ball straight into the pitcher’s hands and went out ignominiously. Then Berthelet went out on three strikes, and the spirits of the sympathetic spectators rose perceptibly. Two out and no runs for the visitors.

“Things are looking dark for your friends from Jonesville,” said the Judge. “And, by the way, isn’t there danger of their getting what you call ‘a goose-egg’ in this game, Alice?”

“O yes, papa,” she answered, “I shouldn’t wonder the least bit if they should be whitewashed in this inning, but there are so many chances against it that I wouldn’t like to boast too much beforehand. Those Jonesville boys are awful sly!”

===Chinese===
“不管怎么说，这一下真漂亮，”汉克·米切尔说，“第一局就此收场，三人出局，卡塔帕队得三分，琼斯维尔队得两分。喂，你们可得振作起来，卡塔帕，不然咱们又要挨揍了。”

“等卡塔帕队打到最后一局上场，就有好戏看了。他们这是猫着劲儿等着打野鸭呢，你可别忘了。咱们跟他们交手回数太多啦，汉克，你是知道的。”这是范奥曼精明的评语。第二局开始了，琼斯维尔队的左野手马丁率先上场击球。正如眼光老辣的汉克低声说的，按理他本不该上得了一垒，可查利·金和海勒姆·波特把球玩漏了，他就稳稳当当上了垒。辛普森把球不偏不倚打进投手手里，灰溜溜地出局了。接着贝特莱三击不中出局，富有同情心的观众们情绪明显高涨起来。两人出局，客队一分未得。

“你们琼斯维尔的朋友们前景不妙哇，”法官说。“对了，照你们的说法，他们这局有没有吃‘鸭蛋’的危险呀，爱丽丝？”

“哦，有的，爸爸，”她答道，“这一局他们要是被零封，我一点儿也不奇怪；不过变数太多，我可不敢把话说得太满。那些琼斯维尔的小子们鬼得很！”

===Original===
“That’s Sam Alexander at the bat now, trying in vain to strike the ball.” And, as Alice spoke, Alexander walked to first base on called balls, and Martin cleverly made his home run, scoring one for the Jonesvillians. “So they will not be whitewashed, at all events,” said Alice, with a little sigh.

Studley now made his second base by a ground ball to third base which Will Sprague failed to stop, and by which also Alexander came home. Larry Boyne, smiling, but keenly alive to the critical condition of affairs, now went to the bat, made a magnificent ball to center field and went to first base whither he was quickly followed by Morrison, and Studley scored another run for the Jonesville Nine. Next, amidst great excitement, for the play was now waxing hot, Ellis struck a splendid right fielder, by which Larry and Morrison easily reached the home plate and Studley got to second base. The spectators trembled with excitement as Wheeler made a capital safe hit to center field, Studley got in, Wheeler reached the second base, stole to third, and, by the wild throwing of the Catalpas, got home on a passed ball.

Next, Martin got to first base on a slow ball to right field, and then home on passed balls. He was followed by Simpson, after two strikes, on which he got to first base and came dangerously near being put out by Hart Stirling, who made a fine one-handed catch amidst the ringing applause of the spectators, Alice Howell’s small handmaid exciting much mirth by her shrill exclamation of “isn’t he grand!” when Hart, with a tremendous leap, secured the ball as it was flying far above his head.

Berthelet then went out on a foul tip leaving Simpson on the base and closing the innings for the Jonesvillians. Al Heaton having gone to the bat for the Catalpas, made his first base on called balls, and when Ben Burton, who succeeded him at the bat, made a good hit, he reached third base. Burton then got to second base, and Al Heaton reached the home plate, while Larry Boyne was attempting to throw Burton out at second base. Lew Morris next got to first base through the muffing of Studley, but was forced out by Charlie King, who sharply followed him to the first. Will Sprague sent the ball well up into the sky, but Berthelet, the agile and keen-eyed young Frenchman in the right field, caught it handsomely, and Will retired in good order. John Brubaker went to first base, and then Ellis, the Jonesville pitcher, made a muff with his ball, giving the Catalpas one tally. Hiram Porter followed with a safe hit, but George Buckner went out on a foul ball and the inning closed with a score of ten for the Jonesville boys and eight for the Catalpas.

===Chinese===
“现在击球的是萨姆·亚历山大（Sam Alexander），正白费力气地挥棒呢。”爱丽丝话音未落，亚历山大就凭坏球保送上了垒，马丁乖巧地跑回本垒，为琼斯维尔队得了一分。“这么着，他们无论如何吃不成零蛋了。”爱丽丝轻轻叹了口气说。

这时斯塔德利击出一记飞向三垒的地滚球，威尔·斯普拉格没能拦住，他上了二垒，亚历山大也借此跑回本垒得分。拉里·博因微笑着上场击球，心里却对局势的危急了如指掌；他击出一记精彩之极的中外场球，上了一垒，莫里森紧跟着也上了垒，斯塔德利又为琼斯维尔九人队跑回一分。接着，比赛正酣，全场群情激奋，埃利斯击出一记漂亮的右外场球，拉里和莫里森轻轻松松踏回本垒，斯塔德利进了二垒。惠勒又打出一记漂亮的中外场安打，斯塔德利得分，惠勒上了二垒，又盗向三垒，趁卡塔帕队传球漫无准头，靠一记捕手漏接球跑回了本垒——观众们激动得直打颤。

随后，马丁靠一记飞向右外场的慢球上了一垒，又靠捕手的接连漏接跑回本垒。接着是辛普森，挨了两击之后上一垒，险些被哈特·斯特林弄出局——哈特单手一记漂亮的接杀，赢得观众雷鸣般的掌声；而爱丽丝·豪厄尔的小使女一声尖亮的“瞧他多威风！”，惹得众人大乐，因为哈特奋力一跃，把正从他头顶高高飞过的球稳稳抓在了手里。

贝特莱随后一记擦棒球出局，把辛普森留在垒上，琼斯维尔队这一局就此结束。轮到卡塔帕队，阿尔·希顿上场击球，凭坏球保送上一垒；接着本·伯顿打出一记好球，希顿趁机进了三垒。伯顿再上二垒，阿尔·希顿踏回本垒得分——那会儿拉里·博因正想把冲二垒的伯顿传杀出局。随后卢·莫里斯趁斯塔德利漏接上了垒，却被紧接着冲向一垒的查利·金封杀出局。威尔·斯普拉格把球高高送上天去，可右外场那个身手敏捷、目光锐利的法国小伙子贝特莱漂亮地把球接住，威尔从容退场。约翰·布鲁贝克上了一垒，随后琼斯维尔投手埃利斯接球失误，送了卡塔帕队一分。海勒姆·波特接着一记安打，可乔治·巴克纳一记界外球出局，这一局结束时，琼斯维尔队十分，卡塔帕队八分。

===Original===
The Jonesvilles opened the third inning by sending Alexander to the bat. He was sent to first base on called balls, and was followed by Studley, who sent a ball to Ben Burton at shortstop, but which Ben muffed, and Studley got safely to first base. Larry Boyne followed with a winged ball which he sent flying to the right field and which enabled him to reach second base and brought Alexander and Studley home. Morrison sent an air ball to left field, by which he reached first base, and Larry came home. Then Ellis hit a ground ball to Ben Burton at shortstop, which Ben muffed again, allowing Larry to come home and Ellis to get to first base. Wheeler made first base on a ground ball to left field, and Martin sent a slow ball to center field which reached the first base before him. During the passage of the ball, however, Morrison came home, and Ellis subsequently tallied on a passed ball. Simpson went to the bat and was struck out, and Berthelet, who followed, was neatly caught out on a foul fly by Ben Burton, who thus partially retrieved his reputation and the inning was closed for the Jonesvilles.

The showing for the Catalpas was now pretty dark, and it did not improve during their next inning. Al Heaton, who led for the home nine, was put out in attempting to steal from first to second base, and Ben Burton, who followed him, met with a similar disaster. Lew Morris went to first base on a ball to shortstop which Alexander overthrew to first base. Next, Charlie King hit an air ball which was caught by Alexander at shortstop, leaving three out with Morris dead on the second base. The score then stood, Jonesvilles, 15; Catalpas, 8.

“A whitewash!” cried Hank Mitchell, uncertain whether he ought to exult as an old adversary of the home club, or be downcast as a citizen of the town of Catalpa. But, his patriotism rallying in time, he cried to Andrew Jackson Simis, a Jonesville spectator, “I s’pose you think your boys are going to get away with us, this time? Just you wait till the last innings, and then you will see them come up with a rush.”

===Chinese===
第三局，琼斯维尔队派亚历山大率先上场击球。他凭坏球保送上一垒；接着斯塔德利击出一记飞向游击手本·伯顿的球，本却漏接了，斯塔德利稳稳上了一垒。拉里·博因接着击出一记疾飞的平直球，直飞右外场，让他上了二垒，还送亚历山大和斯塔德利回本垒得分。莫里森击出一记左外场高飞球，自己上一垒，拉里也回了本垒。随后埃利斯击出一记地滚球飞向游击手本·伯顿，本又漏接了，让拉里回了本垒，埃利斯自己上了一垒。惠勒靠一记左外场地滚球上了一垒，马丁击出一记中外场慢球，球比他先到一垒。不过在球传递期间，莫里森回了本垒，埃利斯随后也靠一记捕手漏接球得了分。辛普森上场击球，被三振出局；接着的贝特莱被本·伯顿利落地接杀一记界外高飞球，本由此挽回了几分颜面，琼斯维尔队这一局宣告结束。

卡塔帕队的前景此刻已相当黯淡，而轮到他们击球的这一局也不见起色。主队率先上场的阿尔·希顿在从一垒盗向二垒时被触杀出局，接着的本·伯顿也遭了同样的殃。卢·莫里斯击出一记飞向游击手的球，亚历山大向一垒传过了头，卢上了垒。接着查利·金击出一记腾空球，被游击手亚历山大接杀，三人出局，莫里斯死在二垒上。这时比分是：琼斯维尔队15分，卡塔帕队8分。

“零封！”汉克·米切尔喊道，一时拿不准自己该作为主队的老对头幸灾乐祸呢，还是作为卡塔帕镇的居民垂头丧气。好在他的爱镇之心及时振作起来，冲着一位琼斯维尔观众安德鲁·杰克逊·西米斯（Andrew Jackson Simis）嚷道：“我猜你以为你们那帮小子这回能吃定我们了？等着瞧吧，到了最后一局，你会看见他们猛地反上来的。”

===Original===
“They’d better begin to rush pretty quick, then,” was the sneering answer. “I guess your goose is cooked.” There was a stir among the Dean County Nine, who, with their friends, sat together at the end of the range of seats, when this unfriendly remark was flung out. There were threatening glances and clenched fists in the group of Catalpa boys.

“Here! here! no squabbling!” cried Deputy Sheriff Wheeler, hurrying up, as his vigilant eye fell on the angry-looking knot of lads. “These men are visitors; can’t you behave yourselves?”

But the Catalpas were in nowise cast down. Lew Morris, their captain, went among the boys and impressed on them something of his own cheerful courage and roused them to the importance of making a tremendous effort in the next inning. Perhaps the Jonesvillians were unduly elated. Their first man at the bat, Alexander, was put out by sending the ball almost directly into the hands of Hiram Porter at first base. Then Studley sent a good ball to center field, on which he went to first base, and went to second while Larry Boyne was batting. Larry tipped a foul fly which Al Heaton caught, and Morrison, who succeeded him, was caught out in a precisely similar manner, and the inning closed with Studley left on the second base and a whitewash for the visitors.

===Chinese===
“那他们最好赶紧开始反，”对方冷笑着回答，“我看你们是完蛋了。”坐在看台尽头一带的迪安县九人队和他们的朋友们，听了这句不友好的话，一阵骚动。卡塔帕的小伙子们这边怒目相向，拳头都攥紧了。

“喂！喂！不许吵嘴！”副治安官惠勒喝道，他警惕的眼睛一落到那群怒气冲冲的小伙子上，便急忙赶了过来。“人家是客人，你们就不能懂点规矩？”

但卡塔帕队丝毫没有气馁。队长卢·莫里斯走到小伙子们中间，把自己那份乐观的勇气灌输给他们一些，激励他们认识到下一局必须拼尽全力的重要性。也许是琼斯维尔佬得意过了头。他们第一个上场击球的亚历山大，把球差不多不偏不倚地送进了一垒手海勒姆·波特手里，出局了。接着斯塔德利击出一记漂亮的中外场球，上了一垒，又在拉里·博因击球时进了二垒。拉里擦出一记界外高飞球，被阿尔·希顿接住；随后上场的莫里森如出一辙，同样被接杀出局，这一局以斯塔德利留置二垒、客队吃了个零蛋而告终。

===Original===
There was great uproar in the crowd around the field, as soon as the Catalpas went in their turn to the bat. The townsfolk forgot all decorum in their delight over the semblance of victory thus snatched from defeat. They cheered the Catalpas as they came in from the field, and by their noise, at least, showed that no impartial judgment could be expected from the majority of the spectators. Judge Howell critically looked over the crowd and remarked to Alice that he thought it was bad mannered in the townspeople to exult over the defeat or reverses of their visitors.

“But it is because they know that the Catalpas are going to be beaten, after all,” said Alice, with a tone of great despondency.

“Going to be beaten?” asked the Judge, with surprise. “Why, haven’t they just given the Jonesvilles a whitewash, as I think you call it, and the score is 15 to 8, with your favorites going to the bat?”

“Yes, papa, that is so; but you see that the Jonesvillians play a much better fielding game than the Catalpas, and I am sure that our club will never be able to regain what they have lost.”

===Chinese===
轮到卡塔帕队上场击球时，场边人群顿时沸腾了。镇民们为这从败局中抢回来的一点胜利的影子欢喜得忘乎所以、全无体统。卡塔帕队员从场上走进来时，他们齐声喝彩，单凭这份喧闹至少可以看出，不能指望大多数观众做出公道的评判。豪厄尔法官（Judge Howell）挑剔地扫视着人群，对爱丽丝说，他觉得镇民们这样为客队的失利和受挫而欢腾，实在有失风度。

“可这正是因为他们知道，卡塔帕队到头来终究要输啊。”爱丽丝说，语气十分沮丧。

“要输？”法官诧异地问，“嘿，他们不是刚刚让琼斯维尔队吃了个——我记得你们管那叫零蛋——而且回合比分还是15比8，接下来上场击球的可正是你偏爱的这一队？”

“是的，爸爸，是这样；可您瞧，琼斯维尔队的防守比卡塔帕队强得多，我敢肯定，我们俱乐部永远追不回落下的分数了。”

===Original===
Miss Alice soon began to think that she had lost hope too soon, for the Catalpas scored three runs in their inning, Hart Stirling having made a home run on a tremendous ball sent to left field where it was muffed shamefully, first by Martin and then by Simpson. Will Sprague and John Brubaker followed him successfully, and Hiram Porter, who had made his first base, was put out by Morrison. The same fate overtook George Buckner and Al Heaton, who were put out by the active and vigilant first baseman of the Jonesvilles. Nevertheless, the inning closed with a decided gain for the home nine, the score being 15 for the Jonesvilles, 11 for the Catalpas.

There was intense but suppressed excitement all around the field, as the visitors sent Ellis to the bat, and he was at once caught out by Hart Stirling on a fly sent to second base. Wheeler made first base, and Martin, who followed him, was put out on first base, while Wheeler came home on a ball balked by Charlie King. Simpson was put out on first base, and the Catalpas took their inning, sending Ben Burton to the bat. He was caught out by Studley; then Lew Morris was put out at first base by a ball sent by Alexander to Morrison; next Charlie King went out on called balls, and, amidst cries of “another whitewash!” the inning closed with a score of 16 to 11, in favor of the visitors.

In the sixth inning, the Jonesvilles added eight to their score, and the Catalpas gained seven, thus making the home nine a little more hopeful, although the relative distance of the two nines was not changed. The feature of this inning was a grand hit to the center field made by Larry Boyne, on which he made first base and brought home Alexander and Studley, who were on the second and third bases, respectively. The score stood thus: Jonesvilles, 24, Catalpas, 18. And there was no exultation in the ranks of the townsfolk.

===Chinese===
爱丽丝小姐很快便觉得自己未免绝望得太早，因为卡塔帕队在这一局里连得三分：哈特·斯特林一记轰向左外场的重炮打出了本垒打，那球先被马丁、后被辛普森丢人现眼地接连漏接。威尔·斯普拉格和约翰·布鲁贝克紧跟着先后得分；上了一垒的海勒姆·波特被莫里森弄出局。乔治·巴克纳和阿尔·希顿也遭了同样的命运，都被琼斯维尔队那机警戒备的一垒手送上出局。不过这一局结束时主队还是明显扳回了一截，比分变成琼斯维尔队15分，卡塔帕队11分。

客队派埃利斯上场击球，全场笼罩在强烈而压抑的激动之中；他一记飞向二垒的高飞球，立刻被哈特·斯特林接杀。惠勒上了一垒，随后的马丁在一垒出局，惠勒则趁查利·金投球犯规跑回本垒得分。辛普森也在一垒出局，轮到卡塔帕队进攻，本·伯顿率先上场击球。他被斯塔德利接杀出局；接着卢·莫里斯被亚历山大传给莫里森的球封杀在一垒上；随后查利·金因判球出局，在“又吃零蛋啦！”的喊声中，这一局以16比11、客队领先结束。

第六局，琼斯维尔队添了8分，卡塔帕队得了7分，主队因此又生出几分指望，虽然两队之间的差距并未缩小。这一局的亮点是拉里·博因一记气势恢宏的中外场安打，他借这一击上了一垒，还把分别在二垒和三垒上的亚历山大和斯塔德利送回了本垒。比分变成：琼斯维尔队24分，卡塔帕队18分。镇民队伍里已听不见半点欢腾。

===Original===
Larry Boyne went to the bat in the next inning, for the visiting Nine. He sent a magnificent air ball so high that it seemed lost in the misty blue of the October sky. But it descended straight into the hands of John Brubaker in the right field, and a chorus of “ah‑h‑h’s” went up from the assembled multitude. Morrison was caught out on a foul fly; Ellis shared his fate, and Wheeler was put out on first base. Great was the exultation among the citizens of Catalpa. The Jonesvillers had been again whitewashed. The short October day was wearing on apace, but the chances of the Catalpas were improving as the light went down in the west.

The home nine added three to their score in the inning, home runs being made by John Brubaker, Hiram Porter, and George Buckner. Al Heaton and Ben Burton were both put out by foul flies. Charlie King was put out on first base, leaving Lew Morris on third base. But as the score stood 24 for the Jonesvilles and 21 for the home nine, the spirits of the majority of the spectators, whose sympathies were all one way, began to rise. Perhaps the Jonesvillers would be sent home without the championship.

But these hopes were dashed by the next inning, which was the eighth, the Jonesvilles having gained one run, while the Catalpas were ignominiously “whitewashed.” The visitors showed their good qualities in the field by a fine double play in their inning. Hart Stirling being on the first base, Will Sprague hit short to Ellis, who sent the ball to Studley at second base, cutting off Stirling; and John Brubaker, in attempting to steal from first to second base, was run out by Studley and Morrison.

===Chinese===
下一局，客队轮到拉里·博因上场击球。他击出一记漂亮的高飞球，蹿得那么高，仿佛消融在十月朦朦胧胧的蓝天里。可球落下来时，端端正正掉进右外场约翰·布鲁贝克的手里，聚集的人群齐声发出一阵“啊——！”。莫里森一记界外高飞球被接杀；埃利斯步了他的后尘，惠勒也在一垒出局。卡塔帕镇的居民们欢欣鼓舞。琼斯维尔队又吃了一记零蛋。十月的白昼本就短促，此刻更是飞快地向晚，可随着日光西沉，卡塔帕队的胜算却渐渐大了起来。

主队在这一局里添了3分，约翰·布鲁贝克、海勒姆·波特和乔治·巴克纳都打出了本垒打。阿尔·希顿和本·伯顿双双被界外高飞球接杀出局。查利·金在一垒出局，把卢·莫里斯留在三垒上。不过比分是琼斯维尔队24分、主队21分，大多数观众的心全偏在一边，此刻士气开始回升。说不定琼斯维尔佬就得两手空空、没捧着冠军回去了。

可是下一局——第八局——把这些希望都击碎了：琼斯维尔队又得一分，卡塔帕队则窝窝囊囊地吃了“零蛋”。客队在他们那半局里以一记漂亮的双杀显露了防守上的真功夫：哈特·斯特林在一垒上，威尔·斯普拉格短击给埃利斯，埃利斯把球传向二垒的斯塔德利，封杀了斯特林；约翰·布鲁贝克想从一垒盗向二垒，被斯塔德利和莫里森触杀出局。

===Original===
Nobody stirred from the field, although the day was dying slowly and the simple habits of the Catalpa women called them home to their household duties. The decisive inning was near at hand, and as Alice stood up in her father’s carriage, in order to get a better view of the game, the hitherto orderly crowd closed in around the players. Spectators and players drew a long breath as Larry Boyne went to the bat for the Jonesvilles. He wielded the bat with great skill and dexterity; but Charlie King’s pitching was wonderfully clever, and Larry went out on a foul tip to Al Heaton, catcher. Morrison made third base on a safe hit; Ellis made first base and Morrison came home on a ball muffed by Charlie King, and then Martin, on a center field ball hit, brought Ellis and Wheeler home. Simpson now made first base on a hit to the right field, and an overthrow brought Martin home and gave second base to Simpson. Berthelet was caught out on a foul fly by Al Heaton, and Simpson, in attempting to steal home, was run out by Al Heaton and Will Sprague.

“Three out on the last inning!” roared two or three of the Dean County Nine, great hulking fellows, who stood near the carriage of the Judge. Alice looked at them reproachfully, although her cheeks were ruddy with half-suppressed excitement.

“It’s real mean of them, isn’t it, papa?” she said. “They will not seem to consider that we should be very angry if we were treated thus in Jonesville.”

Now went Hiram Porter, big and handsome Hiram, to the bat for the Catalpas. Hiram looked as tall as a giant in the gathering twilight, and he stood up in manly fashion. But Hiram was put out on first base by a ball sent by Studley to Morrison, and George Buckner, who followed him, had great ado to save himself. But he made first base, and Al Heaton next sent a singing ball to center field, on which he went to second base and Buckner to third. Ben Burton then undertook to bat Buckner home, but he was, himself, put out on first base. Lew Morris then took the bat, sent a high ball to center field and secured the first base. Charlie King followed to the first, and amidst despondent cries of “Three out!” the game and the inning ended with a score of 29 for the Jonesville Nine and 23 for the Catalpas.

===Chinese===
虽说天色慢慢暗下去，卡塔帕的女人们素来的习惯也在唤她们回家操持家务，场边却没有一个人挪动。决胜局就在眼前了，爱丽丝站到父亲的马车里，好把比赛看得更清楚些；此前一直秩序井然的人群这时向球员们围拢过来。拉里·博因为琼斯维尔队上场击球时，观众和球员都屏住了长长的一口气。他挥棒娴熟灵巧；可查利·金的投球实在精妙，拉里一记擦棒球出局，被捕手阿尔·希顿接住。莫里森一记安打上了三垒；埃利斯上了一垒，莫里森趁查利·金漏接跑回本垒得分；随后马丁一记中外场安打，把埃利斯和惠勒都送回了本垒。辛普森靠一记右外场安打上了一垒，一次传球过头送马丁回本垒得分，也让辛普森进了二垒。贝特莱一记界外高飞球被阿尔·希顿接杀；辛普森试图盗回本垒，被阿尔·希顿和威尔·斯普拉格触杀出局。

“最后一局三人出局喽！”迪安县九人队里两三条大汉吼道，他们都是膀大腰圆的家伙，就站在法官的马车旁边。爱丽丝嗔怪地望着他们，双颊却因半压抑的激动而通红。

“他们这样真是太刻薄了，是不是，爸爸？”她说。“他们好像根本不想想，要是在琼斯维尔，人家这么对待我们，我们会多恼火。”

现在轮到卡塔帕队，高大英俊的海勒姆·波特上场击球。在渐浓的暮色里，海勒姆显得像巨人一般高，他堂堂正正地站定。可海勒姆被斯塔德利传给莫里森的球封杀在一垒，随后上场的乔治·巴克纳好不容易才保住自己——他上了一垒，接着阿尔·希顿一记呼啸的平飞球射向中外场，自己上了二垒，巴克纳进了三垒。本·伯顿随后想把巴克纳送回本垒，自己却在一垒出局。卢·莫里斯接过球棒，击出一记中外场高球，抢占了一垒。查利·金紧跟着上了垒；在“三人出局！”的绝望喊声里，这一局和整场比赛一同落幕：琼斯维尔九人队29分，卡塔帕队23分。

===Original===
Deputy Sheriff Wheeler, forgetting for the time his official dignity, stood up in what was once the judges’ stand and shouted, “Three cheers for the champions of Northern Illinois! Now, then! Hip! Hip! Hip!”

The cheers were given with a pretty good will, considering how great was the disappointment of the townspeople. The captain of the Catalpas set a laudable and manly example to his comrades by going straight to Larry Boyne, the captain of the Jonesville Nine, and, grasping him warmly by the hand, congratulating him on the victory so honorably and handsomely won.

“Of course you can’t expect that a fellow can say that he is glad to have lost the day; but you have worked hard for the pennant, and it belongs to you without any grumbling.”

===Chinese===
副治安官惠勒一时忘了官家的体统，站到昔日的赛马裁判台上，高声喊道：“为北伊利诺伊的冠军们欢呼三声！来吧！嗨！嗨！嗨！”

这三声欢呼倒喊得相当齐心尽力，想想镇民们失望之深，已经算难得了。卡塔帕队的队长为队友们树立了一个值得称道的硬汉榜样：他径直走向琼斯维尔九人队的队长拉里·博因，热烈地握住他的手，祝贺他赢得了一场如此光明磊落、漂漂亮亮的胜利。

“当然，你不能指望一个家伙会说输了这一天他很高兴；不过这面锦旗你们是拼了命挣来的，它归你们，我们没有半句怨言。”

===Original===
Larry, with his ruddy face still ruddier than before, responded in frank fashion and then the crowd began to melt away, for the darkness was coming on. Passing by the Judge’s carriage, yet entangled in the throng of vehicles, Larry glanced up at the pretty girl whom he had noticed with distant admiration. The Judge intercepted his glance, and leaning over with what was meant to be a gracious smile, said, “This is Larry Boyne, the famous catcher of the downriver nine? Well, I congratulate you, young man, on your well-won victory and on your own beautiful playing.”

Larry very much taken aback by this unexpected condescension from the great man of Catalpa, touched his cap, blushed and stammered and gladly rejoined his comrades.

===Chinese===
拉里的红脸膛比先前更红了，他爽朗地应答了几句，人群便开始散去，因为夜幕正在降临。拉里打法官的马车旁经过，还被车辆人群纠缠着，抬眼望了望那位他早远远暗暗倾慕的漂亮姑娘。法官截住了他的目光，带着一个自以为是和蔼可亲的微笑，俯身说道：“这位就是拉里·博因，下游那支九人队的著名捕手？嗯，年轻人，祝贺你赢得这场当之无愧的胜利，也祝贺你个人漂亮的球艺。”

拉里被这位卡塔帕大人物突如其来的礼贤下士弄得大为局促，碰了碰帽檐，红着脸结结巴巴应了几句，便急忙归队找队友们去了。

===Original===
“Fine young man, that,” said the Judge, sententiously, as his carriage slowly drew out of the crowd and moved toward the gate.

“If a few such players as he were in the place of some of the muffs in the Catalpa Nine,” said Alice, “I think that the championship of the whole State would belong in this town.”

===Chinese===
“好一个俊俏的年轻人。”法官言简意赅地说，马车慢慢挤出人群，向大门驶去。

“要是卡塔帕九人队里那几个漏接王的位置，换上几个像他这样的球员，”爱丽丝说，“我看全州的冠军就该落在这个镇子了。”

===Original===
“Why I do believe my little daughter is crying!” cried the Judge.

“I am not crying,” said Alice stoutly. “But I confess that I am mad enough to cry. Are we always going to be beaten by every scrubby nine that comes here, I’d like to know?”

Dr. Selby, the staid and dignified village town apothecary, who was walking by the carriage, heard the indignant outburst, and looking up, said with a smile, “We’ve got the timber here for a first-class nine, Miss Alice, but the thing is to get the timber together.”

Judge Howell, with his grandest manner, said, “If there is any movement to retrieve the honor of Catalpa in the baseball field, please count on my assistance and support.”

===Chinese===
“哎呀，我相信我的小女儿准是哭了！”法官叫道。

“我才没哭，”爱丽丝倔强地说。“不过我承认，我气得都要哭了。我倒想知道，难道随便哪支杂牌九人队上我们这儿来，我们都要输给他们不成？”

塞尔比医生（Dr. Selby）——镇上那位沉稳庄重的药剂师——正走在马车旁边，听见了这番愤愤的发作，抬头微笑道：“咱们这儿有一流九人队的好料子，爱丽丝小姐，难的是把这些料子凑到一块儿。”

豪厄尔法官摆出他最堂皇的气派，说道：“若有什么重振卡塔帕棒球场上的荣誉的举动，请务必算上我的一份协助和支持。”

## XIV A Strange Message from Home / XIV 来自家乡的奇怪消息

===Original===
“Well,” cried Neddie Ellis, cheerily, as the nine filed into Captain Hiram Porter’s room, which had been used as a rallying-place, as it was the largest assigned to any member of the club, “well, we have one more chance at the Calumets, and there is hope while there’s life. Hey, Larry?”

Larry did not immediately reply. He was regarding Ben Burton with suspicion. That individual had received a telegram from the hands of a messenger, as he came into the house, which, having read, he tore into very small pieces and threw away with a disturbed expression of countenance. Ben’s eyes were now fixed on Hiram, who, on coming into the room, had noticed on the mantelpiece a telegram addressed to himself. Ben Burton’s face grew white as his captain, tearing open the envelope, read the despatch with astonishment and wrath depicted on his usually pleasant visage.

===Chinese===
「好啦，」九人鱼贯走进海勒姆·波特队长的房间时，内迪·埃利斯欢快地喊道——那房间一直被用作集会之所，因为它是分给俱乐部成员的房间中最大的一间——「好啦，我们还有一次跟卡柳梅特队较量的机会，留得青山在，不怕没柴烧。哎，拉里？」

拉里没有立刻答话。他正满腹狐疑地打量着本·伯顿。这个人进屋时刚从信差手里接过一份电报，读完便把它撕得粉碎扔掉，脸上神色不定。此刻本的眼睛紧盯着海勒姆——海勒姆一进房间就注意到壁炉台上有一份写给他自己的电报。队长拆开信封，读着电文，平日和悦的面容上现出惊愕与怒火，本·伯顿的脸随之变得煞白。

===Original===
“Read her out, Captain,” cried “The Lily.” “Read her out and let us divide the bad news with you. I’m sure it’s bad news, isn’t it, Neddie?”

Without stopping to consider whether it were discreet or not to divulge the message that was causing him so much perturbation, Hiram, casting a sharp glance at Ben Burton, said, “It is bad news, boys, for it accuses one of our number of treachery. It is from Tom Selby, and it reads thus:—

> Look out for Ben Burton; he has sold the game.

===Chinese===
「念出来吧，队长，」「百合」喊道。「念出来，让我们跟你分担这个坏消息吧。我敢肯定是个坏消息，是不是，内迪？」

海勒姆顾不上斟酌，把这份令他如此心绪不宁的电文公之于众是否妥当，只朝本·伯顿投去锐利的一瞥，说：「是个坏消息，伙计们，它指控我们中的一员犯有叛卖行径。电报是汤姆·塞尔比发来的，内容如下：——

> 提防本·伯顿；他把比赛卖了。

===Original===
“It’s an infernal lie!” shouted Ben, passionately, and very red in the face, and shaky in the limbs. “What does Tom Selby know about the game, and how could I sell the game in Catalpa? I’ll thrash Tom Selby as quick as I get home; see if I don’t!”

“No you won’t,” said Albert Heaton, who entered the room at this moment. “No you won’t. Hear this, Mr. Burton. It’s a despatch from Dr. Selby, dated at Catalpa, 5:20 p.m. You see they had then got the news that the game was lost:—

> I am afraid you did not get Tom’s despatch to the captain, for we hear that the game is gone. Hunt up despatch to Hiram, sent to lodgings.

===Chinese===
「这是卑鄙无耻的谎言！」本激愤地吼道，脸涨得通红，四肢发颤。「汤姆·塞尔比懂什么比赛？我又怎么可能把卡塔帕这边的比赛卖了？我一到家就要揍汤姆·塞尔比一顿；不信走着瞧！」

「不，你不会的。」阿尔伯特·希顿这时走进屋来。「不，你不会的。听听这个，伯顿先生。这是塞尔比医生（Dr. Selby）发来的电报，发自卡塔帕，下午五点二十分。你瞧，那时他们已经得知比赛输了：——

> 恐怕你们没有收到汤姆发给队长的电报，因为我们听说比赛已经丢了。去找一找发给海勒姆、寄到住处的电报。

===Original===
“What’s that despatch you’ve got there Hi? Is it Tom’s?”

“Yes,” answered the captain. “It is from Tom. Read it.”

Albert read the despatch deliberately and said: “I see it all now. My despatch was sent to Judge Morris’s office, where I found it when I stopped in there on my way back from seeing the ladies on board of a streetcar for the north side. Your despatch should have been sent to the ball grounds, and the idiots here have kept it until it was too late. Oh, this is too bad!” and Albert fairly groaned.

“They couldn’t tell what was in the despatch, Al,” said Larry, soothingly. “There’s no use crying over spilt milk. But what I should like is an explanation from Mr. Burton.”

===Chinese===
「你手里那份电报是怎么回事，海伊（Hi）？是汤姆发的吗？」

「是的。」队长答道。「是汤姆发来的。念念吧。」

阿尔伯特从容地把电报读完，说：「这下我全明白了。我那份电报被送到了莫里斯法官（Judge Morris）的办公室，我送几位女士上有轨电车去北区之后，回去的路上顺便进去，才在那儿找到了它。你这份电报本该送到球场去的，这儿的蠢货们却把它一直压着，直到为时已晚。唉，真是太糟糕了！」阿尔伯特简直发出了呻吟。

「他们又不知道电报里写的是什么，阿尔。」拉里宽解道。「覆水难收，哭也无益。不过我想听的，是伯顿先生给个解释。」

===Original===
All eyes were now turned on Burton, who defiantly faced his accusers. He was evidently determined to brave out the charge made against him from Catalpa. His cheek grew red and pale by turns, and he failed to keep the serenity that he attempted.

“See him shake,” said “The Lily,” with bitter contempt. “Did any man ever shake like that when he was innocent. Oh, no, Bennie did not play a muffing game, this afternoon, for nothing!”

“I tell you that’s a lie!” roared Ben, furious with rage. “Any man who says I threw the game is a slanderer and I’ll fight him. Any man would show feeling and shake, as you call it, Bill Van Orman, if accused of doing such a mean thing as selling out his club, and you know it.”

More in sorrow than in anger, Captain Hiram ordered the boys to drop the matter for the present. It could not be determined, in the absence of specific testimony, what amount of truth would be found in the startling charge made against a member of the club. They must wait until they reached home, he said, before it would be worth while to take any steps in the matter. Meantime, he would advise (but not order) that the members of the club drop the business and say nothing about it, especially not to any outsider.

===Chinese===
此刻所有的目光都转向伯顿，而他毫不畏缩地迎着指控他的人。他显然打定主意，要硬扛下这桩从卡塔帕传来的指控。他的脸颊一阵红一阵白，怎么也装不出他想装出的那份镇定。

「瞧他抖的，」「百合」满含刻薄的轻蔑说道。「哪个清白无辜的人会抖成这样？哦，没错，本尼（Bennie）今天下午那通接球漏接，可不是白漏的！」

「我告诉你们，那是谎言！」本怒不可遏地咆哮。「谁说我打假球，谁就是造谣中伤，我要跟他干一架。换了任何人，被指控干出出卖自己俱乐部这种下作勾当时，都会动容发抖——照你们的说法——比尔·范奥曼，你自己心里也清楚。」

海勒姆队长与其说是恼怒，不如说是痛心，他吩咐伙计们眼下暂且放下这件事。在缺乏具体证词的情况下，这桩加在俱乐部一名成员头上的惊人指控里有多少实情，还无从判定。他说，必须等回到家里，再着手处理此事才有意义。在此期间，他建议（但不是命令）俱乐部成员们放下这件事，对它只字不提，尤其不要对外人提起。

===Original===
It was good advice that the captain gave, and the members of the club all followed it so far as speaking of the matter to outsiders was concerned. It was asking too much that they should not talk it over among themselves. By common consent, however, Ben Burton was avoided by all hands. He stood about the house until after supper, then, without leaving any word as to his intentions, he quietly disappeared and was seen no more.

“What a wretched streak of luck!” murmured Larry Boyne to Neddie Ellis. “If that despatch had been sent to Al Heaton, or to Hiram at the ball grounds, all would have been well. We could have withdrawn Ben Burton and put Will Sprague, or Al Heaton, in his place, before the game began. Oh, why did Tom do such a foolish thing as to send the message here?”

“Tom is an idiot!” said Neddie, indignantly. “He’s a feather-head; always was, and always will be! Let’s look at that despatch again, captain.”

===Chinese===
队长的忠告很中肯，就不同外人谈论此事而言，俱乐部成员们都照办了。至于要他们彼此之间也绝不议论，那就强人所难了。不过大家心照不宣，人人都躲着本·伯顿。他在屋子内外挨到晚饭之后，随后没留下一句交代去向的话，便悄悄消失了，再没人见过他。

「运气真是糟透了！」拉里·博因低声对内迪·埃利斯说。「要是那份电报送到了阿尔·希顿手里，或者送到球场交到海勒姆手上，一切都会安然无事。我们本可以在比赛开始前撤下本·伯顿，换威尔·斯普拉格（Will Sprague）或者阿尔·希顿顶他的位置。唉，汤姆干吗做这样的傻事，把电报发到这里来？」

「汤姆是个蠢货！」内迪愤愤地说。「他是个没脑子的轻浮家伙；从前是，以后也永远是！队长，咱们再看看那份电报。」

===Original===
Critical examination of the message showed that it was received in Chicago at half-past one o’clock. It had left Catalpa at half-past eleven o’clock in the forenoon.

“Two hours to send that little message!” almost shrieked Neddie Ellis. “It’s that giddy, flirting girl that works the telegraph office in Catalpa! That’s what’s the matter with the message. Now you just remember that, boys.”

“Softly! softly! Neddie,” said Larry. “You mustn’t accuse the operator. Perhaps the line was down, or somebody else blundered. At any rate, the mischief is done. We’ll wait until we get home before we try to find out what it all means.”

===Chinese===
对电报仔细一查，发现它是下午一点半到达芝加哥的，而上午十一点半才从卡塔帕发出。

「拍这么一条小电报竟用了两个钟头！」内迪·埃利斯几乎尖声叫了起来。「准是卡塔帕电报局那个轻浮爱调笑的丫头干的！电报就是坏在她手里。伙计们，你们可都给我记住这一条。」

「轻点儿！轻点儿！内迪。」拉里说。「可不能随口指摘报务员。也许是线路断了，也许是别人出了差错。无论如何，祸事已经铸成。咱们等回了家，再想法弄清这一切的来龙去脉。」

===Original===
“Aha!” cried “The Lily,” as if he had seen a sudden burst of light. “Now we know why Ben was late in the field. Don’t you remember he stole out after we had got through practicing, this noon, and was gone half an hour, or so? Where was he? Why, he looked as if he had been stealing sheep when he came back. I’ll tell you where he had been. He had been to the telegraph office on the corner below the grounds, telegraphing to some confederate in Catalpa.”

“Smart boy, Bill; but why should he go to the next block below the grounds when there is an office in the building? And how could his telegram to his confederate, if he has one, get back here in Tom Selby’s message?”

“That’s more than I know, Cap, but I should say that he wouldn’t dare to send any crooked message from the ball grounds, where he is known.”

“There is good sense in that, Billy boy,” said Charlie King, who had joined the party while the discussion was going on. “There is plausibility in it, too, for I remember seeing Ben go into that office and make some inquiries, as we were going to the grounds, day before yesterday, to practice.”

===Chinese===
「啊哈！」「百合」叫了起来，仿佛眼前豁然亮起一道光。「这下我们知道本为什么迟迟才上场了。你们不记得今天中午我们练完球后，他溜了出去，约莫去了半个钟头吗？他去哪儿了？嗐，他回来时那副模样，活像刚偷了羊。我来告诉你们他去了哪儿。他去了球场下面拐角处的电报局，给卡塔帕的某个同伙发电报去了。」

「聪明的小伙子，比尔；可楼里就有电报局，他干吗还要跑到球场下面隔一个街区的地方去？再说，他当真有同伙的话，他发给同伙的电报，又怎么会兜回来落进汤姆·塞尔比的电文里？」

「这我可就不知道了，队长；不过要我说，他不敢从球场那儿发任何见不得人的电报，那儿人人都认得他。」

「这话很有道理，比尔小子。」查利·金说——讨论进行时他也加入了这一伙。「而且说得也像真有那么回事，因为我记得前天我们去球场练球的路上，看见本进过那家电报局，还打听了些什么。」

===Original===
Meanwhile, Mr. Heaton was trying to comfort the young ladies in Judge Morris’s family, but his well-meant efforts were discouragingly received by the fair champions of the Catalpa club. Miss Alice was perfectly certain, she averred, that Ben Burton had purposely thrown the game. She had watched him narrowly, and had been, at times, half inclined to send down word to Mr. Boyne, or to the captain, rather (and this was said with a blush), that Burton was playing false. The players could not see it, but she could, and she knew him so well that she could not keep her eyes off him while he was playing, whether it was in the field, at the bat, or base-running.

Later in the evening, Albert came in with two or three of the Catalpa men, bearing the doleful news from Tom Selby. “Didn’t I tell you so?” demanded Alice, with animation. “Didn’t I tell you, Larry Boyne, to beware of that young man?”

===Chinese===
与此同时，希顿先生正在安慰莫里斯法官家的几位年轻小姐，然而这番好心好意，在那几位卡塔帕俱乐部的漂亮女拥趸那里得到的回应却令人泄气。爱丽丝小姐一口咬定——她如是断言——本·伯顿是存心打的假球。她一直紧紧盯着他，有几次都半想传话下去给博因先生，或者不如说给队长（说这话时她红着脸），说伯顿打球不老实。球员们看不出来，她却看得出；她太了解他了，以至于他在场上打球时——无论防守、击球还是跑垒——她的眼睛就没法从他身上挪开。

晚间迟些时候，阿尔伯特带着两三名卡塔帕队员进来，带来了汤姆·塞尔比那封令人沮丧的电讯。「我不是跟你们说过了吗？」爱丽丝兴冲冲地质问。「我不是跟你说过吗，拉里·博因，要提防那个年轻人？」

===Original===
“You did indeed, Miss Howell,” replied Larry, with mock dejection. “And we would have looked out for him, as you suggested, if we had had any tangible suspicion, or any proof whatsoever, that he was crooked. But how could we make a stand against one of our own number, merely on so vague a hint as that which we had?”

“If *I* were a member of the Catalpa club,” said the girl, with spirit, “I would not have so evil a young man as Ben Burton in it, evidence or no evidence.”

“Miss Alice is right,” said Neddie Ellis, “I always did dislike Ben Burton, and I would have voted against him, if it had not been that he was such a good man at shortstop that I couldn’t think of putting my little prejudices against what seemed to be the good of the nine.”

===Chinese===
「你确实说过，豪厄尔小姐。」拉里装出一副垂头丧气的样子答道。「倘若我们当时有任何切实的怀疑，或任何一点证据，证明他手脚不干净，我们自然会同你建议的那样提防他。可我们手里只有那么一点含含糊糊的暗示，怎么好凭它跟自己队里的人过不去呢？」

「要是*我*是卡塔帕俱乐部的一员，」姑娘意气激昂地说，「我才不会让本·伯顿这么恶毒的年轻人留在队里，管它有没有证据。」

「爱丽丝小姐说得对。」内迪·埃利斯说。「我从来就不喜欢本·伯顿，我本会投票反对他入队的，要不是他守游击手守得实在太出色，我实在没法把自己那点小小的偏见，摆在看似关乎全队利益的天平上。」

===Original===
Once more it was agreed that it was useless to discuss the matter until the party had reached home, when the charges against Burton, and the evidence, if there were any, would be brought up in due form.

By the time the players and their friends had embarked on the westbound train, next day, they had recovered somewhat their usual high spirits. The buoyancy of youth and the natural hopefulness of healthy young fellows like these came to their relief, and the gay, chattering party that took possession of one end of a railway car, that morning, could hardly have been compared with the depressed and angry knot of youngsters that had discussed defeat and treachery, the night before. If they had been sold out, they argued to themselves, and had still fairly held their own against the famed Calumets, what was not possible for the team when purged of an unworthy member?

===Chinese===
大家再次商定，在回到家乡之前讨论此事毫无用处；等到了家，对伯顿的指控，以及证据——如果有的话——自会按正当程序提出。

第二天，等到球员们和他们的朋友们登上西行的火车时，他们已多少恢复了平日的高昂兴致。青春的轻盈快活和这些健壮小伙子天生的乐观帮了他们的忙；那天早晨，这支欢声笑语、占了车厢一头的队伍，同前一天晚上那群议论着败绩与叛卖、又沮丧又愤怒的少年们相比，简直判若两队。他们心里盘算着：要是被人出卖了，还能同赫赫有名的卡柳梅特队拼个旗鼓相当，那么等清除掉一个不配留队的成员之后，这支队伍还有什么办不到的呢？

===Original===
So they neared home with hearts lightened of a grievous burden and were once more cheered with the reflection that they had achieved one notable victory, at least, since their departure for Chicago, although a defeat counterbalanced that triumph.

And when the train drew up before the Catalpa depot, the returning adventurers were gladdened by the sight of innumerable flags flying over the town in the distance. They were to be received with congratulations, after all, not as humiliated captives.

“That is because we come home neck and neck, I s’pose,” said “The Lily,” as the notes of a brass band startled his ample ear.

“It’s because we are not so badly off as we might be, Billy boy,” replied Larry Boyne.

===Chinese===
就这样，他们卸下心头一副沉重的负担，临近家门，一想到自出征芝加哥以来至少已赢得一场可观的胜利，便重新振奋起来——尽管一场败绩抵消了那份荣光。

当火车停在卡塔帕站前，凯旋而归的冒险者远远望见镇上飘扬着的无数旗帜，不由得喜上心头。他们到底是要受人祝贺的，而不是作为蒙受羞辱的俘虏回来。

「我猜，这是因为我们不分胜负地打平了回家。」「百合」说，铜管乐队的乐声惊动了他那对大耳朵。

「这是因为我们的处境还不算糟到无可救药，比尔小子。」拉里·博因答道。

## VIII A Turn of the Tide / VIII 潮势逆转

===Original===
Defeat, utter and overwhelming, followed the Catalpas to Bluford, where they played the “Zoo-Zoo Nine” of that city. The “Zoo-Zoos” were picked players, the lineal descendants of a company of Illinois Zouaves renowned in the Civil War for their bravery, dash, and skill as skirmishers. The original founders of the club had long since disappeared from the field of action, but their successors bore up the banner of their illustrious namesakes with infinite credit. None of the Catalpa people had gone to Bluford to witness the game, Al Heaton being sick at home and the other immediate friends of the Nine being too busy with their farms and merchandise.
