And so it happened that the only news that came to the town from Bluford dribbled in from the Keokuck evening papers, sent by wire to the editor of *The Catalpa Leaf*, late at night. Mr. Downey did not think it worth while to post on his bulletin board the discouraging news that the “Zoo-Zoo Nine” had beaten the Catalpas by a score of eleven to one. But the news got out, of course, for the whole town was on the alert to hear the result from Bluford.

Albert Heaton was sitting up in bed, alternately shaken with ague and parched with fever, when his little sister brought him the unwelcome tidings. He groaned aloud and asked if Alice Howell had heard the news. Mrs. Heaton, a motherly woman who had no patience with baseball players that go about the country, like circus-riders, remarked, with some asperity, that she should suppose that Judge Howell would put a stop to Alice’s giving so much time and attention to baseball. For herself, if she had a grown-up daughter, she would try and put something else into her head than baseball and such mannish and vulgar doings. If Alice’s mother was alive, it would be mighty different in the Howell family. As it was, the Judge allowed Alice to do just about as she pleased, and it was a shame, so it was, for a nice young girl like Alice to be permitted to make a tomboy of herself. Flirting with that young Irish fellow from Sugar Grove! Did anybody ever hear of the like?

===Chinese===
彻底而惨重的失败，一路跟着卡塔帕队到了布卢福德（Bluford），他们在那儿同该市的「祖祖九人队」（Zoo-Zoo Nine）交了手。「祖祖队」是精选出来的好手，是一支伊利诺伊祖阿夫连队的嫡系传人——那支连队在南北战争中以骁勇、剽悍和散兵战的技艺闻名。俱乐部的创始人们早已从征场上消失，但他们的后继者把那响亮名号的旗帜扛得光彩照人。卡塔帕没有人去布卢福德观战，阿尔·希顿在家病着，九人队的其他至交好友又都忙于农场和买卖。于是，从布卢福德传回镇上的唯一消息，是从基奥卡克（Keokuck）的晚报上一点一滴渗漏来的——电报深夜拍给了《卡塔帕叶报》（*The Catalpa Leaf*）的编辑。唐尼先生（Mr. Downey）觉得，把「祖祖九人队」以十一比一击败卡塔帕队这个令人丧气的消息贴上公告栏，实在不值得。可消息当然还是传开了，因为全镇的人都竖着耳朵等着布卢福德的战报。

阿尔伯特·希顿正靠坐在床上，一会儿疟疾发作抖个不停，一会儿又烧得口干舌燥，他的小妹妹给他带来了这个不受欢迎的消息。他大声呻吟起来，问爱丽丝·豪厄尔听说了没有。希顿太太是位慈母般的妇人，对那些像马戏团骑手一样四处闯荡的棒球球员毫无耐心；她语带尖刻地说，她倒以为，豪厄尔法官早该制止爱丽丝把这么多时间和心思花在棒球上。换成她自己，若有个成年女儿，她定要设法往女儿脑袋里装点别的东西，而不是棒球这类男人气的粗俗玩意儿。爱丽丝的母亲若是还在世，豪厄尔家绝不会是这个样子。如今呢，法官任由爱丽丝想干什么就干什么，这真是丢脸事——千真万确是丢脸事——像爱丽丝这样一个好端端的年轻姑娘，竟被纵容成了个野丫头。还跟舒格格罗夫（Sugar Grove）来的那个爱尔兰小伙子眉来眼去！谁听说过这种事？

===Original===
“Oh, mother,” sighed poor Albert. “If you only knew how sick and sore I am for the boys, you would let up on Larry. If you had let me go with the Nine, perhaps I might have helped them out of the defeat. At any rate, it might have been less of a clean-out than it is. Dear me! How cold I am! Cover me up and let me be.”

With a pang of remorse at having added unwittingly to Albert’s sufferings, his mother soothed the sick boy and left him to sorrowful meditations. “And I was fool enough to think that the boys would be able to challenge the Calumets.” With these repentant meditations, Albert sunk into a feverish and uneasy sleep. He might have dreamed (perhaps he did) that at that very moment, Alice Howell was looking out into the gloom of the moist summer night and lamenting with bitterness the defeat of “our nine.”

===Chinese===
「唉，妈妈，」可怜的阿尔伯特叹道。「您要是知道我为伙计们有多难受、多痛心，就不会再数落拉里了。您要是让我跟九人队一起去，说不定我还能帮他们跳出这场败局。至少，也不至于输得像现在这样一败涂地。天哪！我好冷！给我盖好被子，别管我了。」

母亲想到自己无意中加重了阿尔伯特的病痛，一阵悔恨袭上心头，她安抚了病中的孩子，留下他独自黯然神伤。「我竟然傻到以为，伙计们能有本事向卡柳梅特队挑战。」怀着这些懊悔的念头，阿尔伯特沉入了燥热不安的睡眠。他也许梦见了（说不定真梦见了）——就在那一刻，爱丽丝·豪厄尔正望着潮湿夏夜的幽暗出神，为「我们九人队」的败绩黯然神伤。

===Original===
Next day, when *The Leaf* came out, and fuller particulars of the game were made known in a despatch from Charlie King, there was nothing to mitigate the gloom of the friends of the Catalpas. Singularly enough, some of the Dean County Nine, who had been among the most enthusiastic boomers of the Catalpa Nine, now assumed a most discouraging attitude. They were sure, so they said, that the Catalpas would be defeated all along the line. They had won the game at Sandy Key by a scratch. They had found their true level in Bluford. They would be beaten along the river, for it was well known that the nines in the river towns were far ahead of those in the interior of the state.

===Chinese===
第二天，《叶报》出来了，查利·金发来的电讯披露了比赛的更多细节，可没有什么能减轻卡塔帕队朋友们的阴郁。奇怪的是，迪安县九人队（Dean County Nine）里的一些人——他们本是卡塔帕九人队最起劲的吹鼓手——如今却摆出一副大泼冷水的架势。他们说，卡塔帕队准会一路输到底。他们在桑迪基（Sandy Key）那场球赢得侥幸。他们在布卢福德才现出了真实的斤两。到了沿河一带他们还要输，因为河畔城镇的九人队远比本州内陆的高明，这是人所共知的。

===Original===
Something of this talk reached the ears of Al Heaton, who was still suffering from fever-and-ague. He took up his bottle of cholagogue and shook it at his terrified little brother (who had retailed the gossip of the drug store, where he had been sent on an errand), and said, “If you hear any such infernal nonsense as that, downtown, Dan, you go and tell Tom Selby that I want him to lick the first fellow that says anything against our nine. Do you mind me?”

Little Dan promised stoutly that he would give Tom the message. Whether he did or not, it came to pass that Henry Jackson and Thomas Selby had a discussion, that very night, and that Dr. Selby sent his son home with strict injunctions to cover his face with brown paper and vinegar, while the big-fisted Henry went to bed with a bit of raw beef on his eye.

===Chinese===
这些闲话有几句传到了阿尔·希顿的耳朵里，他的寒热病还没有好利索。他抓起那瓶驱胆药水，朝吓坏了的小弟弟晃着（这孩子是被打发去药店办事的，把那边的闲话学舌了回来），说：「丹（Dan），你要是在街上再听见这种混账胡话，就去告诉汤姆·塞尔比，我要他狠揍第一个说咱们九人队坏话的家伙。听明白了没有？」

小丹一口应承，说一定把话带到。不管他带没带到，反正当天夜里，亨利·杰克逊（Henry Jackson）和托马斯·塞尔比（Thomas Selby）果然「理论」了一番：塞尔比医生严令儿子回家用棕纸蘸醋敷脸，而那位拳头硕大的亨利则贴着一片生牛肉上了床，敷在眼睛上。

===Original===
There is nothing like news from the field of battle to bring out the partisan feelings of a community far from the scene of strife. Catalpa was stirred to its very depths by the ill tidings brought from Bluford. Those who disapproved of baseball asserted themselves in the most unexpected and exasperating manner. Nobody had suspected that there were in Catalpa so many who sympathized not with the home nine and who secretly wished that they might be defeated. But the fact that the nine had met with disaster only stimulated their friends to new courage and stronger hopes for the future. This was a time, they said, for the friends of the nine to show themselves. Mr. Heaton sent an encouraging despatch to Larry Boyne, assuring him that the temporary reverse had only strengthened the confidence of home friends of the club. Even Judge Howell, who was greatly concerned lest the nine should be unduly depressed by their reverses, authorized Lewis Morris to write to Hiram Porter, as Captain of the club, and say to him that the club must be prepared for occasional defeats and that the next news from “the front” would undoubtedly be inspiring to the many supporters of the Catalpas.

===Chinese===
再没有什么比战场传来的消息，更能激发远离战场的乡镇的党派情绪了。布卢福德传来的噩耗把卡塔帕从底到面搅了个透。那些不赞成棒球的人，以最出人意料、最叫人冒火的方式跳了出来。谁也没想到，卡塔帕竟有这么多不向着主队、暗地里巴不得他们输球的人。然而九人队遭遇惨败这一事实，反倒激发他们的朋友们鼓起了新的勇气、怀着更强烈的希望。他们说，如今正是九人队的朋友们挺身而出的时候。希顿先生给拉里·博因发去一封鼓舞人心的电报，向他保证，这一时的挫败只会增强俱乐部家乡友人对它的信心。就连豪厄尔法官——他十分挂念，唯恐九人队被连番失利压垮——也授权刘易斯·莫里斯（Lewis Morris）写信给俱乐部队长海勒姆·波特，告诉他俱乐部必须做好偶尝败绩的准备，而「前线」传来的下一个消息，无疑将令卡塔帕队众多的支持者为之一振。

===Original===
“The Judge is a brick!” said Larry Boyne, when this message was read to the members of the club, as they lounged in one of the bedrooms of Quapaw House, in Galena, where the boys were waiting to begin the championship series of games with the Red Stockings.

“That’s just what he is!” exclaimed “The Lily,” bringing his somewhat battered fist down with emphasis on a convenient pillow. Bill had had hard luck in the late contest. His fingers had been badly sprained and twisted, and he had played with infinite difficulty on account of the battering that he had received in a game played with the Fulton City Nine, when the Catalpas were on their way to Bluford from Sandy Key. But he was still confident and determined.

===Chinese===
「这位法官真是条汉子！」有人向全体队员念这封信时，拉里·博因说。他们正懒散地躺在加利纳（Galena）夸帕旅馆（Quapaw House）的一间卧室里，小伙子们在那儿等着同红袜队（Red Stockings）拉开冠军系列赛的战幕。

「他正是这样的人！」「百合」喊道，把他那只吃了些苦头的拳头重重地砸在顺手的一只枕头上。比尔在最近那场比赛里运气糟透了。他的手指扭伤得不轻，都有些变形了；卡塔帕队从桑迪基前往布卢福德途中同富尔顿城九人队（Fulton City Nine）打了一场比赛，他在那场球里挨了不少磕碰，打起球来困难重重。但他依然信心十足、意志坚定。

===Original===
“I suppose some of the folks at home think that we are going to get beaten right along, every day from this out,” he continued, with a scornful laugh. “They don’t know us, do they, Larry? They don’t know what we had to contend with in Bluford, what with being used up with that hard ride on the strap-iron railroad and the lame fingers of your humble servant. Oh, yes, I suppose there is downheartedness among the boys at home.”

“But I know one chap who is not downhearted,” said Larry Boyne, cheerfully, “and that is Al Heaton. He will never get discouraged, whatever happens. And then there is his father, his despatch shows where he stands. Al is clear grit and so is his father; you may depend on that, boys.”

===Chinese===
「我猜家里有些人以为，从今往后我们天天都要吃败仗。」他带着轻蔑的笑继续说。「他们不了解我们，对吧，拉里？他们不知道我们在布卢福德吃了什么苦头——乘那条铁带铁路一路颠簸得筋疲力尽，外加鄙人这双残废的手指。哦，是啊，我看家乡的伙伴们中间准是有人灰心丧气了。」

「可是有一个人我知道他绝不灰心。」拉里·博因欢快地说，「那就是阿尔·希顿。无论出了什么事，他绝不会泄气。还有他父亲，他那封电报表明了他的立场。阿尔是条硬汉，他父亲也是；这一点你们尽管放心，伙计们。」

===Original===
Ben Burton, who had virtually lost the game in Bluford by his repeated muffing of the ball, as well as by his failure at the bat, sneered as he said, “I suppose a certain young lady in North Catalpa prompted the Judge’s despatch, didn’t she, Larry?”

Larry, with reddening cheeks, protested that he had no idea that Judge Howell needed any prompting from anybody to send a good word to the boys when they were away from home; he was too kindhearted a man, although a little stiff, to require any hint from outsiders to do the fair thing by the Baseball Club in whose welfare he had already shown great interest.

“I didn’t say ‘outsiders,’ Larry,” replied Burton, persistently. “I said that he was probably prompted by a young lady.”

At this, Larry deliberately rose and walked out of the room, without a word.

===Chinese===
本·伯顿在布卢福德那场球里，接二连三地漏接球，击球又尽皆落空，等于是他把比赛输掉的。他冷笑着说：「我猜，法官那封电报是北卡塔帕某位年轻小姐授意的吧，是不是，拉里？」

拉里涨红了脸，抗议说他绝不相信豪厄尔法官还需要谁来授意，才会给出门在外的伙计们捎句好话；他是个心肠太好的人——尽管有点古板——绝不需要外人提点，才会善待他早已深切关怀的棒球俱乐部。

「我可没说『外人』，拉里。」伯顿不依不饶地答道。「我是说，多半是位年轻小姐授意的。」

听了这话，拉里不慌不忙地站起身，一言不发地走出了房间。

===Original===
“I say, Ben, can’t you quit your everlasting nagging of Larry,” broke in Hiram Porter, as the door closed with a bang behind that indignant young man. “What’s the use of your getting into a debate, every day or two, about some mysterious young lady that you two fellows are thinking about? Let up! I wish you would.”

Ben muttered something about the Captain’s showing his little brief authority in matters that did not concern the club, when, by general consent, the meeting was broken up for the more important business of practice on the Galena Baseball Grounds, placed at the disposal of the visitors by the managers of the championship series.

===Chinese===
「我说，本，你就不能别没完没了地挖苦拉里吗？」那位愤然的年轻人砰地一声带上门出去后，海勒姆·波特插话道。「隔三差五就为你们俩惦记着的那位神秘年轻小姐抬一通杠，有什么意思？收敛点吧！我求你了。」

本咕哝了几句，说队长这是在不关俱乐部的事务上耍他那一星半点的威风；这时大家一致同意散会，去干更要紧的正事——到加利纳棒球场练球，那场地是冠军系列赛的主办者们拨给客队使用的。

## III After the Battle / III 大战之后

===Original===
To say that the town of Catalpa was very deeply mortified by this latest and most signal defeat of the favorite Nine would be a mild way of putting the case. For weeks afterwards, nothing was talked of in the place but the disgraceful overthrow of the Catalpa Nine. Very soon, so high did the debate run, there were two sides formed among the townspeople, one party blaming the Catalpas for their lack of training and practice, and the other excusing them for their evident inability to cope with the sturdy farmer boys from “down the river.”

“I tell you it is not mere brute muscle that our fellows want,” said Squire Mead, one of the great lights of the town, “it’s not brawn, but skill, that they must acquire before they can stand up against the baseball players of this part of the country. Let them pay more attention to work, and less to frills, and they will come out all right.”

===Chinese===
要说卡塔帕镇因为心爱的九人队遭此最新、最惨的一次败绩而深感受辱，还只是轻描淡写的说法。此后几个星期，镇上谈的没有别的，全是卡塔帕九人队那场丢尽脸面的惨败。很快，争论愈演愈烈，镇民分成了两派：一派指责卡塔帕队缺乏训练和练习，另一派则为它们开脱，说他们分明敌不过「河下游」来的那些壮实农家小伙。

「我跟你们说，咱们的小伙子缺的可不只是蛮力气，」镇上的一位头面人物米德老爷（Squire Mead）说，「他们要同这片地方的棒球球员较量，必须先学到手的不是膂力，而是技艺。让他们多在正经功夫上下心，少在花架子上费神，自然会有出息。」

===Original===
But Dr. Selby, whose son was one of the rising players in the less aristocratic Dean County Nine, would have none of this sort of argument. Tom Selby was not only a wiry and agile player in the field, but he was the best oarsman on the river, and he could lift a barrel of flour, properly slung, “without turning a hair.” He had done it often. His father believed in muscle.

“Now there’s Bill Van Orman, the Dean County Nine’s catcher,” Dr. Selby would say, “who is like an ox in appearance, and I really believe could stave in the panel of that door with one blow of his fist, but who gets about the bases as spry as a cat, and who has got down the curve to such a fine point that nobody can pitch like him in half a dozen counties. Sam Ellis, the champion pitcher of the Jonesvilles, cannot hold a candle to Van’s pitching. And do you pretend to tell me that any light-waisted young fellow, like Will Sprague, for instance, could ever, by all the training in the world, make such a catcher or such a pitcher as Bill?”

===Chinese===
可塞尔比医生的公子正是门第不那么显赫的迪安县九人队里崭露头角的球员之一，他全然听不进这套论调。汤姆·塞尔比在场上不仅是个坚韧敏捷的球员，还是全河最好的桨手，一桶吊扎妥当的面粉，他「眉头都不皱一下」就能提起来。他常干这事。他父亲信奉的是膂力。

「瞧瞧迪安县九人队的捕手比尔·范奥曼，」塞尔比医生总爱这么说，「他生得像头牛，我真信他一拳能把那扇门的门板砸穿，可他跑起垒来却灵巧如猫，而且把弧线球练得炉火纯青，方圆六七个县没人投得过他。琼斯维尔队的王牌投手萨姆·埃利斯（Sam Ellis），给范的投球提鞋都不配。你们还想告诉我，哪个细腰身的小伙子——比如威尔·斯普拉格那样——靠天底下所有的训练，能练成比尔这样的捕手或投手？」

===Original===
It was the old question over again﻿—skill against muscle. But Judge Howell, whose opinions on all subjects whatever commanded respect, probably gave voice to the average public judgment when he said, “What we want, gentlemen, is muscle *and* training. I am confident that in this good town of Catalpa there are more than nine young men who can give time to the practice necessary for the purpose, and who are endowed by nature with the requisite powers for the development of first-rate baseball players.”

“Good for you, Jedge!” It was Tony May, an aged and disreputable loafer in the store where this debate was taking place, who spoke. Tony was usually called “Rough and Ready” because of his frequent use of that phrase as applied to himself. Having applauded the Judge’s remark, he drew back, a little confusedly, and murmured “ ‘Scuse me, Jedge, I didn’t mean to be interruptious, but you know I’m rough and ready, rough and ready, Jedge, and that ’ere remark of yourn does seem to be about the fust sensible thing I’ve hearn in this ’ere jag of words. ‘Scuse me, Jedge, fer sayin’ so; you know I’m rough and I’m ready.” And the speaker subsided into a corner pulling his coonskin cap down over his shaggy brows.

===Chinese===
这又是那个老问题——技艺对膂力。不过豪厄尔法官无论对什么问题的高见都受人敬重，他这番话大概道出了一般公众的判断：「先生们，我们需要的是膂力*加*训练。我确信，在卡塔帕这个好镇上，拿得出必要时间参加所需训练的年轻人不止九个，而且他们天生就具备造就一流棒球球员的必要素质。」

「说得好，法官老爷！」说话的是托尼·梅（Tony May），辩论所在的店铺里一个上了年纪、不大体面的闲汉。大家平常都叫他「老硬汉」（Rough and Ready），因为他老爱把这话套在自己身上。他为法官的高见喝过彩，便有点讪讪地缩回去，喃喃道：「您老别怪，法官老爷，俺不是存心插嘴捣乱，可您知道俺是个粗人，粗是粗，顶得上用，法官老爷，您老这句话，实在是俺在这一堆子话里听见的头一件在理的话。俺这么说您老可别怪；您知道，俺粗归粗，随时顶得上。」说完，这位发言人便缩进角落，把浣熊皮帽拉下来盖住他那两道乱蓬蓬的眉毛。

===Original===
Judge Howell, with an additional stiffness perceptible in his manner, waved his hand towards the dry goods boxes in the angles of which “Rough and Ready” had dropped and said, “Our friend here is enthusiastic. He has a right to be. His son Fremont has certainly distinguished himself, before now, as the right fielder of the Dean County Nine. But does anybody know if that handsome young Irish lad, Larry Boyne, could be drawn from the Jonesville Nine, in case we should desire to reinforce our home nine by drafts on foreign material, so to speak?”

Nobody knew; but Jason Elderkin, the storekeeper, leaned over his counter, pausing in his occupation of measuring off a yard of Kentucky jean, and said:

“I tell you what it is, Judge, that’s the likeliest young fellow in these parts. He lives with his mother over to Sugar Grove, and started in to read law with ’Squire Welby, over to Dean Center; but he had to give it up on account of his father’s being killed by being crushed under a tree that he was felling. Awful blow to the boy, likewise to his ma. The Jonesvilles pay him something for playing with them; so I’ve hearn tell.”

===Chinese===
豪厄尔法官的举止里透出一分加重的拘谨，他朝「老硬汉」瘫坐其中的布匹货箱的箱角挥了挥手，说：「我们这位朋友满腔热忱。他有这个资格。他的儿子弗里蒙特（Fremont）从前就作为迪安县九人队的右野手崭露过头角。不过，假如我们想——不妨这么说——借外来的好手充实我们的主队，有谁知道能不能把那个英俊的爱尔兰小伙子拉里·博因从琼斯维尔九人队里挖过来？」

没人知道；不过店主贾森·埃尔德金（Jason Elderkin）正量着一码肯塔基粗斜纹布，这时停下活计，探身越过柜台说：

「我告诉您吧，法官，那是这一带最出挑的小伙子。他跟他母亲住在那边的舒格格罗夫，起先在迪安森特（Dean Center）跟韦尔比老爷（’Squire Welby）学法律；可他父亲伐树时被倒下的树砸死了，他只得作罢。这对那孩子是个可怕的打击，对他娘也是。琼斯维尔队给他打球是付钱的；俺是这么听说的。」

===Original===
This suggestion created a momentary stir in the congress, for the gathering had by this time assumed such a character. Two or three of the speakers did not see how anybody could think of making a professional club out of an amateur, such as the Jonesville Nine pretended to be. If Larry Boyne was paid a salary, why were not others? And if salaries were paid to the men, it was a professional club, wasn’t it?

“I don’t know enough about what we may call the etiquette of the game to decide what is an amateur and what a professional club,” remarked Judge Howell, in slow and dignified accents. “But if we are in earnest in this proposition to organize a really creditable baseball club in Catalpa, and I take it that we are,”﻿—and here he glanced at “Rough and Ready,” who had slid forward into sight again﻿—“and I take it that we are, I say, we may as well make up our minds to put our hands into our pockets and help the boys a little, otherwise we shall go down again.”

“Right as a trivet, Jedge,” cried Rough and Ready. “Right as a trivet; for unless we take hold all together, we shell go down to where flour is nine dollars a bar’l and no money to buy it at that; ’scuse me, gen’lemen, but I’m rough and ready, you know. I allow that the Jedge here speaks the senterments of the community.” And the old man retreated into the depths of his coonskin cap.

===Chinese===
这个提议在这场「国会」里掀起了一阵小小的骚动——聚到这时，这场集会已经有了这般性质。有两三位发言者不明白，怎么能把一个业余俱乐部——比如琼斯维尔九人队所自称的那种——弄成职业俱乐部。既然给拉里·博因发薪水，为什么不给别人发？既然给球员们发薪水，那就是职业俱乐部了，不是吗？

「对于我们可以称之为这项比赛之规矩的东西，我知道得还不够，断不定哪个算业余、哪个算职业俱乐部。」豪厄尔法官语调缓慢而庄重地说。「但倘若我们是真心要在卡塔帕组织一个真正拿得出手的棒球俱乐部——我认为我们是的」——说到这里，他朝又蹭到人前来的「老硬汉」瞥了一眼——「我认为我们是的，我说，那我们不妨下定决心，把手伸进腰包，帮小伙子们一把，不然我们还得再栽跟头。」

「一点儿不差，法官老爷，」「老硬汉」嚷道。「一点儿不差；因为咱们要是不一齐上心，就得败落到面粉卖九块钱一桶、还掏不出钱买的地步；老爷们别怪，俺是个粗人，可顶得上用。俺琢磨着，这位法官老爷说的，正是大伙儿的心里话。」老头说完，又缩回了浣熊皮帽的深处。

===Original===
The oracle of the grocery store was right in saying that Judge Howell spoke the sentiments of the community in regard to the necessity of taking hold in earnest and organizing a baseball club, if anything serious was to be accomplished. The project took definite shape at once.

“Why,” said Weeks, the bridge-tender, who, from his position, came into contact with half of the townspeople, nearly every day, as they crossed and recrossed the river. “Why, every town north of Bloomington, as far as I know, has got a champion baseball nine, and why should Catalpa be behind the rest? That’s what I want to know. And if we are to have champions, we have got to take hold and help the boys, like they do in other towns. And the very first thing I want to see done is the licking of them Jonesvilles. They are so everlastingly set up by their carrying off the pennant that they are ready to challenge all creation. So I’m told.”

===Chinese===
杂货店的这位「先知」说得没错：豪厄尔法官确实道出了社区的心声——要想干成任何像样的事，就必须认真上心、组织一个棒球俱乐部。这个计划当即有了明确的眉目。

「嗐，」桥梁看守人威克斯（Weeks）说——凭他的位置，镇上一半的人来回过河，差不多天天都要同他打照面。「嗐，据我所知，布卢明顿（Bloomington）以北的每个镇子都有一支冠军棒球九人队，凭什么卡塔帕要落在人后？我就想弄明白这个。咱们既然要有冠军，就得像别的镇子那样上心帮小伙子们一把。而我头一件想看到的，就是狠狠揍那帮琼斯维尔队一顿。他们夺了锦旗，就得意得没了边儿，恨不得向普天之下的所有队伍叫阵。人家是这么告诉我的。」

===Original===
Around many an evening fire and in many a lounging-place in the town, the question was animatedly discussed, as autumn waned into winter, and most outdoor sports became a little unseasonable. It was decided, in that informal and irregular way with which a western community settles its internal affairs, that there must be in Catalpa a first-rate baseball nine, and that it must be organized before the spring opened.

===Chinese===
随着秋去冬来，大多数户外运动渐渐不合时令，这个问题在镇上许多夜晚的炉火边、许多闲坐的场所被热烈地讨论着。以西部社区处理内部事务那种不拘一格的方式，大家议定了：卡塔帕必须有一支一流的棒球九人队，而且必须在春天到来之前组织起来。

## XII After the Victory / XII 胜利之后

===Original===
“The boss says he would be obliged to you if you would make less noise.”

It was a tall and red-faced young man who brought this message to the Catalpa Nine, as they were gathered in the room of Captain Hiram Porter, in their lodging-house, after the great match game. Al Heaton had hurried to join the boys, as soon as he had sent to Catalpa his despatch announcing the result of the contest in the most glowing terms consistent with the rate of telegraph tolls and the needed conciseness of a despatch. All hands were in that flow of animal spirits that might have been expected from nearly a dozen young fellows who are elated over a great victory and who have laboriously repressed their jubilation until they are alone.

===Chinese===
「老板说，劳驾诸位，能不能少弄出点动静。」

给卡塔帕九人队捎来这话的，是个高个子红脸膛的年轻人；此时大赛方歇，他们正聚在寄宿旅馆里海勒姆·波特队长的房间里。阿尔·希顿早已给卡塔帕发完了电报——在电报资费与电文必需的简洁所容许的范围内，用最热烈的字眼宣告了比赛的结果——便赶忙来同伙计们会合。一屋子人都处在那股血气方刚的兴头上：将近一打年轻人为一场大胜欢腾鼓舞，又一直苦苦按捺着庆祝的冲动，直到只剩自家人时才爆发出来。

===Original===
“There! I told you, boys, that your skylarking would bring up the landlord. Oh, I say, Neddie, quit your fooling. You can’t throw ‘The Lily,’ if you try all night; and we are making such a racket that the whole house is disturbed.” This was Captain Porter’s admonition.

“Besides,” said Larry Boyne, who was panting with the unwonted exertion of boosting Charlie King over the headboard of the bedstead, where Charlie was determined he would not go, “besides all that, it’s time for you and me, Hi, to get ready to go out to dinner.”

“Where are you two fellows going to dinner?” demanded half a dozen voices at once. “Are you going to throw off on us in that way?”

Captain Hiram explained that he and Larry had accepted an invitation to take dinner with Judge Morris, with whose family Mr. Heaton and Albert were staying during the progress of the games in Chicago. The Morrises, he added, lived on the north side of the river, and he and Larry should be ready to start, instead of “cutting up” to show how tickled they were with their recent victory.

===Chinese===
「瞧！我早说过吧，伙计们，你们这么胡闹会把房东招上来的。哎呀，我说，内迪，别犯傻了。你就是折腾一整夜也摔不倒『百合』；再说咱们闹出的动静太大，整座房子都被吵得不安生了。」这是波特队长的告诫。

「再说，」拉里·博因说——他正费着不寻常的力气，把查利·金往床架的床头板那边推举，累得直喘，而查利打定主意死也不肯过去——「再说，海伊，你我都该收拾收拾，准备出门赴宴了。」

「你们两个家伙要去哪儿吃饭？」半打声音同时质问。「就这么把我们甩下不管了？」

海勒姆队长解释说，他和拉里已经应了莫里斯法官的邀请，要去他家吃晚饭；芝加哥赛事进行期间，希顿先生和阿尔伯特就寄住在莫里斯家。他补充说，莫里斯家住在河的北岸，他和拉里该动身了，不该再这样「闹腾」来显示他们对刚到手的胜利有多高兴。

===Original===
“But ’twas a famous victory,” quoted Larry, “for all that, and I would just as soon stay with the boys and celebrate it as go out to dine with Judge Morris, who, they say, is a heavy swell.”

“I happen to know that Miss Alice Howell and her friend Miss Ida are stopping with the Morrises, Larry,” said Ben Burton, with an unpleasant leer, “and you and Hiram will be in clover; so you can afford to shake us until the next game.”

Larry grew very red in the face at this, and there was a dangerous gleam in Hiram Porter’s eye as he noted the ill-natured scowl on Burton’s countenance. He restrained himself, however, and said, “Why do you continually harp on the Judge’s daughter, Ben? The young lady is from our own town, and she is more interested in the success of the Catalpas than some of its members, I reckon; at least, I think so, judging from appearances.”

===Chinese===
「不过，这确是一场著名的胜利，」拉里引了一句诗，「尽管如此，我倒宁可同伙计们留下来庆祝，也不愿出去陪莫里斯法官吃饭——听说他还是个了不起的大阔佬呢。」

「我恰好知道，爱丽丝·豪厄尔小姐和她的朋友艾达小姐眼下正住在莫里斯家，拉里，」本·伯顿面带不善的斜睨说，「你跟海勒姆这下要掉进福窝里了；所以嘛，你们大可以把我们晾到下一场比赛再理会。」

听了这话，拉里的脸涨得通红；海勒姆·波特注意到伯顿脸上那副恶狠狠的神色，眼里也闪过一丝危险的光。不过他忍住了，说：「你干吗老是揪着法官的女儿不放，本？这位小姐是咱们镇上的人，依我看，她对卡塔帕队成败的关切，比队里某些成员还要多；至少从种种迹象看，我是这么想的。」

===Original===
“What do you mean by that, Hi Porter?” demanded Ben, hotly. “You have insinuated that sort of thing too many times in my hearing. And I want you to understand that you can’t put on any captain’s airs over me, now that we are off the field. I am my own master for tonight anyway.”

“Come, come, boys,” interposed Larry, soothingly. “Don’t let us mar the enjoyment of this evening by lugging in any old quarrels or little differences. We shall all have to pull together tomorrow, if we are to beat the Calumets. They are going to give us a stiff brush, and you may depend on that. Come, Hiram, let’s be off.”

===Chinese===
「你这话什么意思，海伊·波特？」本火辣辣地质问。「这种含沙射影的话，我耳朵里听得太多了。我告诉你，现在下了球场，你休想再拿队长的架子压我。反正今晚我自己做自己的主。」

「好了，好了，伙计们。」拉里打圆场说。「别把陈年旧怨和小是小非扯进来，搅了今晚的兴致。明天要想打赢卡柳梅特队，咱们大伙儿都得齐心协力。他们准会给咱们来一场硬仗，这一点你们尽管放心。走吧，海勒姆，咱们出发。」

===Original===
Burton said something, sullenly and indistinctly, about the certainty of the defeat of the Catalpas, tomorrow, which caught the ear of “The Lily,” who, still puffing with the effects of his tussle with Neddie Ellis, was regarding the malcontent Ben with an expression of wonder on his good-natured face. He slowly dropped out a few words of comment, in his usual fashion, upon Burton’s unfriendly attitude and then added:

“I say, I wonder why you don’t give up playing baseball, since you find so little fun in it. ’Pears to me you are all the time out ’o sorts-like. You don’t enjoy good health, Ben, and that’s what is the matter along of you. Now, why do you think that the Calumets are going to get away with us, tomorrow?”

But before Ben could form a reply and cover the confusion that crept over his face, Neddie Ellis, who was the universal favorite of the club, broke in with, “Oh, I say, boys, do you know what these Chicago people call us? why they call us ‘The Cats.’ That’s short for Catalpas, I suppose. We ought to call the Calumets ‘The Cads,’ and I guess that would be getting even.”

===Chinese===
伯顿阴沉着脸、含含糊糊地咕哝了几句，说明天卡塔帕队必败无疑，这话落进了「百合」的耳朵——他同内迪·埃利斯角力一番还没喘匀气，正用那张憨厚的脸带着诧异的神情打量这个满腹牢骚的本。他以惯常的慢条斯理的腔调，对伯顿这副不友好的态度评论了几句，接着又说：

「我说，真不明白你既然觉得打棒球这么没意思，干吗不干脆撂挑子不打了。依我看，你好像老是提不起精神似的。你身子骨不爽利，本，毛病就出在这上头。倒是说说，你凭什么觉得卡柳梅特队明天准能收拾了咱们？」

可本还没来得及想出答话、遮住爬上脸去的慌乱，内迪·埃利斯——他是全队上下人见人爱的宝贝——就插进嘴来：「哎呀，我说，伙计们，你们知道这些芝加哥人管咱们叫什么吗？他们叫咱们『猫队』（The Cats）。我猜是卡塔帕队的简称吧。咱们该管卡柳梅特队叫『痞子队』（The Cads），我琢磨着这就算扯平了。」

===Original===
Under cover of the laugh which this sally raised, Hiram, Larry, and young Heaton departed to fulfil their engagement on the north side, Ben Burton looking after them with a darkened countenance.

“Ben is angry because he is not invited to Judge Morris’s,” said Larry, as the three young fellows stepped lightly off in search of a street car. “He has a jealous temper, and the least thing that looks like a slight sets him off.”

“Well,” said Albert, “Alice said that the Judge would have liked to have invited the whole nine, if he had had room to entertain them properly; but he hadn’t, and so he invited only those with whom the governor was most acquainted.”

“To say nothing of Miss Alice?” added Hiram, slyly.

Albert admitted that Miss Alice’s wishes were consulted in the matter, and that it was only natural that she, being a visitor, should indicate her preferences in the matter.

===Chinese===
趁着这句妙语激起的一片哄笑，海勒姆、拉里和小希顿动身去赴河北岸的约，本·伯顿阴沉着脸目送他们离去。

「本生气是因为没请他去莫里斯法官家。」三个小伙子轻快地出发去找有轨电车时，拉里说。「他性好嫉妒，一丁点看似怠慢的小事就能点着他的火。」

「唉，」阿尔伯特说，「爱丽丝说，法官倒是想请全体九人，倘若他有地方像样地款待他们的话；可他没有，所以就只请了那些同我家老爷子最熟的人。」

「更别说还有爱丽丝小姐的意思呢？」海勒姆狡黠地补了一句。

阿尔伯特承认，这件事上也征求过爱丽丝小姐的意思；她既是客人，在这件事上表露一下自己的偏好，本是再自然不过。

===Original===
“What does it signify, anyhow?” said Larry, a little impatiently. “It seems to me that Ben Burton is ready to fly out at the least provocation. I almost wish we had never thought of going over to Judge Morris’s. I am sure I have tried my level best to keep the peace with Ben, but he seems to grow more and more cantankerous every day. To think of raising a breeze over such a trifle as this of our going out to dinner without him! It makes me ashamed of my companionship with him.”

The conversation was stopped by their entering a street car where they were entertained by the audible comments of the passengers on the wonderful game that had been played that afternoon. Baseball in Chicago is one of the favorite pastimes of the people. But there was so much of the element of unexpectedness in the result of that day’s game that it set the tongues of everybody to wagging. Unknown and in silence, the champions of the Catalpa Nine heard themselves and their playing discussed with great freedom and animation. The general verdict was that “The Cats” would, next day, receive their reward in the shape of a “basket of goose eggs” with which they would depart for home, sadder and wiser for their visit.

===Chinese===
「这到底有什么要紧的？」拉里有点不耐烦地说。「在我看来，本·伯顿简直是一点就着。我几乎后悔不该想着去莫里斯法官家。我敢说，我已经尽了最大努力同本和睦相处，可他好像一天比一天难缠。竟为咱们没带他出去吃饭这样的鸡毛蒜皮闹一场风波！这让我都为同他做伴感到羞臊。」

谈话被上电车打断了；车厢里乘客们高声议论着那天下午的精彩比赛，正好给他们解闷。棒球是芝加哥人最喜爱的消遣之一。可那天比赛的结果太出人意料，惹得人人的舌头都动了起来。卡塔帕九人队的冠军们默默无闻地坐在那里，听着人们毫无顾忌、兴致勃勃地议论他们自己和他们的球艺。大家的一致结论是：「猫队」明天将领到应有的报偿——「一篮子鸭蛋」，然后揣着这趟做客换来的教训和明白，灰溜溜地打道回府。

===Original===
“What do you think of that for an opinion, Larry?” asked Hiram, laughingly, as they alighted from the car, one block from their destination. “What do you think of the woman in the corner who said that the Calumets were only encouraging us on to our defeat?”

Larry replied that that was precisely what Ben Burton thought, and Hiram ejaculated, “Oh, he does, does he? Then it seems that our shortstop and our adversaries, or the friends of our adversaries, agree as to what is going to happen tomorrow.”

“Perhaps they are right,” said Albert, cheerily. “But here we are,” and stopping before a handsome house, he darted up the steps and rang the door bell.

While the lads waited for admission, Larry turned and looked westward, with wistful eyes, and said,

“I wonder how they are taking the news in Catalpa, about now?”

===Chinese===
「你对这个高论有何见教，拉里？」他们在离目的地一个街区的地方下了车，海勒姆笑着问。「你觉得角落里那位太太怎么样？她说卡柳梅特队不过是在纵着咱们，好把咱们往败局里送。」

拉里回答说，这恰恰就是本·伯顿的想法，海勒姆脱口而出：「哦，他是这么想的，是吗？那看来，咱们的游击手同咱们的对手——或者说对手的朋友们——对明天会发生什么，倒是意见一致。」

「说不定他们说得对呢。」阿尔伯特乐呵呵地说。「反正咱们到了。」他在一幢漂亮的宅子前停住，蹬蹬蹬奔上台阶，按响了门铃。

小伙子们等着开门的当口，拉里转过身，怅然望向西方，说：

「不知这会儿，卡塔帕那边收到消息是什么光景？」

===Original===
Albert’s reply that they were probably having a jollification really described what was at that moment taking place. Tom Selby was the happy recipient of early telegrams from Larry, and the editor of *The Leaf* sustained his reputation by putting out bulletins from Al Heaton and his father, at frequent intervals during the progress of the game. The excitement waxed high as the contest proceeded, and when the final result was reached, the town was fairly mad with joy. The event had eclipsed everything of the kind that had happened during the season. Every man who had a flag hung it out to the breeze. Jedediah Van Orman, “The Lily’s” father, took up a collection from the willing shopkeepers and bought a supply of powder, with which he proceeded to fire a salute from four anvils, the only artillery then accessible in the town. Victory brooded over Catalpa, and in every house as the red sun went down, that night, there was but one theme of conversation﻿—baseball.

===Chinese===
阿尔伯特回答说，那边多半正在欢庆——这话还真说中了此刻正在发生的事。汤姆·塞尔比幸运地最先收到了拉里拍来的几封电报；《叶报》的编辑也不负众望，在比赛进行期间频频张贴阿尔·希顿和他父亲发来的战报。随着比赛推进，群情越发热烈，等最终结果传来，全镇简直欣喜若狂。这场赛事压倒了本赛季同类事件中的一切。家有旗帜的人家，都把旗子挂出来迎风招展。「百合」的父亲杰代迪亚·范奥曼（Jedediah Van Orman）从一呼百应的店主们那里凑了一笔钱，买下一批火药，用它从四座铁砧上放起了礼炮——那是当时镇上唯一弄得着的炮火。胜利的气氛笼罩着卡塔帕，那天晚上，当红日西沉，家家户户谈论的话题只有一个——棒球。

## VI An Interesting Episode / 六 一段有趣的插曲

===Original===
Although the stock of the Catalpa Baseball Club was divided among many shareholders in the town of Catalpa, it was evident that the mere holding, or non-holding, of shares made no difference with those who were engaged in the active duties of playing. To be sure, the nine had not yet begun their summer campaign. The first of April was early enough for the beginning of outdoor practice, and active work in the field would not open until the first of May; but enough had been done, in the preliminary organization and preparing for the summer's work, to test the temper of the members of the club. It was not a purely businesslike venture into which these young men had gone for the purpose of making capital or money for themselves. They were burning to retrieve the reputation of "Old Catalpa" as they called their town, albeit it was one of the youngest in Northern Illinois.

And so, as Larry Boyne and Al Heaton were sitting on the rail fence that encloses the Court House of Dean County, in Catalpa, discussing the future prospects of the club, both were confidential and intimate in their exchange of opinions concerning the members of the nine.

"No, I tell you that you are wrong, Al, in your estimate of Ben Burton," said Larry, earnestly. "I do not think that I could be prejudiced against Ben; and I try to judge him fairly; and so I cannot bring myself to believe that he would be tricky, or that he would undertake to play any foul game on me, or on anybody else, for that matter. He is sullen and moody, at times, and I know that he took to heart his defeat as candidate for captain of the club. I know that he don't like me, although I don't know why he should dislike me, as he certainly does."

===Chinese===
虽然卡塔帕棒球俱乐部（Catalpa Baseball Club）的股份分在卡塔帕镇许多股东的手里，但显而易见，对那些承担实际打球职责的人来说，仅仅持有或不持有股份，并没有什么分别。诚然，九人队还没有开始他们的夏季征程。四月一日开始户外训练已经算早的了，正式上场操练要到五月一日才会展开；但在前期组织和夏季备战方面，要做的事已经够多，足以检验俱乐部成员们的脾性。这帮年轻人投身其中，并不是为给自己赚取资本或金钱的一桩纯买卖。他们一心渴望挽回「老卡塔帕」——他们如此称呼自己的镇子——的名声，尽管这座镇子已是北伊利诺伊最年轻的城镇之一。

于是，拉里·博因（Larry Boyne）和阿尔·希顿（Al Heaton）正坐在卡塔帕镇上迪安县法院四周的木栅栏上，谈论着俱乐部的前景；两人谈起九人队的队员来，都推心置腹、亲密无间。

「不，我跟你说，阿尔，你对本·伯顿（Ben Burton）的看法错了。」拉里恳切地说。「我想我对本不可能有什么偏见；我尽量公道地评判他；所以我没法让自己相信他会耍花招，也没法相信他会对我——或者对任何人——使什么阴损的伎俩。他有时候阴沉沉的、喜怒无常，我也知道他竞选俱乐部队长失利这件事一直让他耿耿于怀。我知道他不喜欢我，虽然我不明白他凭什么讨厌我——可他确实讨厌我。」

===Original===
"Pooh! Larry," was Albert's frank reply, "you know well enough that he fancies that you are in his way as a suitor for the hand of a certain young lady, whose name shall not be mentioned even in this very select society. He knows that that young lady smiles on you in the most bewitching way, and he knows—"

"Oh, see here, Al," interrupted Larry, with flaming cheeks, "you are riding your horse with a free rein, don't you think so? I have no right to think of any young lady with the seriousness you seem to put into the matter. I am young, poor, and without friends or influence."

"Hold on there, Larry," cried young Heaton, warmly. "You have no right to say that. You will never want for friends. You have a town-full of them, and when you need anyone to stand by and back you up in anything you undertake, you can just put out your hand, without getting off of this rail, to find one friend that will be the man to stand right there as long as he is wanted."

Larry laid his hand on Albert's knee as he said, "I know that, Al, and it is good to know it and to have you say it in that straightforward way of yours, and I will say too, that your father called me into the mill, the other day, and said pretty much the same thing to me; and he told me that he should consider it a favor, or something of that sort, if I would allow him to have a fatherly lookout for the folks at home, while I am off, this summer, in case anything should happen." And Larry's honest blue eyes filled with moisture as he looked far off over the outlying prairie, in the vain effort to conceal how deeply he had felt the kindness showed to him.

===Chinese===
「呸！拉里，」阿尔伯特（Albert）直率地答道，「你心里清楚得很：他认定你挡了他的道，害他追不上某位年轻小姐——她的名字嘛，就算在咱们这么挑拣的小圈子里也不便提起。他知道那位小姐朝你笑起来那叫一个勾人魂魄，他还知道——」

「哦，行了，阿尔，」拉里涨红了脸打断他，「你这马缰绳放得也太宽了，自己不觉得吗？我可没有权利像你说的那样一本正经地惦记哪位年轻小姐。我又年轻，又穷，没朋友，也没靠山。」

「打住，拉里，」小希顿热情地嚷道，「你可没权利这么说。你永远不会缺朋友。你有一整个镇子的朋友，等你干什么事需要有人撑腰打气的时候，你只消伸出手去——连这条栅栏都不必下——就能找到一位朋友，需要他站多久，他就站多久。」

拉里把手放在阿尔伯特的膝盖上，说道：「这我知道，阿尔。能知道这一点，又能听你用你这直来直去的方式说出来，真好。我还想说，前几天你父亲把我叫进磨坊，对我讲了大体相同的话；他说，今年夏天我不在家，如果我肯让他以父辈的心肠照看着我家里的人，万一有个什么事——他会把这看作赏脸，大概是这么个意思。」拉里那双诚实的蓝眼睛湿润了，他眺望着远处的大草原，想掩饰这份情谊在他心里激起的深深波澜，却是徒劳。

===Original===
"That was very good of the Governor, I'm sure," said Albert, stoutly, "and I don't care if he is my father of whom I am saying it. But it's nothing more than fair for him, and for the rest of us who stay at home, to do what we can to keep your mind at ease about your folks while you are out in the ball field for the summer. But what I was getting at is this: Ben Burton is down on you; he will try to get the advantage of you, if he can; and, what is of more consequence to all of us, he would not scruple to bring the whole club into disgrace for the sake of gratifying any selfish purpose that he might happen to have in view."

"But what evil purpose could he have?" demanded Larry.

"As I said before, I don't know. I don't want to do Ben an injustice, but I do know that he is underhanded and mean. So you look out for him. As far as his relations to you are concerned, I might say, if you were not so everlastingly toploftical about it, that he is jealous of you on account of your supposed good standing with Alice Howell—"

"Oh, hush‑h‑h‑h!" cried Larry, looking around in unfeigned consternation, to see if there were listeners near. "You really must not mention that young lady's name in that manner, nor in any manner connected with my own. It would be almost insulting to her, it would fill the Judge with wrath (and I shouldn't blame him for being angry), to know that gossiping young fellows like us were using his daughter's name in this light fashion."

"And why, I should like to know?" answered Albert. "He need not put on any high and mighty airs. I have heard my father say that when the Howellses came here from Kentucky, when the Stone River country was first settled, and old man Hixon was running his ferry across the stream here, they were so poor that they wore bed-ticking clothes, went barefoot, and lived on hog and hominy for many a year afterwards. Side-meat was good enough for them then. The fat of the land is not good enough for them now. It just makes me sick! Such airs!" And honest Albert got down from the fence to give freer expression to his deep disgust.

===Chinese===
「老爷子这么做真是好样的，我敢说，」阿尔伯特理直气壮地说，「哪怕我夸的是自家老子，我也不在乎。不过，趁你夏天在外头打球，他和我们这些留在家里的人尽力让你对家里人放宽心，这不过是分内之事，再公道不过了。可我想说的是这个：本·伯顿跟你过不去；只要办得到，他就会设法占你的上风；而且——这对我们大家更要紧——只要能满足他心里碰巧打着的什么自私算盘，他会毫不迟疑地把整个俱乐部拖进丢人现眼的境地。」

「可他又能有什么坏心思呢？」拉里追问。

「我刚才就说了，我不知道。我不想冤枉本，可我确实知道，这人阴一套阳一套，气量又小。所以你防着他点儿。至于他跟你之间的事儿嘛——要不是你在这上头永远端着那么高的架子，我倒想说：他嫉妒你，就因为人家都说你在爱丽丝·豪厄尔（Alice Howell）那儿吃得开——」

「哦，嘘——嘘——嘘！」拉里嚷道，慌慌张张地四下张望，看看附近有没有人偷听。「你真的不该那样提起那位小姐的名字，也不该把她的名字跟我的名字以任何方式扯到一块儿。要是让人知道，咱们这帮嚼舌头的年轻人这样轻飘飘地拿他女儿的名字说事儿，那对她几乎是种侮辱，也会让法官大人勃然大怒（他要是发火，我一点不怪他）。」

「我倒想问问，凭什么？」阿尔伯特回嘴道，「他犯不着摆什么高高在上的臭架子。我听我父亲说过，豪厄尔家刚从肯塔基迁来那会儿——那时斯通河一带才刚有人定居，老希克森还在河上摆渡——他们穷得身上穿的是床垫布做的衣裳，脚上连鞋都没有，往后好些年靠咸肉玉米糊过活。那会儿有腌五花吃就够好的了。如今山珍海味都配不上他们了。真叫我恶心！瞧那副德行！」诚实的阿尔伯特从栅栏上跳下来，好让他满腔的厌恶抒发得更痛快些。

===Original===
Larry went away from this casual meeting with his staunch friend Albert with a sense of depression. His nature was unsuspicious and he chose to think that all men were as honest and as frank as he certainly was. Young Heaton's talk had shaken his faith in human nature as far as that was represented in one man—Ben Burton, the open-eyed and bluff Ben Burton. No wonder Larry repelled Al Heaton's notion that Ben "was not altogether square" and should be watched.

Larry was to stop at Armstrong's blacksmith shop, on the north side, on his way home, to have his horse shod. So, as he was leading the animal across the bridge, lost in thought and dwelling somewhat darkly on his conversation with Al Heaton, he did not notice that a young lady, very charmingly dressed and daintily booted and gloved, was tripping along toward him from the opposite side of the river, in the foot-walk that skirted the lower side of the rickety old wooden bridge. He did not look up until his steed, never very easily startled out of a heavy and slouching gait, jumped wildly at a sudden flash from a sky-blue parasol which the young lady deliberately shook at him.

"Whoa, Nance!" cried Larry, astonished at the beast's unprecedented skittishness, "you old fool!" but here he stopped, for his eyes fell on the bewitching apparition on the other side of the timbered rail, and he colored deeply red as he beheld Miss Alice ready to giggle at his confusion.

"Good day, Mr. Boyne," said the girl, "I am glad I have met you. I wanted to ask you how the club is getting along, and if you think you will be in good condition for the coming season. To be sure, papa tells me that he has every confidence in your success; but then, papa is hardly a judge in baseball matters, you know, although he has learned a great deal lately, and so have many other people, and they all seem very confident; but the wish is father to the thought, you know, and so I thought I would like to see someone in whose judgment and candor I could put a great deal of confidence, a very great deal, you know, and see what he thinks about the prospect before us. I say 'us,' you see, because it is a sort of town matter. Now isn't it?"

===Chinese===
这次和挚友阿尔伯特的不期而谈之后，拉里带着一腔郁结走开了。他天性不爱猜疑，宁愿相信人人都和他一样诚实坦率。小希顿的一席话动摇了他对人性的信心——就人性落在某一个人身上而言，这人便是本·伯顿，那个目光坦荡、直来直去的本·伯顿。也难怪拉里不肯接受阿尔·希顿那套说法——说本「不那么正派」，得防着点儿。

拉里回家路上，还要顺道去北边的阿姆斯特朗铁匠铺给马钉掌。于是他牵着马过桥，一路沉思，心里还阴一阵晴一阵地琢磨着与阿尔·希顿的那番谈话，并没留意一位年轻小姐正从河对岸迎面袅袅走来——她衣着俏丽，靴子手套都精致讲究，走在那座摇摇欲坠的老木桥下侧的人行道上。他那匹马平日步子又沉又懒，不大容易受惊，这会儿却被小姐故意朝它抖开的一柄天蓝色阳伞闪了一下，猛地惊跳起来，他这才抬起头来。

「吁——南斯（Nance）！」拉里喊道，这牲口破天荒的神经质让他吃了一惊，「你这老蠢货！」话说到这儿就停住了，因为他的目光落在了木栏杆那头那个摄人心魄的身影上；眼看爱丽丝小姐就要为他这副慌乱劲儿咯咯笑起来，他的脸一下子红透了。

「日安，博因先生，」姑娘说，「遇见您真高兴。我正想问问您，俱乐部进行得怎么样了，您觉得咱们下个赛季能不能进入良好状态。当然啦，爸爸告诉我，他对你们的成功有十足的信心；可是呢，爸爸在棒球的事儿上算不上什么行家，您知道的——虽说近来他学了不少，好些别的人也一样，他们个个好像都信心十足；不过啊，俗话说『愿望是思想之父』，您懂的，所以我想，我还是见一见一位他的判断和坦率都能让我大大放心的人——大大地放心，您知道——听听他对我们眼前前景的看法。我说『我们』，您瞧，因为这多少算是全镇的事。可不是吗？」

===Original===
The young lady had rattled on in a random manner, as if she was giving time for Larry to recover himself. Certainly, he needed time. He was covered with blushes, not altogether becoming, for his natural color was quite deep enough for all artistic considerations. But as he stood there, cap in hand, the river breeze lightly lifting his brown curls and fanning his hot cheeks, the maiden's bright eyes rested on the picture with a certain sense of satisfaction, and she said to her most secret and hidden inner self that there were very few handsomer young men in the region than he who stood before her.

Larry, laying his brown hand on the timber guard that capped the railing betwixt them, said, "You startled me so, Miss Alice, that I almost forgot my manners; and I haven't much. Oh, you wanted to know about the prospects of the Catalpa Nine? Well, I do not think it would be wise to build many hopes on the future until we have met at least one of the best nines of the country about us. Some of our friends think we are going to sweep the deck. Excuse the expression. And some are even talking of our being the champion nine of the state."

"Why," said the girl, "don't you hope for the championship? Is not that what you are going out to get?"

"Of course, Miss Alice, we hope for everything that is in sight, as the saying is; but we cannot expect, with any sort of reason, for so great success as that during our very first season. The matches are now nearly all made up for the coming season, and if we were never so good players, we should have no chance for the championship, I am afraid."

===Chinese===
年轻小姐就这样滔滔不绝、东拉西扯地说了一气，像是在给拉里留出缓过神来的工夫。他确实需要工夫。他满脸通红——这红并不怎么衬他，因为他的本色已经够深，从一切审美的角度考虑都绰绰有余了。可他站在那儿，帽子拿在手里，河风轻轻掀起他棕色的鬈发，拂着他滚烫的脸颊，少女明亮的目光带着几分满足，落在这幅画面上；她在自己最隐秘的内心深处说，这一带比眼前这位更英俊的年轻人，可没有几个。

拉里把一只晒成棕色的手搁在两人之间栏杆顶端的木护板上，说道：「您把我吓了一大跳，爱丽丝小姐，害得我差点儿忘了礼数——本来我也没多少礼数。哦，您想了解卡塔帕九人队的前景？这个嘛，我认为，在咱们跟周遭乡下至少一支最强的九人队交过手之前，就把许多希望寄托在未来，恐怕不算明智。咱们有些朋友觉得，咱们要把对手一扫而光了。恕我用词粗俗。还有几个甚至已经在谈论咱们当全州冠军队的事了。」

「咦，」姑娘说，「你们不指望夺冠吗？你们出去拼杀，不就是为了拿下冠军的吗？」

「当然指望，爱丽丝小姐，俗话说得好，凡是看得见的我们都指望；可是要我们在头一个赛季就取得那么大的成功，怎么想也不合理。下个赛季的比赛如今差不多全都排定了，就算我们打得再好，恐怕也没有争冠的机会了。」

===Original===
"I never thought of that," said Alice. "What an awful lot you know about baseball. But then that is because you are a man. My papa says that girls have no business learning about baseball. Now what do you think, Mr. Boyne?"

"I am not used to being called 'Mr. Boyne' for one thing," replied Larry, gallantly, "and I should feel very much honored indeed if Miss Howell would remember that I am only 'Larry' the new third baseman of the Catalpa Nine."

The heavy rumble of a farm wagon driving up on the town end of the bridge at that moment warned Larry that he must get out of the way. So, with a few concise words as to the all-absorbing topic of the day, he bowed, replaced his cap, and passed on to North Catalpa.

Sal Monnahan drove the sorrel horses that now came pounding along the wooden way. When she reached her home in Oneosho Village, that evening, she informed her nearest neighbor that she had seen "Larry Boyne lollygagging with that high-strung darter of Judge Howell's, on the North Catalpa bridge, that arternoon, and then when the gal came off she looked as if she had been talking with her sweetheart, her eyes were so shiny, just like dimonds, and her cheeks were as red as a poppy in the corn. It do beat all how that young Irish feller gets on with folks in town. Gals and fellers—all the same."

===Chinese===
「这个我可从没想过，」爱丽丝说，「你对棒球懂得真多呀。不过那是因为你是个男人。我爸爸说，女孩子学棒球没什么用处。您说呢，博因先生？」

「首先，我不习惯被人称作『博因先生』，」拉里殷勤地答道，「要是豪厄尔小姐肯记住，我只是『拉里』——卡塔帕九人队的新任三垒手，那我可真要深感荣幸了。」

正在这时，一辆农用大车隆隆驶上桥的镇区一端，提醒拉里该让路了。于是，就当天这个最叫人着迷的话题又简短说了几句之后，他鞠了一躬，戴好帽子，往北卡塔帕去了。

赶着那两匹栗色马沿木桥咚咚驶来的，正是萨尔·莫纳汉（Sal Monnahan）。当晚她回到奥尼奥绍村的家里，便告诉紧邻的邻居说，她瞧见「拉里·博因那天下午在北卡塔帕桥上，跟豪厄尔法官家那个娇气的丫头黏糊调笑；那丫头下桥的时候，那模样就跟刚跟情郎哥说过话似的，眼睛贼亮，跟钻石似的，脸蛋红得像玉米地里的罂粟花。那个爱尔兰小年轻在镇上的人缘，好得真是邪了门了。闺女小子——一概都对他好。」

===Original===
As for Larry, he went across the bridge, leading his nag, and walking so lightly that it seemed to him that his steps were in the air. While Armstrong was shoeing the horse and chatting the while with Larry, he thought within himself that this was a particularly fine young fellow, and that it was a pity that he was poor. Presently his thoughts took shape and he said:

"Don't you think you are too smart a chap, Larry, to waste your time playing baseball?"

"I am not going to waste much time playing, Tom. I know enough about baseball to know that a player doesn't last as a good player more than ten or twelve years. He is too young to play before he is seventeen years old, and he is done for and is dropped out by the time he is thirty. So if I had any notion of making ball-playing my calling in life, I should have that fact in view to warn me. Oh, no Tom, I am only making this a bridge to carry me over a hard place."

===Chinese===
至于拉里，他牵着那匹驽马走过桥去，脚步轻快得仿佛踩在半空中。阿姆斯特朗一边给马钉掌，一边和拉里闲聊，心里暗想：这真是个格外出色的年轻人，只可惜穷了点儿。过了一会儿，他的念头成了形，便开口说：

「拉里，你不觉得像你这么机灵的小伙子，把时间浪费在打棒球上太可惜了吗？」

「我不会在打球上浪费太多工夫的，汤姆。我对棒球懂得够多，知道一个球员当红的日子超不过十年十二年。十七岁以前年纪太小，打不了；一到三十，就完蛋了，就被淘汰了。所以就算我动过把打棒球当终身职业的念头，也得把这条记在心里警醒自己。哦，不会的，汤姆，我只是把它当作一座桥，好捱过眼前这段难走的路。」

===Original===
"That's good sense. I was afraid you were going off with the baseball fever, and so never be fit for anything else. That's what will become of some of those young kids over in town who don't think of anything, from morning till night, but baseball. I always thought you had more sense into you than most of the boys around here. You are older than your years, Larry," and the plain-speaking blacksmith looked admiringly in the young man's face, "older than your years."

"Older than your years." These words rang in Larry's ears as he swung himself lightly into his saddle and ambled down the river road to Sugar Grove.

The blacksmith looked after him and muttered to himself, "He is smart enough to be anything in the way of a lawyer that there is in these parts. And if he were to cast sheep's eyes on the Judge's daughter, or on anybody else's daughter, for that matter, I just believe he would win her in time. He's got such a taking way with him." And honest Thomas Armstrong resumed his work with a mild glow of pleasure stealing through him as he thought of Larry Boyne and his possibilities.

===Chinese===
「这话在理。我还怕你染上棒球热，从此干别的什么都不成了呢。镇上有些半大孩子就会落得那个下场——从早到晚，除了棒球什么都不想。我一向觉得，你比这一带大多数小伙子都有主意。你比你的岁数老成，拉里，」心直口快的铁匠赞赏地望着年轻人的脸，「比你的岁数老成。」

「比你的岁数老成。」拉里轻身翻上马鞍，沿着河边的路缓辔向舒格格罗夫而去，这句话一路在他耳边回响。

铁匠目送他远去，喃喃自语：「论当律师，这一带有什么样的律师，他就当得了什么样的律师，他够聪明。要是他对法官家的闺女眉目传起情来——对谁家的闺女都一样——我压根儿相信，早晚有一天他能把她娶到手。他就有那么一股招人喜欢的劲儿。」诚实的托马斯·阿姆斯特朗（Thomas Armstrong）重新干起活来，一想到拉里·博因和他的前程，一阵淡淡的喜悦悄悄涌上心头。

## I Great Expectations / I 远大前程

===Original===
Alice Howell was flattening her pretty nose against the window pane as she looked ruefully out into the misty atmosphere that surrounded her father’s house in North Catalpa. It was eight o’clock in the morning, and the great baseball match was set for two o’clock, that afternoon. As soon as she had risen, Alice had run to the window to see what were the signs of the sky, for Alice was an ardent lover of the American game, and her heart was set on the great match that was to come off on the Agricultural Grounds, near Catalpa, that day. The sky was dull and lowering, and there was little chance that the game would be called.

“Your father, the Judge, says you should come to breakfast right away, miss,” said the little handmaid of the house.

Alice turned from the window with an impatient sigh, saying “Oh dear, Jessie, do you suppose the Jonesville Nine will come up to play the Catalpas, this afternoon?”

“ ’Deed I don’t know, miss. I hope so, for Miss Anstress has promised me that I shall go over to see the game if it is played, and goodness only knows when I shall get off again to see a baseball match if I don’t go today.”

===Chinese===
爱丽丝·豪厄尔把她那俊俏的鼻子贴在窗玻璃上，愁眉苦脸地望着北卡塔帕她父亲家周围雾蒙蒙的天色。此时是早晨八点，那场盛大的棒球赛定在当天下午两点。爱丽丝一起床就跑到窗前看天象，因为她是这项美国运动的狂热爱好者，一心惦记着那天要在卡塔帕近旁农业场地举行的大赛。天色阴沉压抑，比赛能开场的希望微乎其微。

「小姐，您父亲法官老爷说，请您马上过来用早餐。」家里的小女仆说。

爱丽丝不耐烦地叹了口气，从窗前转过身来，说：「哎呀，杰茜（Jessie），你说琼斯维尔九人队今天下午会来跟卡塔帕队打吗？」

「俺可不知道，小姐。俺盼着来，因为安斯特丽丝小姐答应过俺，要是比赛开得成，就让俺过去看。天知道，今儿要不去，俺下回再有机会看棒球比赛得等到什么时候。」

===Original===
“But look at the weather! It’s as dark as a pocket, and it looks as if it might rain at any moment. Oh dear! oh dear! it’s too bad, so it is. And this is to be the last game of the season, and the decisive one, too.” And so, more talking to herself than to the small servant who trotted behind her, with a sympathetic air, the pretty Miss Alice went to the breakfast-table where her father waited for her with an aspect of amused dignity.

“One cannot see across the river for the fog, papa,” said the girl, with a disconsolate tone, as she seated herself. “The fences are dripping with moisture, and the dam roars just as it always does when there is a rainstorm coming up. How very provoking!”

“Well, and has my little girl forgotten that it was the day before yesterday that Farmer Boggs was in here from Sugar Grove and said that unless they had more rain before the frosts set in, it would be a hard year for winter wheat? And wasn’t it my little girl who said that she wanted Stone River running full, this fall, in order that she might enjoy her new club skates when the ice came?”

“But, papa, the crops can wait a day or two for the fall rains, I am sure, and I should be willing to give up a whole winter’s skating if the Catalpas would only beat the Jonesville Nine﻿—the horrid fellows! And I am sure they would beat them, if they only played them today, for they are in capital form now.”

===Chinese===
「可你瞧这天气！黑得像口锅底，随时都可能下起雨来。哎呀！哎呀！真是太糟了，真是的。这可是本赛季最后一场比赛，还是定胜负的一战呢。」漂亮的爱丽丝小姐就这样自言自语——倒不是说给那个满脸同情、小步跟在她身后的小女仆听——一路走到早餐桌前，她父亲正带着几分觉得有趣的庄重神气等她。

「雾大得连河对岸都望不见，爸爸。」姑娘落座时郁郁地说。「栅栏上凝着水珠直往下滴，拦河坝轰轰作响，每逢暴雨将至它都是这样。真叫人生气！」

「唔，我的小姑娘忘了没有，前天博格斯农夫（Farmer Boggs）从舒格格罗夫来这儿，说过霜降之前要是没有更多雨水，今年的冬小麦就难过了？再说，想要斯通河今年秋天涨满水、好等上冻时穿她那双新冰鞋痛痛快快滑一场的，不正是我的小姑娘吗？」

「可是，爸爸，庄稼等秋雨，晚一两天总是等得起的，我敢肯定；只要卡塔帕队能打赢琼斯维尔九人队——那帮可恶的家伙！——哪怕让我放弃整整一冬的滑冰我也心甘情愿。而且我敢说，只要今天就同他们打，卡塔帕队准能赢，因为他们现在的状态好极了。」

===Original===
“Hush! hush! my daughter,” said Judge Howell, with a little shudder, “that is slang that you are using, and I shall have to curtail your baseball amusement if you are so ready to pick up the jargon of what they call, I believe, ‘The Diamond Field,’ for I do not want my daughter to mingle the slang of the game with her mother’s mode of speech.”

The Judge was somewhat prosy and not at all in love with the noble game which his daughter, in common with all of the girls of Catalpa, and of the whole Stone River country, for that matter, followed with so much enthusiasm.

===Chinese===
「嘘！嘘！我的女儿。」豪厄尔法官微微打了个寒噤说。「你这是在用俚语了。你要是这么轻易就学会了那个被他们——我记得——称作『内场』的行当里的行话，我就不得不削减你看棒球的乐趣了，我可不想让我的女儿把球场上的俚语，掺进她母亲那一路的谈吐里。」

这位法官有点迂阔乏味，对这项高尚的运动全然不来电；而他的女儿，同卡塔帕乃至整个斯通河地区的所有姑娘一样，追起这项运动来可是热情如火。

===Original===
The baseball club of Catalpa was made up of some of the finest young fellows in the town. Catalpa was situated on both sides of Stone River, in northern Illinois. It was a busy manufacturing and milling community, and from its homes had gone many a stalwart young chap to fight his country’s battles in the southwest. The survivors of the company that went out and came back, decimated as to numbers and not all sound in body, founded the first baseball club of the region. The members of the club called themselves “The Catalpas,” after their town. Most of the players lived on the north side of the river, and were soon dubbed “The North Catalpas” by their rivals who, living on the other side of the stream, and in the main portion of the town, and forming another club, arrogated to themselves the title of “Catalpa’s Champions.”

Gradually, the membership of the two organizations changed. The old soldiers retired in favor of their sons and nephews. The club on the south side of the river was reorganized and an entirely new set of young men came into it. The name of “The Dean County Nine,” was given to the southside club, and, as it was largely composed of young men who worked in the flouring mills and the lumberyards along the river front, it was famous for the brawn and muscle of its players.

The Catalpa Nine, on the other hand, was made up of students in the Seminary, young fellows in the law and county offices of the town, and sons of gentlemen of leisure. There was a chasm as wide as Stone River fixed between the Dean County Nine and the Catalpa Nine, so far as social relations were concerned. The Dean County players called the Catalpas “Aristocrats” and the Catalpas retorted with the epithet of “Stalwarts” applied to their town rivals. When it is added that the finest residences were built on the north side of the river dividing the town, and that the men of more moderate means dwelt on the business side of the stream, the reason for the imaginary line of separation betwixt the two ball clubs will be more apparent.

===Chinese===
卡塔帕的棒球俱乐部由镇上一些最出色的年轻人组成。卡塔帕坐落在伊利诺伊北部、斯通河的两岸。这是一个以制造业和磨坊业为主的繁忙市镇，从这里的家家户户，曾走出许多结实的小伙子，去西南边疆为国征战。当年出征又归来的人们中，那些虽然折损大半、身体也不尽完好的幸存者，创立了本地区第一个棒球俱乐部。俱乐部成员以自己镇子的名字，自称「卡塔帕队」。大多数球员住在河的北岸，很快就被对手们戏称为「北卡塔帕队」；那些对手住在河对岸、镇子的主体部分，另立了一支俱乐部，自封为「卡塔帕冠军队」。

渐渐地，两个组织的成员都换了血。老兵们退了下来，把位置让给儿子和侄子。河南岸的俱乐部经过改组，进来了全新的一批年轻人，得了「迪安县九人队」的名字；由于队员大多是河滨面粉厂和木材场上干活的年轻人，它以球员的膂力和筋骨闻名。

卡塔帕九人队则由学院（Seminary）的学生、镇上法律界和县署机关里的年轻人，以及有闲绅士人家的子弟组成。论社交往来，迪安县九人队同卡塔帕九人队之间隔着一道同斯通河一样宽的鸿沟。迪安县球员称卡塔帕队为「贵族队」，卡塔帕队则回敬镇上对手一个「壮汉队」的诨名。再补上一句——分剖全镇的那条河，北岸盖着最气派的宅邸，家道次一些的人则住在商肆所在的南岸——两家球会之间那条无形的分界线缘何而来，就更清楚了。

===Original===
After repeated and not always friendly matches between the rival clubs, they were drawn together by the appearance of a common enemy. From the little town of Jonesville, situated eighteen miles down the river, came the Jonesvillians, as they called themselves, a powerful and well-trained nine. They had challenged and vanquished the nine of Dry Plains, the Blue Falls Nine, and their own Home Club, commonly known through the Stone River region as “The Jonesville Scrubs.” Flushed with victory, the Jonesvillians had challenged and played two games with the Catalpas, contesting the championship of northern Illinois. It must be admitted that the record of neither of the two Catalpa clubs was one of which the people of the town had any right to be proud. Both clubs, while closely contesting with each other, had been repeatedly beaten by visitors from the surrounding region. Naturally the sympathies of the “Stalwarts” was with the “Aristocrats” when an out-of-town club came to try conclusions. Every true son and daughter of the town of Catalpa was hotly enlisted for the home nine in any contest that might be fought out for the championship. It was aggravating that the Jonesville Nine, most of whom were rough and loud-talking fellows, should conquer the whole country, from the Wisconsin line to Lasalle, and from Chicago to the Mississippi River.

===Chinese===
两支对头球会你来我往交手多次，彼此也并非总是客客气气，后来一个共同敌人的出现，把它们拉到了一处。从河下游十八英里的小镇琼斯维尔，来了自称「琼斯维尔佬」的一支人马——一支实力强大、训练有素的九人队。他们先向德赖普莱恩斯（Dry Plains）的球队、蓝瀑九人队（Blue Falls Nine）以及他们自己的本乡俱乐部下了战书并一一击败——那支本乡俱乐部在斯通河一带通称「琼斯维尔杂牌军」（The Jonesville Scrubs）。凯歌高奏的琼斯维尔佬又向卡塔帕队挑战，打了两场，争夺伊利诺伊北部的冠军。必须承认，卡塔帕这两家球会的战绩，都谈不上让镇民有什么可夸口的。两家球会互相咬得紧，却都屡屡败给周边地区来访的球队。外乡球队前来一决高下时，「壮汉队」的人心自然向着「贵族队」。凡是为冠军衔可能开打的比赛，卡塔帕镇每一个真正的儿女，无不踊跃站在主队一边。令人憋气的是，琼斯维尔九人队——其中大多是大嗓门的粗鲁家伙——竟横扫了从威斯康星州界到拉萨尔（Lasalle）、从芝加哥到密西西比河的整个地界。

===Original===
That was the reason why Miss Alice Howell, the only daughter and the spoiled child of the eminent and widowed district Judge, should be downcast and fidgety when she looked out and saw, on this fateful morning, that the weather gave signs of being unfit for the decisive game for the championship. The Jonesville Nine had won the first game. The Catalpas were victors in the second game. Today, if all went well, would give the championship to the Catalpas. The Catalpas had regularly whitewashed the Dean County Nine, in spite of their stalwart strength. But they had failed to hold their own against many another club from other portions of the country roundabout. In the first game for the championship, the Catalpas had beaten the Jonesvillians by a score of 24 to 13﻿—an overwhelming defeat for the downriver club. But the Jonesville men had carried off the second game with a score of 14 to 13, which was a close game, and was lost by the Catalpas, as their friends all said, by the Catalpas being in bad condition. Albert Heaton, the catcher, was afflicted with blistered hands and could do very little effective work behind the bat; and George Buckner, center fielder, had been obliged to leave the field just before game was called, on account of a sudden sickness in his own home; and this necessitated sundry changes that demoralized the Nine, and disarranged their plans.

===Chinese===
正因如此，在这个要紧的早晨，爱丽丝·豪厄尔小姐——这位丧偶的名门地区法官的独女、掌上明珠——望着窗外，见天气分明不宜举行这场定冠军归属的大赛，才会垂头丧气、坐立不安。第一场琼斯维尔九人队赢了。第二场卡塔帕队取胜。今天，倘若一切顺利，冠军就将归卡塔帕队所有。卡塔帕队虽面对迪安县九人队壮汉般的膂力，却总能把对方剃成光头；可对四乡八里来的其他球会，他们就没法稳占上风。冠军争夺的第一场，卡塔帕队以 24 比 13 击败琼斯维尔佬——对这支下游球队是一场惨败。但第二场琼斯维尔人以 14 比 13 险胜，那是一场咬得极紧的比赛；正如卡塔帕队的朋友们众口一词所说，他们输在状态不佳。捕手阿尔伯特·希顿双手磨出了水泡，在本垒板后面几乎派不上用场；中外野手乔治·巴克纳（George Buckner）又在临开赛前因家中突染急病不得不离场；这就免不了种种临时调动，把全队搞得方寸大乱，部署尽失。

===Original===
“And after all,” said Alice, exultantly, as she recounted these facts to her father, on the morning of the fateful day, “after all, the Jonesvillians only beat by one run. Today, the Catalpas are in splendid form﻿—condition, I mean, and if it only would clear off, I am sure they will send the Jonesville fellows down the river with what Ben Burton calls ‘a basket of goose eggs,’﻿—I beg pardon, papa, for this bit of slang; but you will observe that it is a quotation.”

“Yes, from a favorite author,” said the Judge, rising from the breakfast table, with a shrewd smile.

Alice flushed, a little angrily, perhaps, for she did not like Burton, although he was her cousin and was said to be a suitor for her favor.

===Chinese===
「说到底，」在那个关系重大的早晨，爱丽丝向父亲细数这些旧账时喜形于色地说，「说到底，琼斯维尔佬也只赢了一分。今天，卡塔帕队状态好极了——我是说『状态』——只要天能放晴，我敢保证，他们准会用本·伯顿说的那句『一篮子鸭蛋』，把琼斯维尔那帮家伙打发回河下游去——请您原谅，爸爸，这是一句俚语；不过您得承认，这可是引用来的。」

「是啊，还是从一位心爱的作家笔下引用来的呢。」法官从早餐桌旁站起身，露出一个会心的微笑。

爱丽丝脸红了，也许带一点恼意，因为她并不喜欢伯顿，尽管他是她的表哥，而且据说正在向她求好。

## IX Hope and Suspense / 九 希望与悬念

===Original===
It was the custom in Catalpa for the storekeepers to hang out at their doors a little blue flag when they wanted the services of an errand boy. Seeing this signal at the door of Jason Elderkin's dry-goods store, Rough and Ready, wearing in the heats of summer as in winter his coonskin cap, shambled in and asked what was wanted. Jason lifted his spectacles from his nose and said, jocularly: "Why, Rough and Ready, I thought you had gone up to Galena to see the match between the boys and the Galena Club."

"No sir-ee," replied the old man, "I have stayed at home to keep the town in order. Me and Jedge Howell, we have to look after the boys at home, you know, or some of these frisky young colts like Jase Ayres would get away with the town whilst we were gone." And the old man chuckled as he added, "Cap. Heaton, he and his boy Al have gone together, and they do say that Mrs. Heaton is just wild because she can't keep the old man at home when baseball is going on. Well, it does beat all natur', don't it? Here's Al kept out of the Nine because it isn't high-toned enough for Mrs. Heaton; and here's father and son gone a-galivanting up to Galena to see the show."

"I hear that Al has sent a despatch to the Judge's daughter saying that the Catalpas are going to carry off the honors this time, and no mistake," said the storekeeper. "How's that, Rough?"

"Seein' as how this bundle is going over to Boardman's, I'll jest drop in at the Jedge's house on my way back, and see if Miss Ally has got any news from the seat of war, as it were, and if she has, she'll be sure to tell me. Oh, she's clear grit, too, is that gal, and she knows that I set a heap by Larry. Larry! why, it was him what give my boy all the points he has got in the game, and you may lay your bottom dollar that that boy is goin' to be the all-firedest batter in the Stone River country; and you put that down to remember."

===Chinese===
卡塔帕有个风俗：店家要雇跑腿的小伙计时，就在门口挂出一面小蓝旗。绰号「老硬汉」（Rough and Ready）的老汉看见贾森·埃尔德金（Jason Elderkin）绸布店门口挂出了这个信号，便拖着步子踱了进去，问有什么活计。杰森把眼镜从鼻梁上抬起，打趣道：「哟，老硬汉，我还当你上加利纳（Galena）看咱们小伙子跟加利纳俱乐部的比赛去了呢。」

「才不呢，先生，」老头儿答道，「我留在家里给镇子维持治安。我跟豪厄尔法官，总得有人看住家里这帮小伙子，您知道的，不然咱们一走，贾斯·艾尔斯（Jase Ayres）那样欢蹦乱跳的小马驹准能把镇子闹个底朝天。」老头儿又嘿嘿一笑，接着说：「希顿上尉爷儿俩一道去了，都说希顿太太气得没法儿——棒球赛季一到，她就没法儿让老头子待在家里。嘿，这不是天下奇事么？阿尔呢，因为希顿太太嫌打球不够体面，被拦在九人队外头；这爷儿俩倒好，双双逛到加利纳看热闹去了。」

「我听说阿尔给法官家小姐拍了电报，说这回卡塔帕队要把荣誉捧回家，错不了，」店主人说，「有这么回事吗，老硬汉？」

「这捆东西横竖要送到博德曼家去，回头我就顺道拐到法官府上，瞧瞧爱丽丝小姐有没有从前线——兴许可以这么说——打来的消息；要是有了，她准会告诉我。哦，那丫头可是个有骨气的，她也知道我器重拉里。拉里！嗐，我家小子那一身球上的门道，全是人家教的；你尽管把兜里最后一枚铜板押上，那小子准能成为斯通河一带顶顶厉害的击球手；这话你记下来。」

===Original===
The garrulous old man shouldered his bundle as he spoke and plodded down Bridge Street and so across to the north side of the town. It was the day for the first game of the championship at Galena. The hot sun poured down into the Stone River Valley with great power, and the bleached surface of the old wooden bridge shimmered with undulating lines of heat as Rough and Ready toiled on his way. The roar of the dam had a cooling sound, and the group of cottonwoods and willows on the little island above were green and refreshing to the eye. But no breeze drew up the river, and all of the north side was steeped in liquid sunshine, the trees standing motionless and the yellow road glaring in the blinding light. The toll-keeper's dog panted in the shade of the tollhouse, lolling his tongue as old Rough and Ready passed by, without stopping for a word of gossip with the keeper who dozed within the doorway.

The old man paused, when halfway across the bridge, to lift his furry cap from his head and wipe the servile drops from off his burning brow. While he rested his bundle on the guard rail of the bridge, Miss Anstress Howell, the Judge's aged sister, came mincing along from the North Catalpa side, cool and fresh as if she had never before been outside of a bandbox.

"I wonder ef it will be safe to tackle her for news from Galena?" muttered the old man to himself. "She's a dangerous team to fool with. Mebbe she'll get away with me, but I'll try it."

"Good arternoon, Miss Howell. Fine hot day. Good growin' weather, as the farmers say. Hev you heerd that any of your folks got a despatch from Galena givin' any account of how the ball opens?"

===Chinese===
这张嘴闲不住的老头儿一边说，一边把包袱扛上肩，蹭蹬着走过桥街，再穿过镇子到北边去。这天是加利纳冠军赛第一场比赛的日子。烈日火辣辣地倾泻在斯通河谷里，老木桥晒得发白的桥面蒸腾着一道道晃动的热浪，老硬汉吃力地走着路。水坝的轰鸣听上去反倒凉快，上游小洲上那丛三角叶杨和柳树绿得养眼。可是河面上没有一丝风，整个河北岸都泡在明晃晃的日头里，树影纹丝不动，黄土路在晃眼的强光里泛着白。收费亭的阴影里，看桥人的狗吐着舌头直喘，老硬汉打旁边走过，也没停下跟门洞里打盹的看桥人搭话。

走到桥中央，老头儿停下来，把毛皮帽从头上摘下，抹了抹滚烫脑门上的汗珠。他把包袱搁在桥栏杆上歇肩的当口，法官的老姐姐安斯特蕾丝·豪厄尔小姐（Miss Anstress Howell）从北卡塔帕那头碎步走了过来，清清爽爽，干净得仿佛从没出过帽盒似的。

「找她打听加利纳的消息，保险不保险呢？」老头儿暗自嘀咕。「跟她搭伙计可危险着呢。没准儿她会把我涮了，可我还是要试一试。」

「下午好啊，豪厄尔小姐。天真热。庄稼人管这叫长庄稼的天气。您听说府上哪位收到加利纳打来的电报，讲球赛开场打成什么样了吗？」

===Original===
Miss Howell's manner stiffened a little as she said, with a slight toss of her head, "Judge Howell, my brother, is holding court in Pawpaw, today, for Judge Sniffles, and nobody else but the Judge would be likely to have any despatches concerning baseball."

"Well, Miss Howell, I heerd over in town that Miss Ally had a message of some kind, no offence to you, marm, and I want to hear from the boys powerful bad, you see, and so I make bold to ask if Miss Alice mayn't hev a despatch, or something from Larry, I mean Al."

"There is altogether too much nonsense about this baseball business in Catalpa, Mr. Rough—excuse me, I forget your other name. It does seem to me as if the people had gone crazy, and the weather so hot too! Excuse me, I don't know anything about what is going on in Galena, no more than a child, I may say, and if any grown people want to begin over again and make children of themselves with playing ball, they have my sympathy."

So saying, and flirting off an imaginary fleck of dust from her gown with a spotless handkerchief, Miss Howell resumed her deliberate walk across the bridge. Rough and Ready replaced his cap, and looking after her said, "Sarves me right! I might hev knowed that I should get the worst on it in a talk with her. My grief! But she is a teaser. Has forgot all about the time when she was a young gal, it's so long ago. P'raps she never was young." With this, the old man shouldered his bundle and slowly made his way northward.

===Chinese===
豪厄尔小姐的神气板了起来，微微扬了扬下巴，说：「家兄豪厄尔法官今天正在帕帕替斯尼弗尔斯法官开庭，除了法官本人，家里别的人哪会收到什么谈棒球的电报。」

「这个嘛，豪厄尔小姐，我听镇上人说，爱丽丝小姐收到了个什么口信——太太您可千万别多心——您瞧，我实在太想听到小伙子们的消息了，所以冒昧问一声，爱丽丝小姐兴许收到了一份电报，或是拉里——呃，我是说阿尔——捎来的什么东西。」

「卡塔帕这桩棒球营生里头的胡闹实在太多了，老硬汉先生——恕我失礼，我忘了您另外的尊姓大名。依我看，这些人简直都疯了，天还这么热！恕我直言，加利纳那边眼下怎么样，我跟个小娃娃一样一无所知；要是有哪位大人想从头再来、玩球把自己变回小孩子，那我只有同情的份儿。」

说着，她用一方一尘不染的手帕，掸了掸裙子上并不存在的尘星，迈着四平八稳的步子继续过桥去了。老硬汉把帽子扣回头上，望着她的背影说：「我活该！我早该知道，跟她搭话，吃亏的准是我。我的老天！她可真是个难缠的主儿。自己做姑娘那阵子的事全忘光啦——年头隔得太久喽。兴许她压根儿就没年轻过。」说完，老头儿扛起包袱，慢吞吞地往北去了。

===Original===
But Alice had received a telegram from Galena, and as Rough and Ready climbed the slope by the Judge's house, a sunny head was popped from one of its upper windows and Alice's cheerful voice cried, "Oh, Roughy—excuse me for calling you Roughy, but I'm so glad!—Albert Heaton has telegraphed to me that the Catalpas have made ten runs in the first three innings and the Galenas only one! Isn't that perfectly splendid? Does anybody over in town know anything about it?"

"Bless your bright eyes! Miss Ally, no; the whole town's asleep. It's a hot day, you know, and there's nobody stirring. All the farmers are busy with their crops, and the streets are as lonesome as a last year's bird's nest. Ten to one, did you say? By the great horn spoon! I must go back and wake up the folks."

Suiting the action to the word, the old man tossed Mrs. Boardman's bundle of sheeting over the fence and made his way back to town as fast as his rheumatic legs would carry him. Halfway across, he met Lewis Morris who was on his way over to verify the rumor that he had caught concerning the early success of the Catalpas in Galena.

"Hooray for our side!" cried Rough and Ready, exultingly. "I have heard it from the gentle Miss Ally. Our boys have made ten runs in the first three innings, and the Galena fellows have made one—one whole one."

===Chinese===
可爱丽丝偏偏收到了加利纳的电报。老硬汉爬上法官家旁边的坡道时，一颗金灿灿的脑袋从楼上的一扇窗户里探了出来，爱丽丝欢快的声音喊道：「哦，老硬汉儿——原谅我这么叫你，可我太高兴了！——阿尔伯特·希顿给我拍来电报说，卡塔帕队头三局拿了十分，加利纳队才一分！这不是棒极了吗？镇上有人知道这事儿吗？」

「保佑你那双亮眼睛！爱丽丝小姐，没有；全镇都睡着呢。天热，您知道的，一个人影儿都不动弹。庄稼人都忙着侍弄庄稼，街上冷清得像只去年的鸟窝。十分对一分，你是这么说的？我的天老爷！我得回去把大伙儿叫醒。」

说到做到，老头儿把博德曼太太那捆床单布隔着栅栏扔了过去，拖着两条风湿腿，能走多快走多快地折回镇上。走到半桥，他迎面碰上刘易斯·莫里斯，这年轻人正要过河去，核实他听到的那个传言——卡塔帕队在加利纳开局得胜的消息。

「咱们这边万岁！」老硬汉得意洋洋地喊。「我可是从温柔的爱丽丝小姐亲耳听来的。咱们的小伙子头三局拿了十分，加利纳那帮家伙得了一分——完完整整的一分。」

===Original===
"Then I'll turn right around and tell the news in town!" said Lewis, with excitement. "I'll have to stir the people up, for the whole town has gone to sleep, except Dr. Selby, and he was sweating at every pore, as I came by the drug store, for thinking of another defeat for the Catalpas."

Rough and Ready gazed after the rapidly retreating form of the young man who turned and stepped swiftly across the bridge. Then, putting his hand to his coonskin cap, as if trying to recall something to his mind, he murmured, "If I didn't go and leave that ther bundle of sheetin' in the Judge's dooryard! 'Pears to me as if that pesky baseball had knocked my wits clean out." And, smiling at his own feeble joke, he retraced his steps to the North Catalpa side of the river.

When Lewis Morris reached the center of the town, he saw a knot of men and boys gathered around the bulletin board of *The Leaf*. "Just my luck," he muttered. "Downey has got the news out, and they have taken the edge of it off before I could get back."

But Lewis forgot his little disappointment when he eagerly scanned the bulletin which the editor had posted during his brief run across the bridge. This was what he read:

===Chinese===
「那我掉头就回去，把消息告诉镇上！」刘易斯兴奋地说。「我得把大伙儿搅和起来，全镇都睡熟了，就塞尔比医生醒着——我打药店门口过的时候，他正想着卡塔帕队怕是要再吃一场败仗，急得浑身毛孔冒汗。」

老硬汉望着年轻人转身飞快过桥、迅速远去的背影。随后，他把手按在浣熊皮帽上，像是要把什么事从脑子里捞回来，喃喃道：「瞧我这记性，竟把那捆床单布落在法官家的院子里了！依我看，那该死的棒球把我的脑瓜子都打飞喽。」他为自己这句不咸不淡的俏皮话笑了笑，折身又回河北岸的北卡塔帕去了。

刘易斯·莫里斯赶到镇中心时，只见一群男女孩子围在《叶报》（The Leaf）的公告板前。「就我这运气，」他咕哝道，「唐尼把消息捅出去了，没等我赶回来，这消息的新鲜劲儿就让他们先嚼没了。」

可是刘易斯一看清那位编辑趁他过桥的短短工夫贴出来的公告，就把这点小小的懊恼忘到了脑后。他读到的是这样一则：

===Original===
> An overwhelming victory for our nine! In the contest today, the Catalpas were the victors by a score of 13 to 3. Great enthusiasm prevails and the visiting nine are now being cheered by the excited populace. The result has astonished everybody, none more so than the defeated nine and their immediate friends. Our esteemed fellow townsman, Mr. Albert Heaton, Senior, has telegraphed to *The Leaf* the score by innings, as follows:
> 5
> 4
> 1
> 0
> 0

> 13.

> 3.
> *Errors*, **Galenas, 13; Catalpas, 1**.

===Chinese===
> 咱们的九人队大获全胜！今日之战，卡塔帕队以 13 比 3 取胜。全场群情振奋，兴奋的民众此刻正在为远道而来的客队欢呼。这一结果令所有人惊讶，最惊讶的莫过于战败的九人队和他们的至交好友。本镇受人敬重的市民老阿尔伯特·希顿先生已将被单局比分拍电报告知《叶报》，如下：
> 5
