===Original===
"Here's Lew Morris!" cried brawny Hank Jackson, "Glory enough for one day! hey, Lew? Everybody in Galena was astonished, they say, and so was everybody in Catalpa, for that matter. Why, I was just coming along the street with Andy Brubaker, and we was a-talking about the chances of our nine's giving up the season if they got cleaned out in Galena, when I heard Mr. Downey tell Dr. Selby that the home nine had beat the Galenas on the first six innings, and says I to him, 'If that's so, Mr. Downey, why don't you put it on the bulletin?' Sure enough, he went up them stairs, five at a time, to have it done, and no sooner had he got up there than he put his head outen the winder and screeched, 'The Catalpas have won the game by thirteen to three!' Gosh! you should have heerd the whoop that the boys gave! And there it is, as big as life." And Hank regarded the bulletin board with an affectionate interest.

The fact was that the community of Catalpa was unprepared for any such victory as that which had dropped in upon them, as it were, like a bolt out of a clear sky. The defeat at Bluford had unnerved all but a few faithful and undaunted spirits, and the usual dull current of town life had resumed its sluggishness until the unexpected news from the north had startled the townsfolk into new alertness. It was a great achievement, as the Galenas were famed for their prowess in the Diamond Field. They were reckoned as first in the number of batters in their nine. One of them, Devoy, stood very near the head of the list of champion batters in the state, and another, Shallcross, was not far behind him in his general average. Yet the Catalpas had "got away with" the famous players. It was marvellous how the news flew through the town and out upon the prairie, so that by the time the moon rose, red and full, over the bluffy banks above Catalpa, in innumerable cabins and farmhouses, far out on the distant wheat-farms, and over many an evening meal, the details of the triumph and its probable effect on the fortunes of "our nine" were discussed with a glow of pride, or with a lively curiosity.

The boys," in Galena, resting from their labors, and withdrawn from the admiring attention of the citizens of the town, lounged in a big bedroom in the Quapaw House, and told, over and over again, the stirring incidents of the day—incidents on which so much depended that they now became almost like ancient history in importance. They were not too tired to play another game right then, so exhilarated were they by their unwonted success. There was no murmuring, no jealousy, and no nagging in the party now. Every man was elated and flushed with a sense of his own value as a factor in the game that had been played, as well as in that which was to be played on the morrow.

===Chinese===
「刘易斯·莫里斯来啦！」膀大腰圆的汉克·杰克逊喊道，「这一天的荣耀够受用的！ eh，刘易斯？据说加利纳人人惊掉了下巴，卡塔帕这边又何尝不是。咳，方才我正跟安迪·布鲁贝克在街上走，我们俩正念叨着，咱们九人队要是这回在加利纳被打得落花流水，本赛季会不会干脆撂挑子不打了，就听见唐尼先生跟塞尔比医生说，咱们主队头六局就压过了加利纳队。我就跟他说：『要真是这样，唐尼先生，您干嘛不把它贴上公告板？』可不是嘛，他一步跨五级台阶噔噔噔上了楼去办这事，脚跟还没站稳，就把脑袋探出窗户，扯着嗓子喊：『卡塔帕队十三比三赢了这场比赛！』乖乖！你真该听听小伙子们那一嗓子欢呼！喏，这不，板上写得明明白白、原原本本。」汉克满心喜爱地端详着那块公告板。

实情是，这样一场胜利从天而降，好似晴空里掉下个炸雷，卡塔帕全镇上下全无防备。布卢福德那一败，早已把大伙儿的锐气泄了个干净，只剩下寥寥几个忠心不改、百折不回的硬骨头；镇上日子又照旧恢复了那种慢吞吞的死水微澜，直到北方传来的这份意外消息，才把镇民惊得重新来了精神。这可是了不起的大捷，因为加利纳队的球技在内场上素负盛名。论九人队中击球手的人数，他们被推为头一份。队里的德沃伊，在全州冠军击球手的榜单上名列前茅；另一位的沙尔克罗斯，总平均分也紧随其后，相去不远。然而卡塔帕队愣是「收拾」了这班名将。消息传遍全镇、飞上大草原，那速度真是神了；等到一轮又红又圆的月亮从卡塔帕上游断崖般的河岸上升起时，在数不清的木屋农舍里，在远处麦田边的农家，在多少顿晚饭桌上，人们正带着一股自豪，或是一股活泛的好奇，谈论着这场大捷的种种细节，掂量着它对「咱们九人队」前程的影响。

「小伙子们」这时正在加利纳歇息，躲开了镇上居民钦慕的目光，懒洋洋地瘫在夸帕旅馆一间大卧室里，把当天一桩桩叫人心潮澎湃的事讲了一遍又一遍——这些事关系重大，如今讲着讲着，几乎要被讲成远古的史话了。他们一点儿也不觉得累，当场再赛一场都行，这场难得的大胜让他们如此开怀。队伍里如今没有牢骚，没有嫉妒，也没有互相挑刺。人人意气风发、红光满面，为自己在已赛完的这一场、以及明天要赛的那一场里担当的角色，都觉得分量十足。

===Original===
"Somehow, boys, I feel it in my bones that we are going to beat tomorrow," said Larry Boyne, who had won fresh laurels in the field, that day. And Larry's bright eyes sparkled anew as he spoke.

"Well, that's a new role for you to play, Larry," said Al Heaton who was admiringly hanging over Larry, whom he regarded as the rising player of the country. "You always were a croaker, you know, Larry, old boy, and for you to say that you feel confident of victory now, makes me almost shudder. It seems as if you were losing your head; only I know you are not."

"No, old chap, I am not losing my head. But you know I am rather superstitious; at least, my mother says so, and I have a queer notion, tonight, that we are going to do as well tomorrow as we did today."

"That's an encouraging sign, Larry," broke in Captain Hiram Porter. "But you fellows must all do your level best, all the same, and we mustn't let any notion of our superiority run away with us, for we are not superior, perhaps except that I do think that we are better fielders than the Galena boys."

"Whatever happens tomorrow, Al," said Larry, as they broke up their sitting for the night. "Put it down that I said that we were to win the second game in this championship series."

===Chinese===
「也不知怎么的，小伙子们，我骨头缝里都觉得，明天咱们准赢。」拉里·博因说——这天他在球场上又摘得了新的桂冠。说话间，拉里那双明亮的眼睛又焕发出光彩来。

「哟，这可是给你派的新角色了，拉里，」阿尔·希顿说，他正满怀倾慕地凑在拉里身边，把他看作本地冉冉升起的球员。「你知道，你一向是个乌鸦嘴，拉里老伙计；如今连你都说确信必胜，倒叫我心里发毛了。好像你脑子发热了似的——只不过我知道你并没有。」

「没有，老伙计，我没脑子发热。可你知道，我有点儿迷信；至少我妈是这么说的。今晚我有个怪念头：明天咱们会打得跟今天一样漂亮。」

「这是个好兆头，拉里，」海勒姆·波特队长插话道。「不过你们这些家伙照旧得拿出十二分的本事来，咱们可不能叫什么自以为高人一等的念头牵着鼻子跑，因为咱们并不高人一等——兴许除了这一点：我确实觉得，论当守场员，咱们比加利纳的小伙子们强。」

「明天不管出什么事，阿尔，」夜里散伙就寝的时候，拉里说，「记下来，我说了：这次冠军系列赛的第二场，咱们必赢。」

===Original===
"And if we lose, you will charge it to some adverse fate, won't you, Larry?"

"In the bright lexicon—you know the rest, Al."

By a singular coincidence, at that very hour, Miss Alice Howell, writing to her father the glad news, added a postscript thus: "You will think me overconfident, but I am sure the Catalpas will win the championship."

===Chinese===
「要是输了呢，你就把这账记到什么背时运气的头上，对不对，拉里？」

「在青春的灿烂词典里——后半句你是知道的，阿尔。」

说来也巧，恰恰在那个钟点，爱丽丝·豪厄尔小姐给父亲写信报喜，在信末添了这样一句附言：「您准会想我过于自信，可我确信，卡塔帕队必将赢得冠军。」

## X How the Good News Came / 十 好消息是如何传来的

===Original===
Catalpa was wide awake, next day, although the weather was hotter than ever and the little breeze that drew in from the prairie was laden with heat. The unexpected result of yesterday's game had set everybody to speculating on the issue of this day's contest. Some scandal was created by the appearance of Hank Jackson on the street with a roll of bills, offering to make bets on the game. It had never been the custom of anybody in Catalpa to wager anything on a baseball game, and there was some frowning now on the part of conservative and upright people; and those who were not specially conservative, but who disapproved of gaming, did not hesitate to reprove Hank in terms more forcible than elegant. Hank had spent some days in Bloomington, where he had frequented pool rooms and had acquired a taste for betting, and his brief experience was regarded by the younger portion of Catalpa with much awe and interest. He was followed about by the smaller boys of the town who listened while he bantered some of his cronies into making bets.

But public opinion in Catalpa was not yet educated to the point of engaging in gambling on the uncertain result of a baseball game. Added to this, it should be said, was Hank's persistence in offering bets on the defeat of the home nine. That was an unpopular side. Almost everybody wanted the Catalpas to win the game. It would decide the championship; and, although it was almost too much to hope for, there was a feeling of confidence through the town that was quite inexplicable. So, Hank, after making a swaggering tour of the shops and stores, but without receiving much popular countenance, quietly dropped out of the throngs which gathered at the street corners and in other public places. It was in vain that he argued with rude logic that it was just as safe to bet on a baseball game as on a horse race. Very few who listened to him cared to encourage this new sort of gambling.

This time, it was Al Heaton who fired the heart of Catalpa with the first intelligence from the Diamond Field. It was nearly three o'clock when his first despatch arrived, and the game had been called at two o'clock. There was much grumbling in the main street of the town, where numerous groups stood in the shade of awnings and tall buildings, waiting for the news. The windows of *The Leaf* office opened on this street, as well as on the side street on which the telegraph office was situated. Editor Downey had announced that he had made arrangements with Albert to send news directly from the baseball grounds in Galena, and that he would display a bulletin from his office windows.

Accordingly, when there was hung out a big white sheet of paper, with black lettering thereon, the assembly below was hushed in expectation. The despatch ran thus:

===Chinese===
第二天，卡塔帕全镇都醒着了——虽然天气比先前更热，从草原上吹进来的那点小风也饱含着热气。昨天那场球意想不到的结果，弄得人人都揣测起今天这场比赛的胜负来。汉克·杰克逊揣着一卷钞票在街上转悠，扬言要在这场球上打赌，惹出了些闲话。卡塔帕从来没有谁有在棒球赛上下注的习气，如今一些持重方正的人不由得皱起了眉头；还有一些人谈不上多么守旧，却看不惯赌博这档子事，也毫不客气地数落起汉克来，用词火气十足，顾不上文雅。汉克在布卢明顿（Bloomington）待过些日子，常泡台球房，染上了爱打赌的癖好；卡塔帕的年轻人对他这段短短的阅历，又敬畏又眼热。镇上的小孩子们跟在他屁股后头转，听他连哄带激，把几个相熟的伙伴逗得下了注。

可是卡塔帕的公众舆论，还没有开化到肯为一场棒球赛的悬乎结果去赌博的地步。再者，还得说一句：汉克死乞白赖地净押主队输。这一头可不受人待见。几乎人人都盼着卡塔帕队赢。这场比赛将定出冠军归属；而且，虽说这希望近乎奢望，全镇却弥漫着一种说不清道不明的信心。于是汉克在各家铺面里神气活现地兜了一圈，没讨到多少人附和，便悄没声儿地从街角和其他公共场所聚集的人群里退了出来。他粗声粗气地讲歪理，说赌棒球跟赌赛马一样稳当，可全是白费口舌。听他说话的人里头，没几个想捧这种新式赌法的场。

这一回，头一个从内场点火、烧旺卡塔帕人心的，是阿尔·希顿。他的第一份电报到的时候快三点钟了，而比赛两点钟就已开打。镇子的大街上怨声四起，一群群人站在遮阳篷和高楼的荫凉里，等着消息。《叶报》报馆的窗户正对着这条街，也对着电报局所在的那条横街。唐尼编辑已经放话，说他和阿尔伯特讲好了，消息直接从加利纳球场拍来，他会从报馆窗户张出公告。

于是，当一张写有黑色大字的大白纸张挂出来时，楼下的人群屏息期待。电文如下：

===Original===
> Everybody confident. Larry Boyne says our nine will win the game. Weather hot, and the dust intolerable. Look out for fun.
> Albert Heaton.

"What does he mean by looking out for fun; and who cares what Larry Boyne thinks?" growled Hank Jackson. "I should think he might send us something more bracing than that by this time."

===Chinese===
> 人人信心十足。拉里·博因说咱们的九人队必胜。天气火热，尘土难当。等着瞧好戏吧。
> 阿尔伯特·希顿。

「他说『等着瞧好戏』是什么意思？再说，拉里·博因怎么想，关谁的事？」汉克·杰克逊咕哝道。「我还以为这会儿他总该拍点更提气的东西来呢。」

===Original===
But the straggling cheer that greeted Albert's encouraging message drowned Jackson's grumbling, and the crowd showed by their excitement that they were ready to accept the slightest omen as proof positive that the Catalpa nine would carry the day. So, when Judge Howell's carriage drove up and halted under the shade of the huge catalpa tree that grew in front of Dr. Selby's drug store, from which the fair Alice could see the throng and watch for the bulletin from the newspaper office, there was a little hurrah from some of the younger lads. They seemed to think that the young lady, in some fashion, represented the absent Judge, who was now recognized as one of the steadfast friends of the band of heroes.

"That's a good sign! I'll swear to gracious!" said Rough and Ready, in a low and hoarse whisper, as he saw the Judge's handsome bays, champing their bits, and prancing uneasily under the shade of the spreading catalpa. "It's a good sign, for that gal never went back on the nine, and her coming will bring good luck. Mark my words, Jake!" Jake, the big butcher, nodded his head and only said "yaw," when the bulletin was again flung out from the window of the printing-office.

The magical black letters were read in silence broken only by the stamping of the horses tethered along the street and worried by the flies. This is what the eager spectators read:

> First inning—Catalpas, 1; Galenas, 0.

===Chinese===
可是，阿尔这份鼓劲儿的电报引来零零落落的欢呼，早把杰克逊的咕哝淹没了；人群的兴奋劲儿表明，哪怕是最细微的兆头，他们都乐意当作卡塔帕九人队必胜的铁证。于是，当豪厄尔法官的马车驶过来，停在塞尔比医生药店前那棵大梓树的树荫下——美丽的爱丽丝坐在车里，正好看得见人群、守得了报馆的公告——几个年纪小的少年发出了一小阵欢呼。他们似乎觉得，这位小姐多少代表了不在场的法官——如今法官已被公认为这班英雄好汉最坚定的朋友之一。

「好兆头！我对天起誓！」老硬汉压着嗓子、沙哑地悄声说；他瞧着法官家那两匹漂亮的枣红马咬着嚼子、在张开如盖的梓树荫下不安地踏着蹄子。「这是个好兆头，那丫头从来没对九人队变过心，她一来准带来好运。记住我的话，杰克！」大个子屠夫杰克点了点头；等印刷所的窗户里再次甩出公告时，他只应了一声「是咧」。

那些有魔力的黑字被人们默默认读，四下里只听得见拴在街边的马匹被苍蝇搅得跺蹄的声响。翘首以盼的看客们读到的是：

> 第一局——卡塔帕队 1；加利纳队 0。

===Original===
"A big round goose egg!" screamed Lew Morris, with delight. Then he raised a hurrah, and the small boys took up the yell. Horses jumped and tore at their halters and vagrant dogs barked madly about the street. Then there were smiles and even broad laughter among the devoted supporters of the home nine. Almost everybody looked pleased, and Dr. Selby, with the easy confidence of an old friend, went to the side of the Judge's carriage and shook hands heartily with Miss Alice who was waving her parasol with a vague notion that it was necessary to celebrate the auspicious opening of the game.

"I didn't tell you, did I, doctor, that I dreamed, last night, that we had won the game? Well, I did. Aunt Anstress says that dreams go by contraries and that that means our nine will be defeated. But I don't believe that; do you, doctor?"

"Well, I don't believe in dreams, anyhow, Miss Alice, and so I hardly think that that counts. But we will keep on thinking that the boys will beat, today, and even if we are disappointed, we have yet one more chance."

The doctor, accepting Alice's invitation, took a seat in the carriage from which advantageous point he looked over the gathering throng, now reinforced by arrivals from the region roundabout the town, for the news had gone forth that despatches were coming in from Al Heaton, and every man, woman and child who had the least interest in the game (and these were many) and could leave the labors and duties of the day, was there to hear.

"It looks as it did in the war, when the news from Shiloh and Vicksburg was coming in; doesn't it, doctor?"

"I don't know about that, Alice. I was in the war, myself, you know; was at Port Hudson and Vicksburg. You were a baby then, and I believe your father was in Congress. Yes, I guess it does look like war times. But see! There comes another bulletin!"

===Chinese===
「好大一个滚圆的鸭蛋！」刘易斯·莫里斯乐得尖声喊道。接着他高呼了一声，小孩子们跟着喊成一片。马匹惊跳起来，扯着缰绳；野狗在街上疯叫。随后，主队九人的死忠拥护者当中绽出了笑脸，甚至有人放声大笑。几乎人人面有喜色；塞尔比医生带着老友的从容自信，走到法官的马车旁，同爱丽丝小姐热烈握手——小姐正挥着阳伞，模模糊糊地觉得，这吉祥的开局非庆祝一下不可。

「医生，我昨晚梦见咱们赢了这场球，这话跟您说了没有？嗯，我真梦见了。安斯特蕾丝姑妈说，梦都是反的，那就是说咱们的九人队要输。可我不信这个；您信吗，医生？」

「这个嘛，反正我不信梦，爱丽丝小姐，所以我不觉得那算数。不过咱们照样相信小伙子们今天会赢；就算落了空，咱们也还有一次机会。」

医生领受爱丽丝的邀请，在马车里坐了下来；从这处有利的位置，他把越聚越多的人群尽收眼底——四乡八邻赶来的人不断加入，因为阿尔·希顿电报连连的消息已经传开，凡是对这场比赛有一星半点兴趣（这样的人可不少）、又能撂下当天活计和事务的男女老少，都到场来听了。

「这光景就跟打仗那些年一个样，夏伊洛和维克斯堡的消息传来的时候；是不是，医生？」

「这个我说不好，爱丽丝。你知道，我自己就打过仗；波特哈德森和维克斯堡我都待过。那时候你还是个吃奶的娃娃，我记得你父亲正在国会里。嗯，我看这架势确实像打仗的年月。瞧！又出公告了！」

===Original===
Editor Downey had rigorously excluded from his office all outsiders, and was devoting his personal attention to the all-important business of the day. With his own hands, he hung out the paper sheet bearing these words:

> 2nd inning—Catalpas, 0; Galenas, 1; 3rd inning, Catalpas, 0; Galenas, 0.

"Not so good as it might be," remarked Dr. Selby, cheerfully, "but it will grow better, by and by."

===Chinese===
唐尼编辑把闲杂人等一律挡在报馆外，正亲自坐镇操持当天头等要紧的大事。他亲手挂出那张写着以下字句的纸：

> 第二局——卡塔帕队 0；加利纳队 1；第三局——卡塔帕队 0；加利纳队 0。

「不算顶好，」塞尔比医生乐呵呵地说，「不过待会儿就会好起来的。」

===Original===
A little cloud passed over the face of Alice, and she bit her lip with vexation as Hank Jackson bawled with a rough voice, "Ten to five on the Galenas!"

"If I were a man, I'd like to take that offer," she said, her eyes sparkling.

"Oh, no, you wouldn't, Alice," cried her friend Ida. "You wouldn't encourage gambling on baseball, I'm sure."

"Perhaps not; but if I were a man, I would like to thrash that big ruffian."

===Chinese===
一小片阴云掠过爱丽丝的脸；汉克·杰克逊粗着嗓门喊「押加利纳队，十赔五！」她气得咬住了嘴唇。

「我要是个男人，真想接下这赌注，」她说，眼睛里闪着光。

「哦，不，你才不会呢，爱丽丝，」她的朋友艾达（Ida）嚷道。「我敢肯定，你总不会助长拿棒球赌博的歪风。」

「也许不会；可我要是个男人，倒真想抽那个大恶棍一顿鞭子。」

===Original===
Better news came, after a little while. The bulletin for the fourth inning showed four for the Catalpas and a big round "0" for their opponents. At this, there was a general and apparently concerted hurrah from the company in the street below. Editor Downey, as if thinking the cheer a personal compliment, put his frowsy head out of the window and bowed with as much grace as was possible under the circumstances.

"Mr. Downey's hair looks as if he was laboring under great excitement," said the apothecary, blandly smiling at the editor's somewhat touseled appearance. "Every individual hair is standing on end, as if he were charged with electricity."

Alice laughed joyously and seemed glad to find something under which she could cover her great elation at the good news from the North. Miss Ida uttered sarcastic remarks about the editor's exuberant comments in the morning paper regarding the coming contest in Galena. She declared that she did not think the game nearly as important as any one of the decisive battles of the war. And she was sure that *The Leaf* would be perfectly ridiculous, next day, if the Catalpas were to win the championship. Her remarks were cut short by the display of another bulletin announcing the result of the fifth inning in these terms:—

> Hurrah for our nine! Fifth inning—Catalpas, 0; Galenas, 0.

===Chinese===
过了一会儿，更好的消息来了。第四局的公告显示：卡塔帕队得四分，对手是一个滚圆的大「0」。这一下，下面街上的人群齐声欢呼起来，倒像是事先约好了似的。唐尼编辑仿佛把这声欢呼当成献给自己的礼敬，把他那颗乱蓬蓬的脑袋探出窗外，在当时情形下所能有的最大风度里鞠了一躬。

「唐尼先生的头发瞧着像是激动得不轻，」药剂师说，对着编辑那副有些蓬乱的模样温和地微笑。「一根一根全竖着，跟浑身充了电似的。」

爱丽丝开怀大笑，似乎庆幸找到了个由头，好把她对北方捷报的那股大欢喜遮掩过去。艾达小姐则对编辑在晨报上就加利纳赛事写的那番兴头十足的评论挖苦了几句。她宣称，这场比赛怎么也比不上战争中任何一场决定性的战役要紧。她还断定，卡塔帕队若是赢了冠军，《叶报》明天准得荒唐透顶。她的话被又一张公告打断了，公告这样宣布第五局的结果：

> 为咱们的九人队欢呼！第五局——卡塔帕队 0；加利纳队 0。

===Original===
"What in thunder does that mean?" asked Lew Morris, angrily. "Why does the numbskull tell us to hurrah for our nine when both sides have a zero?"

A yell of derision went up from the crowd, and the editor, hearing groans and catcalls in the street below, put out his head and, with much trepidation, cried, "It was a mistake. I forgot to put on the sixth inning. Catalpas, one; Galenas, nix!"

A loud laugh greeted this sally, and the crowd good-humoredly proposed three cheers for *The Catalpa Leaf*, which were given in a random fashion, mingled with laughter. Mr. Downey, now well-smeared with ink, and perspiring with excitement, acknowledged the salute with gravity.

"Six innings played and the Catalpas are six to the Galena's one!" exclaimed Alice, who was keeping the score with an assiduity that seemed to come from a belief that exactness in the figures would, somehow, affect the final result. Scraps of paper, on which observers had marked the score and had set down their prognostications of the innings yet to come, were circulated through the crowd. The Catalpas now had the lead, and it would be difficult for their adversaries to come up with them.

===Chinese===
「这鬼名堂到底是什么意思？」刘易斯·莫里斯气冲冲地问。「两边都是零蛋，这榆木脑袋干嘛叫咱们为九人队欢呼？」

人群里爆出一阵哄笑；编辑听见楼下街上一片嘘声和倒彩，把头探出来，战战兢兢地喊道：「弄错了！我忘了写第六局。卡塔帕队一分；加利纳队，零蛋！」

这话一出，满街大笑；人群又好脾气地提议为《卡塔帕叶报》三呼万岁，欢呼得七零八落，夹着笑声。这时的唐尼先生墨迹满身，兴奋得直冒汗，郑重其事地答谢了这番致意。

「六局打完，卡塔帕队六分，加利纳队一分！」爱丽丝喊道。她记比分记得一丝不苟，仿佛相信数字记得精确，冥冥之中就能左右最终结果似的。看客们把记了比分、写了对后面各局预测的纸头传来传去。卡塔帕队如今领先，对手要追上来可就难了。

===Original===
Lew Morris, leaning on the door of the carriage, chatted with Alice, drawing on his vivid imagination for pictures of the nine as they were probably looking now, away up there in Galena. He could see, he thought, Hiram Porter devouring the ground as he made his bases with a giant's stride, his handsome face glowing with mingled heat and determination. He could even hear Larry's voice, in a stage whisper, crying, "Go it, Hiram!" And he could see Larry, at third base, when the Catalpas were in the field, making one of those superb running catches of his, Ben Burton looking on, "as if he would eat him up," added Lewis, jocularly.

"Why should Ben want to eat Larry up?" asked Dr. Selby, innocently. "Does he love him so?"

"On the contrary, quite the reverse," laughed Lewis. "Larry is showing himself to be the best player in the nine, and as Ben thought that *he* was the best, and is finding out that he is not, he loves Larry accordingly. Besides that, he is jealous of Larry for other reasons," and the young man fixed a bold look on the blushing face of Miss Alice. She turned away to see if another bulletin were not ready, and the doctor shook his head deprecatingly at Lewis.

There was much time for talk, however, before another despatch from the seat of war appeared. The impatient crowd, panting in the heat that was more and more oppressive as the sun approached the west, flung all sorts of appeals upwards to the windows of the office of *The Leaf*. There was no response, although Mr. Downey, as if to contradict Hank Jackson's loud jeer that the editor had gone to sleep, showed his shaggy head at the window and made a negative motion with the same. There was no news.

===Chinese===
刘易斯·莫里斯倚着车门和爱丽丝闲聊，凭着他那活灵活现的想象力，描绘九人队此刻在遥远的加利纳多半是副什么模样。他想，他简直看得见海勒姆·波特迈着巨人的大步踏垒，恨不得一步把地跨过去，俊朗的脸膛上热气与决心交相辉映。他甚至听得见拉里压着嗓门的舞台腔在喊：「加油，海勒姆！」他还看得见，卡塔帕队上场守备时，拉里在三垒上演他那记漂亮的奔跑接杀，本·伯顿在一旁瞧着——「瞧那样子，恨不得把他生吞了」，刘易斯打趣地补了一句。

「本凭什么想把拉里生吞了？」塞尔比医生懵然地问。「他那么爱他吗？」

「恰恰相反，正正倒了个个儿，」刘易斯笑道。「拉里正显出自己是九人队里最好的球员，而本原以为*自己*才是最好的，如今正发现自己不是，所以他才这么『爱』拉里。再说，他嫉妒拉里还有别的缘故。」说着，年轻人直勾勾地盯着爱丽丝小姐绯红的脸。她扭过头去，看看是不是又该出公告了；医生朝刘易斯摇摇头，怪他不该这么说。

不过，等下一份前线战报出来之前，大家有的是工夫闲聊。太阳偏西，暑气越发逼人，不耐烦的人群喘着气，朝《叶报》报馆的窗户抛上去五花八门的催问。毫无回音——虽然唐尼先生为了驳斥汉克·杰克逊那声大喊大叫的奚落——说编辑睡着了——把他那颗毛茸茸的脑袋在窗口亮了亮，又用那颗脑袋摇了摇。没有消息。

===Original===
Finally, just as some of the less patient were beginning to make their way homewards, like a banner of victory, the sheet of paper again appeared. This time, it was blazoned with these returns:—

> 7th inning—Catalpas, 1; Galenas, 0; 8th inning—Catalpas, 0; Galenas, 1.

"An even thing for the two innings!" cried Lew Morris triumphantly. "The Galenas cannot possibly pull up in the last inning! The game is ours! The game is ours!"

Lew's jubilant shout was taken up by the crowd, which now grew denser again, and the excitement mounted to fever heat as the sun sank behind the cottonwoods below the town. Satisfied that the game and the championship were virtually won, some of the elder citizens, after exchanging congratulations with everybody that had a word of joy on their lips, walked homewards. But some of them stopped on the road and turned a listening ear towards the main street to hear the rousing cheer that soon went up, telling the town and all the Stone River Valley that the game was won and that our nine had captured the pennant of Northern Illinois.

A grimy and inky young imp, on the roof of *The Leaf* building, hoisted a particularly inky and grimy flag as the editor hung out from his window this bulletin:—

===Chinese===
最后，就在几个最没耐性的人开始往家走的时候，那张纸又出现了，宛如一面胜利的旗帜。这一回，上面赫然写的是这样的战报：

> 第七局——卡塔帕队 1；加利纳队 0；第八局——卡塔帕队 0；加利纳队 1。

「这两局打了个平手！」刘易斯·莫里斯得意洋洋地喊。「最后一局加利纳队说什么也追不上来啦！这场比赛是咱们的了！是咱们的了！」

刘易斯欢腾的喊声被人群接了过去，人群这时又稠密起来；太阳落到镇子下游的三角叶杨背后时，兴奋劲儿烧到了白热。眼看这场球和冠军头衔十拿九稳，几位年长的市民跟每个嘴上挂着喜气的人道了贺，便往家走了。可有些人走到半道又停下，侧耳朝着大街，听那很快便响起的震天欢呼——它告诉全镇和整个斯通河谷：球赛赢了，咱们的九人队夺下了北伊利诺伊的锦旗。

《叶报》大楼的房顶上，一个满身油墨的小淘气鬼升起一面格外乌黑邋遢的旗子，与此同时，编辑从窗口张出了这张公告：

===Original===
> The victory is complete! Old Catalpa to the front! Glory enough for one day! Following is the score by innings:

===Chinese===
> 大获全胜！老卡塔帕扬眉吐气！这一天的荣耀够受用的！以下为逐局比分：
> 1
> 0
> 0
> 4

> 今晚加利纳队将在夸帕旅馆设宴款待卡塔帕队，届时定有一场痛快的欢聚。
> 阿尔伯特·希顿。

===Original===
"And now for the championship of the State, dad?" shouted Tom Selby, exultingly, as his father descended from the carriage of the Judge. Alice, who was beaming with delight, could hardly speak her joy. The great contest was over, and the home nine would come back covered with glory. But she shook her head at Tom's vainglorious remark. The league games were all made up for the season, she knew, and it would be difficult, if not impossible, to secure a challenge from any club in the league. Oh, no, she couldn't think of it. Tom must not think of it, at least, not until another summer.

The good doctor smiled at the lad's enthusiasm and said that glory enough for one day meant glory enough for one season. There were other contests before the home nine, and they could be content, or they should be, to wear the laurels already won, whatever happened to them hereafter. They could not lose any prestige by any manner of means.

When Judge Howell arrived by the early evening train from Pawpaw, he was surprised to see the dingy flag of *The Catalpa Leaf* drooping lazily from its staff. He had not forgotten that the second game in the Northern District Championship was to have been played that afternoon; and he remembered his daughter's prediction of success. But it seemed incredible that this should have actually come to pass. As he alighted from the train, his judicial dignity a little soiled by travel and perspiration, he was met by Rough and Ready, who, with a slight touch of his coonskin cap, the only recognition of high station of which he was ever capable, said, "Any baggage, Jedge? carry it as cheap as anybody. Our nine has flaxed out the Galenas—eight to three! Big thing, Jedge! Lemme take that gripsack. Great day for old Catalpa, Jedge. Your darter, she said as how she allowed that you mought like to get the news straight, so I told her I'd come up and tell you quick. Thank you, Jedge." And, dropping a silver quarter into his pocket, Rough and Ready turned and collared a stranger from whom he wrested his valise and marched triumphantly down into the town.

===Chinese===
「接下来该争全州冠军了吧，爸？」汤姆·塞尔比（Tom Selby）兴高采烈地喊，这时他父亲正从法官的马车上下来。爱丽丝喜气盈盈，欢喜得几乎说不出话来。大赛已毕，主队九人将载誉归来。可对汤姆这句心气高涨的话，她摇了摇头。她知道，联盟的比赛本赛季已全部排定，想从联盟里任何俱乐部求来一场挑战赛，纵非绝无可能，也是难上加难。哦，不行，她想都不敢想。汤姆也别想这个了——至少，得等来年夏天。

好医生对着少年的热乎劲儿笑了笑，说：一天的荣耀够受用，也就等于一个赛季的荣耀够受用了。主队九人面前还有别的比赛，无论今后遇到什么，他们都满可以——也理应——戴着已经赢得的桂冠心满意足。无论如何，他们的威望是丢不了的。

豪厄尔法官乘傍晚的早班车从帕帕回来时，惊讶地看见《卡塔帕叶报》那面灰扑扑的旗子有气无力地垂在旗杆上。他没有忘记，那天下午本该打北部赛区冠军赛的第二场；他也记得女儿预言必胜。可这事竟然真的应验了，又叫人难以置信。他下车的时候，法官的威仪让旅途和汗水沾染了些许——老硬汉迎了上来，轻轻碰了碰他那顶浣熊皮帽——这是他对高官显贵所能行的唯一礼数——说道：「有行李吗，法官？我搬行李跟谁家一样便宜。咱们的九人队把加利纳队揍扁啦——八比三！了不得，法官！让我来拿那个手提包。老卡塔帕的大日子啊，法官。您家丫头说，她琢磨着您保准想听个准信儿，我就跟她说，我这就跑来快快告诉您。谢谢您，法官。」一枚两毛五的银币落进腰包，老硬汉转过身，一把拽住一位生客，从他手里夺过旅行箱，得胜还朝似的雄赳赳进了镇。

===Original===
When the Judge, clothed once more in the dignity of cleanliness and his home headship, heard that night from the animated lips of his daughter the story of the winning of the championship, he said, with an air of graceful condescension, "It was a famous victory, Alice. We have reason to be proud of our nine; and I will venture to say that when we get the full particulars of the game, we shall find that that fine-looking young fellow, Lawrence Boyne, contributed the largest share to the triumph."

When the details of the game were brought to Catalpa, next day, in a letter to *The Leaf*, it was found that the Judge knew just what he was talking about.

But greater news than this came with Larry Boyne and Hiram Porter, a week or two later. The nine had been playing a few games along the river towns and had rested for a day or two in Rock Island, after playing the Dacotahs of that city. Several of the nine took advantage of a lull in their engagements to visit Catalpa. Mr. Heaton and Albert had returned home, and Larry and Hiram had gone to Chicago on some mysterious errand, nobody knew just what. Neddie Ellis was one of those who had come back to Catalpa while the time was passing before they should play the new series of games beginning with the Moline club. Neddie looked very wise when asked where Larry and Hiram had gone, and Albert Heaton assumed a most important air whenever he said anything about the doings of the two absent members of the nine.

But it all came out in due time. Captain Porter and his trusty lieutenant arrived by the noon train, and before the sun had set everybody in Catalpa knew that a match had been arranged between the Catalpa nine and the Calumet club for the State Championship. It was indeed wonderful news, and nothing since the war had happened to stir the population of that region as the intelligence. There were divers opinions regarding this unexpected development. Many thought that it was indiscreet for so young and green a club as the Catalpas to challenge the Calumets—the famous and renowned Calumets. Then there were others who thought that it was presumptuous for the Catalpa boys even so much as to ask any leading club to play them merely because a triumph had been unexpectedly achieved in Galena. But all agreed that it was a great feather in the cap of "our nine" that the Chicago club should have accepted the challenge, or should have agreed to meet them on any terms whatever.

===Chinese===
当天夜里，法官重新穿戴起一身清爽和一家之主的威仪，听女儿眉飞色舞地讲述夺冠的经过，然后带着优雅屈尊的神气说：「这是场赫赫有名的胜利，爱丽丝。咱们有理由为九人队骄傲；而且我敢说，等咱们拿到比赛的详细战报，准会发现那位仪表堂堂的年轻人劳伦斯·博因（Lawrence Boyne），对这场大捷的贡献数他最大。」

第二天，比赛的详情由一封致《叶报》的信寄到了卡塔帕，人们这才发现，法官说的句句在行。

可是一两周后，随拉里·博因和海勒姆·波特一道来的，是比这更大的消息。九人队沿河各镇连打了好几场，跟罗克艾兰城的达科他队（Dacotahs）交手之后，在那儿歇了一两天。队里几个人趁着赛程的空当回卡塔帕看了看。希顿先生和阿尔伯特已经回家，拉里和海勒姆却去了芝加哥，办什么神神秘秘的差事，谁也说不清。内迪·埃利斯（Neddie Ellis）也是回卡塔帕的人之一；在迎战以莫林俱乐部（Moline club）开头的新一轮系列赛之前，日子就这么打发着。问到拉里和海勒姆去了哪儿，内迪总是一副胸有成竹的聪明相；阿尔伯特·希顿呢，只要说起九人队这两位缺席队员的行踪，就摆出一副事关重大的架势。

不过到时候一切自会水落石出。波特队长和他那位可靠的副手乘午班车到了，太阳还没落山，卡塔帕全镇都知道了：卡塔帕九人队与卡柳梅特俱乐部（Calumet club）的全州冠军赛已经谈成了。这真是了不起的大新闻；自打战争结束以来，还没有哪桩事像这个消息这样，搅动过这一带的人心。对这桩意外的事态，众说纷纭。许多人认为，像卡塔帕这样又年轻又嫩的俱乐部，去挑战卡柳梅特——大名鼎鼎、赫赫有名的卡柳梅特——实在欠考虑。另一些人则认为，卡塔帕的小伙子们仅仅因为在加利纳意外打了个胜仗，就开口邀任何一家一流俱乐部来赛球，未免自视过高。不过大家一致公认：这家芝加哥俱乐部肯接受挑战——或者说肯在任何条件下应战——本身就是「咱们九人队」帽子上的一根大翎毛。

===Original===
"I am not certain whether I am glad or sorry that our nine will play the Calumets, papa," said Alice Howell. "I mean that I cannot tell yet whether I shall be disappointed if they lose. I depend a great deal on my impressions, you know, and I haven't any as yet."

The Judge smiled at his daughter's odd notion of waiting for impressions, and replied, "I do not wait for any inspiration on the subject, my child. I am sure that the Catalpa nine will be badly beaten. I don't know much about baseball, but I do know enough to know that the Calumet club has been in the newspapers for a long time as the great baseball club of the northwest."

"That's so, papa," sighed Alice, "and I have dreadful forebodings when I think of the risk that they have undertaken."

"Nothing venture, nothing have, Alice, and it will be no disgrace if our nine are defeated by the Calumets. Unless they are very badly beaten indeed, and that is not improbable, to be sure, they will bring some new honors off the field."

===Chinese===
「咱们的九人队要跟卡柳梅特比赛了，爸爸，我说不清自己是高兴还是遗憾，」爱丽丝·豪厄尔说。「我是说，我还说不好他们要是输了，我会不会失望。您知道的，我这个人多半凭印象行事，可眼下我还没有一点儿印象。」

法官对女儿这种坐等印象的怪念头微微一笑，答道：「这件事我可不等什么灵感，我的孩子。我敢肯定，卡塔帕九人队会输得很惨。棒球我懂的不多，可我也懂得够多了——卡柳梅特俱乐部长期以来在报上被称为西北地区第一棒球劲旅。」

「正是这话，爸爸，」爱丽丝叹道，「一想到他们担下的这个风险，我就有不祥的预感。」

「不入虎穴，焉得虎子，爱丽丝；咱们的九人队就算输给卡柳梅特，也不算丢脸。除非输得极惨——说实话这也不是不可能——否则他们下场时总能再添几分新的荣誉。」

===Original===
The Judge's conservative and moderate view of the case was that of the average of Catalpa. To play the Calumets was in itself an honor.

Henry Jackson represented the most discouraging element in Catalpa public opinion. And when Ben Burton returned to town for a day's holiday, and became at once unusually familiar with Hank, Larry's face clouded and Alice Howell confidentially informed her friend Ida Boardman that she never could abide Ben Burton, and that now she knew he was a man who would consort with mean companions. Nothing could be lower, she thought, than the course that Henry Jackson had taken during the late contest between the Catalpas and the Galenas.

It was only by a lucky accident that the Calumets had been able to find a place in their later engagements for a championship series of three games with the Catalpas. The sudden sickness of several members of the Osceola club, engaged to play the Calumets, had made it necessary to cancel all the engagements of the former club for the season. The Osceolas had been overtaken by a contagious disease that had made sad havoc that summer, as many will remember, among strangers who visited the lower portion of the State, which had been under water from late in February until the beginning of May. But the ill-luck of the Osceola club was the means of opening a way for the Catalpas to play the Calumets; and that was felt to be something almost providential—at least, in the town of Catalpa.

===Chinese===
法官这种持重平和的看法，代表了卡塔帕的普遍舆论。能同卡柳梅特交手，本身就是一种光荣。

亨利·杰克逊代表了卡塔帕舆论中最叫人泄气的那一路。待到本·伯顿回镇上歇一天假，立刻跟汉克打得火热、不同寻常，拉里的脸就阴了下来；爱丽丝·豪厄尔也私下告诉她的朋友艾达·博德曼（Ida Boardman），说她从来就受不了本·伯顿，如今更认定他是个肯跟下三滥同伴鬼混的人。她想，在刚过去的那场卡塔帕队对加利纳队的比赛中，亨利·杰克逊的做派真是下作到了家。

卡柳梅特能在后半段的赛程里腾出地方，跟卡塔帕打三场冠军系列赛，纯属一场侥幸的偶然。原定与卡柳梅特交手的奥西奥拉俱乐部（Osceola club）几名队员突然病倒，致使该俱乐部本季度的全部赛约不得不取消。奥西奥拉队染上的是一种传染病；许多人还记得，那年夏天，这种病在到访本州南部的外乡人中间为祸甚烈——那一带从二月底一直泡在大水里，直到五月初才退。可奥西奥拉队的不幸，偏偏为卡塔帕队打开了与卡柳梅特交手的门路；这在人们看来简直近乎天意——至少，在卡塔帕镇上是这么觉得的。

## XI In a New Field / 十一 新赛场

===Original===
"I wish so many of the Catalpa folks had not come in to see the game, today," said Larry Boyne, discontentedly, on the morning of the first of the championship series of games in Chicago, late in the following October. "It is bad enough to feel like a cat in a strange garret as I do here, without the feeling added of being watched by our friends from home, who will be so awfully cut up if we do not win."

"But you are not afraid of our losing, are you, Larry? And I am sure there is one young lady, at least, whose smiles will encourage you," said Hiram Porter, with a grin that was meant to be sly and also cheery. "It is pretty generally understood among the boys (and as long as we are alone together, there is no need of our being shamefaced about it) that you and Miss Alice have come to an understanding, as the saying is. You needn't say whether that is so or not, Larry, my boy. But, if I were in your place, I would be glad to have those beautiful and sympathetic eyes watching my play. It would make me put in my very best licks, you may be sure of that."

Larry murmured something about there being a difference in people, and turned the subject to the preparations to be made for the day's event. The Catalpas had had only a little opportunity to make themselves familiar with the Chicago baseball grounds. At the end of a game played on the previous day, they had a little practice at pitching, and had taken in the situation of the arena sufficiently to enable them to be not entirely strangers to the place.

They found themselves inside of a complete enclosure, skirted by a grandstand at one end and uncovered and open seats at the other. A high board fence bounded the grassy lawn on which the Diamond Field was laid, and the seats for spectators rose above this fence, so that the players were securely left to their own devices while the game should be in progress. A breeze from the lake, tempered by the October sun, swept over the grounds, and was broken, when the wind arose, by the screen formed by the board enclosure.

===Chinese===
「我倒希望今天别来这么多卡塔帕乡亲看球。」十月末，芝加哥冠军系列赛第一场开打的那个早晨，拉里·博因闷闷不乐地说。「在这儿已经够像一只钻进陌生阁楼的猫了，偏偏还要加上一层被人从家乡来的朋友们盯着的感觉——咱们要是赢不了，他们该多伤心啊。」

「可你不怕咱们输吧，拉里？我敢肯定，至少有一位年轻小姐，她的微笑会给你鼓劲儿的。」海勒姆·波特说，咧嘴一笑，想显得又狡黠又欢快。「小伙子们心里差不多都明白了（反正就咱们俩在这儿，没什么好难为情的）：你和爱丽丝小姐，就像俗话说的，已经心有默契了。是不是这么回事，你不用讲，拉里，我的小子。可我要是处在你的位置，有那双美丽而善解人意的眼睛看着我的球，我高兴还来不及呢。保管能叫我使出浑身的解数，这你尽管放心。」

拉里含含糊糊地说了句人和人不一样什么的，就把话头转到当天赛事的准备上去了。卡塔帕队几乎没什么机会熟悉芝加哥的棒球场。头一天一场比赛打完，他们练了练投球，把这竞技场的形势大致看了看，总算不至于对这地方全然陌生。

他们发现自己置身于一个四围封闭的场地里：一端衬着正面大看台，另一端是无遮无盖的露天座位。一道高高的木板围栏圈出草坪，棒球内场就铺在这片草坪上；观众的座位高过围栏，好让比赛进行时球员们安安稳稳地不受打扰。一股湖上来的风，让十月的太阳焐得温和了些，掠过场地；风一起头，就被木板围栏形成的屏障挡了下去。

===Original===
When the nine, with beating hearts and quickened pulses, entered the grounds on the day so fraught with importance to them, they were a little dumbfounded to see that an immense crowd of people, perhaps ten thousand, all told, occupied the vast array of seats that lined the amphitheater. A brass band blared and brayed in a tall stand set apart for them, and the entrance of the Catalpa nine was the signal for a burst of kindly applause that helped to reassure the lads composing that now well-known club. Since the matches played in the river towns, the nine had met some of the best-known clubs in the State, and in Iowa. With varying success, but generally doing credit to their own native place, the Catalpas had attracted attention by their uniformly excellent play, their manly bearing, and by their steady habits. They had made no enemies. So, when the young fellows, clad in their blue and white uniform, came into the range of vision of the throngs in the grandstand and boxes, a round of applause greeted them, and one enthusiastic citizen from Catalpa, no less a person than the deputy sheriff of Dean County, ventured to propose three cheers for the Catalpa nine. The proposition fell very flat, and, covered with confusion, the deputy sheriff sat down and mopped his manly brow.

As Hiram Porter threw up the penny for the toss, Larry's eye involuntarily sought a curtained box to which his attention had been directed, the day before, as he had inspected the grounds in company with Miss Ida Boardman, Miss Alice Howell and two other ladies from Catalpa. The party was under the guidance of Mr. Heaton. Albert was never long in one place. He was too highly excited to be depended upon as an escort for the young ladies, and he divided his time between his old companions of the Catalpa nine and the pitcher of the Calumets, Samuel Morse, an old school chum, who had helped signally in arranging the present contest.

So, as Larry's glance lighted on the first box to the right of the grandstand, it caught an answering smile from Miss Alice, and Albert Heaton, who was momentarily fluttering about the box, waved his hand to the favorite third baseman of the Catalpas and said, under his breath, "Sail in, old boy!"

"You don't imagine that Mr. Boyne heard that, do you, in all this noise?" asked Alice, with rosy face and sparkling eyes.

===Chinese===
当九人队心怦怦跳、脉搏加速地走进这个对他们意义重大的场地时，他们看到沿着圆形剧场排开的庞大座位阵上黑压压坐满了人——粗粗一算，也许足足有一万——不由得有点发懵。一支铜管乐队在专为他们搭起的高台上吹得震天响；卡塔帕九人队一进场，便博得一阵善意的掌声，让如今已声名在外的这家俱乐部的小伙子们安心了不少。自河畔各镇的比赛以来，九人队已与本州和艾奥瓦州几支最有名的俱乐部交过手。成绩虽有起落，但总体上没给家乡丢脸；卡塔帕队以他们一贯出色的球技、堂堂正正的风度和稳健的做派赢得了注意。他们没有结下冤家。于是，当这帮身着蓝白两色球衣的小伙子走进大看台和包厢里众人的视野时，迎接他们的是一阵掌声；卡塔帕来的一位热心市民——不是别人，正是迪安县的副治安官——大胆提议为卡塔帕九人队三呼万岁。这提议应者寥寥，副治安官窘得满脸通红，坐下来直抹他那男子汉的脑门。

海勒姆·波特把一分钱硬币抛向空中猜先时，拉里的眼睛不由自主地去寻那间挂帘的包厢——前一天他陪着艾达·博德曼小姐、爱丽丝·豪厄尔小姐和另外两位卡塔帕来的女士参观场地时，注意力就被引到了那里。这一行人由希顿先生领着。阿尔伯特从来在一个地方待不长。他兴奋过了头，靠不住，当不好年轻小姐们的向导；他的工夫一半分给卡塔帕九人队的老伙伴，一半分给卡柳梅特队的投手萨缪尔·莫尔斯（Samuel Morse）——他是阿尔的老同学，这次比赛的促成出了大力。

于是，拉里的目光落到大看台右边第一间包厢上时，正好接住了爱丽丝小姐回过来的微笑；正在包厢近旁打转的阿尔伯特·希顿朝卡塔帕队这位受宠的三垒手挥了挥手，压低嗓音说：「上吧，老伙计！」

「这么吵，博因先生才听不见呢，你总不至于这么想吧？」爱丽丝问，脸蛋红扑扑，眼睛亮晶晶。

===Original===
"No, I don't suppose that Larry heard or saw anything but what he saw and guessed at in that telegraphic look of yours, Miss Ally," replied Albert, mockingly. "Larry, the dear boy, knows well enough what I would be saying to him; and I hope he knows what you would be telegraphing him by way of encouragement. Hurrah! Hiram has won the toss! He'll send the Calumets to the bat, see if he don't."

Albert was right. The home club were sent to the bat, and Thomas Walsh, of the Black Hawks, took his place as umpire. This was the order in which the two clubs were named and stationed on that eventful day:—

Catalpas.
- **Larry Boyne**, 3rd B.
- **Samuel Morrison**, L.F.
- **Neddie Ellis**, C.F.
- **Charlie King**, P.
- **Hart Stirling**, 2nd B.
- **John Brubaker**, R.F.
- **Hiram Porter**, 1st B. (Capt.)
- **Ben Burton**, S.S.
- **Wm. Van Orman**, C.
Calumets.
- **Darius Ayres**, 1st B. (Capt.)
- **Samuel Morse**, P.
- **John Handy**, 3rd B.
- **Rob Peabody**, R.F.
- **Thomas Shoff**, C.F.
- **Glenn Otto**, S.S.
- **James Kennedy**, 2nd B.
- **Charlie Webb**, C.
- **James McWilliams**, L.F.

The Catalpa boys thought there should have been breathless silence in the enclosure as Hiram Porter, having carefully placed his men, called to the umpire "play!" Play was accordingly called, but there was silence, by no means, in the grounds. The clatter of late comers reaching their seats, the buzz of conversation that yet arose from the crowds in the amphitheater, and the cry of boys selling scorecards disturbed the serenity of the ardent champions of the Catalpa Nine. They wondered why people should talk when so momentous a game was about opening. And Alice, with a feverish sigh of impatience, said to Miss Ida that she should think that the Chicago people had very little manners. Whereupon Miss Anstress, with great severity, said that the spectators were not so much in love with the players that they cared a pin whether either side won. This unkind remark was turned aside by Mr. Heaton who said that there were not a few among the onlookers who had bet money in the gambling rooms outside and who did care very much which side won the game.

===Chinese===
「不，我看拉里什么也没听见、什么也没看见——除了你那道电报似的一瞥里看见和猜到的东西，爱丽丝小姐。」阿尔伯特揶揄地答道。「拉里这可爱的家伙，心里清楚得很我要跟他说什么；我也希望他知道，你想拍给他的鼓励电文写的是什么。好哇！海勒姆猜先赢了！他准会让卡柳梅特队先攻，不信走着瞧。」

阿尔伯特说对了。主队被派去先攻，黑鹰队的托马斯·沃尔什（Thomas Walsh）就位担任裁判。在那个重大的日子里，两家俱乐部依下面的顺序报号站位：

卡塔帕队。
- **拉里·博因（Larry Boyne）**，三垒手
- **萨缪尔·莫里森（Samuel Morrison）**，左野手
- **内迪·埃利斯（Neddie Ellis）**，中外野手
- **查利·金（Charlie King）**，投手
- **哈特·斯特林（Hart Stirling）**，二垒手
- **约翰·布鲁贝克（John Brubaker）**，右野手
- **海勒姆·波特（Hiram Porter）**，一垒手（队长）
- **本·伯顿（Ben Burton）**，游击手
- **威廉·范奥曼（Wm. Van Orman）**，捕手
卡柳梅特队。
- **达赖厄斯·艾尔斯（Darius Ayres）**，一垒手（队长）
- **萨缪尔·莫尔斯（Samuel Morse）**，投手
- **约翰·汉迪（John Handy）**，三垒手
- **罗布·皮博迪（Rob Peabody）**，右野手
- **托马斯·肖夫（Thomas Shoff）**，中外野手
- **格伦·奥托（Glenn Otto）**，游击手
- **詹姆斯·肯尼迪（James Kennedy）**，二垒手
- **查利·韦伯（Charlie Webb）**，捕手
- **詹姆斯·麦克威廉斯（James McWilliams）**，左野手

卡塔帕的小伙子们原以为，海勒姆·波特把队员仔细安顿停当、朝裁判喊出「开赛！」之后，场内理应鸦雀无声。开是开赛了，可场内绝非安静。迟到的人踩着噼里啪啦的脚步寻座位，圆形剧场里的人群仍嗡嗡地交谈，卖记分卡的孩子们叫卖不绝，搅扰着卡塔帕九人队这班热血冠军的心境。他们纳闷：这么要紧的一场比赛就要开场了，人们怎么还有话可说。爱丽丝焦躁地叹了口气，对艾达小姐说，照她看，芝加哥人真是没什么规矩。安斯特蕾丝小姐听了，板着脸说：看客们可没那么稀罕这些球员，哪边赢他们才懒得操心呢。这句不中听的话，被希顿先生岔开了——他说，围观的人里头，在场外赌房里押了钱的可不止一个两个，这场比赛谁输谁赢，他们是真上心的。

===Original===
All this talk was brought to an end when Darius Ayres, the captain of the Calumets, stood up at the bat and made ready for the first play. Darius was a tall and shapely young fellow, renowned for his long-field hits, and a swift runner. He had an evil look in his eyes, as some of the Catalpa visitors thought, and when he struck a straight ball, like a cannon shot, to right field, there was a little shudder in one of the private boxes. But John Brubaker, always alert, captured it on a hard run. This put the Catalpas in good spirits at once. The game had opened well for them. "Two good signs, Alice," said Ida Boardman. "Won the toss and caught out the first man!"

John's clever catch did not pass unnoticed, for the numerous supporters of the Catalpas raised a little cheer which was taken up and continued around the enclosure as Sam Morse went to the bat for the home club. But Samuel fared no better than his captain, and retired on a short and easy fly to Ben Burton. The first half of the inning was ended by John Handy, who hit a hot grounder to Larry Boyne at third base. Larry mastered it in fine style and made a lightning throw to Hiram Porter on first base. The eyes of the visitors and their friends fairly sparkled as the Catalpas came in from the field. They had made a good beginning.

But no sooner had the nine reached the players' bench than Ben Burton began to criticise the manner in which honest John Brubaker had been rewarded for capturing what Ben was pleased to call "a two-old-cat fly." Larry, politely requesting Burton to be civil, picked up his bat and faced the pitching of the renowned Sam Morse. He made two ineffectual plunges at the ball, and, while the catcher of the Calumets was adjusting his mask so as to enable him to come up closer to the player, Larry stole a glance at his comrades and was mortified and annoyed to see a derisive smile on the blonde face of Ben Burton, while the other seven occupants of the bench wore an uneasy expression. Ben Burton was evidently making them uncomfortable. Larry moistened his hands, and, carefully gauging one of Morse's favorite in-shoots, hit the ball with all his might. The flying sphere went swiftly into the left field and yielded the stalwart third baseman of the Catalpas two bases. Alice involuntarily clapped her hands, happily unmindful of the sour looks of her observant aunt.

===Chinese===
等到卡柳梅特队队长达赖厄斯·艾尔斯站上击球位、摆好架势准备第一击，这些闲话才算收场。达赖厄斯是个身材高挑匀称的年轻人，以长远打和跑垒飞快闻名。卡塔帕来的一些观众觉得，他眼里有一股凶相。他把一记直线球像炮弹一样打向右外野时，某间私人包厢里有人小小地哆嗦了一下。可是约翰·布鲁贝克素来眼明脚快，一阵猛跑把球接了下来。这一下卡塔帕队顿时来了精神。比赛开局对他们有利。「两个好兆头，爱丽丝，」艾达·博德曼说。「猜先赢了，头一名击球员又被接杀了！」

约翰这手漂亮的接杀没有白费：卡塔帕队的众多拥护者小小地欢呼了一阵；主队的萨姆·莫尔斯走上击球位时，欢呼声又沿着围栏转了一圈接了下去。可萨缪尔的运气不比他队长好，一记又短又软的高飞球，被本·伯顿接杀出局。上半局以约翰·汉迪收尾：他朝三垒的拉里·博因打出一记滚烫的地滚球。拉里漂亮地把球驯服，闪电般传给一垒的海勒姆·波特。卡塔帕队从场上下来时，家乡来客和他们朋友们的眼睛都亮了。他们开了个好头。

可九人队刚坐回球员席，本·伯顿就挑剔起老实的约翰·布鲁贝克得到的那份喝彩——接的不过是用本的话说「一记过家家似的高飞球」。拉里客客气气地请伯顿嘴下留德，然后拿起球棒，面对大名鼎鼎的萨姆·莫尔斯的投球。他两次挥棒都落了空；趁卡柳梅特队的捕手调整面罩、好往前逼近击球员的当口，拉里朝伙伴们溜了一眼，却看见本·伯顿那张金发的脸上挂着一丝讥笑，心里又羞又恼；球员席上其余七个人也都一脸不自在。本·伯顿显然让他们难受。拉里润了润手，仔细掂准莫尔斯一手招牌内曲球的路数，用尽全力击中球。飞球直奔左外野，为卡塔帕队这位健壮的三垒手赢得两个垒。爱丽丝不由自主地拍起手来，全然没在意旁边一直在观察她的姑妈那一脸难看的神色。

===Original===
Sam Morrison next stood up before the redoubtable Morse, and hit an easy grounder to Glenn Otto, at shortstop, and Samuel was retired at first base. His shot, however, advanced Larry to third base, and Neddie Ellis took up the bat. But Neddie could not yet understand the puzzling curves of the Calumets' pitcher, and, having wildly struck the air three times, went out. This made two out for the Catalpas, with Larry Boyne anxiously waiting on the third base. Not long did he wait, however, for Charlie King, long of limb and keen of eye, came to the bat with great expectations on the part of the sons of Catalpa. Charlie thought favorably of the first ball pitched at him by Morse and he sent it flying to the center field for one base, and allowed Larry to come home amidst a little round of applause from the Catalpa section of the spectators. During the cheer that greeted the successful play, Charlie attempted to steal to second base but was thrown out by Billy Webb, and the ardor of the spirits of Catalpa was consequently soon dampened.

The Calumets now went to work with a will at the beginning of their second inning, and, after receiving some hints from Jamie Kennedy, who assumed to know a little about the mysteries of King's curves, Robert Peabody, the Calumets' right fielder, a Michigan University man and a famous athlete, handled the bat and called for a low ball from the pitcher of the Catalpas. This was delivered, but not where Rob had asked for it, and he politely refused to strike at it, muttering to Captain Darius, "I won't strike until I get one just knee-high." Charlie King overheard this little byplay and continued to put the ball in the vicinity of Peabody's shoulder until the umpire called "six balls." It was now about time for King to give the Chicago player a good ball, but Peabody could not be tempted to strike at it, after being ordered by his captain to try and take his base on called balls. The result was that tricky Charlie King delivered three balls in rapid succession just where the dissatisfied right fielder of the Calumets had requested them, and the umpire called, "One strike!" "Two strikes!" "Three strikes!" "Striker out!"

The ashen stick was then taken up by Tom Shoff, who sent the ball in the direction of Ben Burton at shortstop, and who fumbled it, dropping it several times as if it were a hot potato, allowing Tom to reach first base in safety. Next, Glenn Otto hit a ball to Hiram Porter who fielded it handsomely, putting out the striker but allowing Shoff to go to second base. While Jamie Kennedy was at the bat, a passed ball allowed Shoff to complete three quarters of his homeward journey. With two out and a man on third base, Captain Porter naturally felt alarmed. He cautioned his men to be cool and careful, "especially cool," he added. After two strikes were called on Kennedy, he solved one of Charlie King's in-shoots and, to the delight of the Chicago onlookers, sent the ball rolling in center field while Shoff sped swiftly homewards; and the score stood 1 and 1. The Calumets' half of the inning was ended by the retiring of Webb on a foul tip to "The Lily," as Bill Van Orman was now universally called. The Catalpa boys were not disheartened; they had confidence in each other, and they went to work again with a determination to try and recover what they had lost. In the second inning, however, they found themselves unsuccessful. Hart Stirling was fielded out at first base by Jamie Kennedy; John Brubaker, following him, met with the same fate, being thrown out at first by Glenn Otto; and Hiram Porter ended the inning by hitting a skyscraper to James McWilliams at left field.

===Chinese===
萨姆·莫里森下一个站到令人生畏的莫尔斯面前，把一记软和的地滚球打向游击手格伦·奥托，萨缪尔在一垒被杀出局。不过他这一打把拉里送上了三垒，接着内迪·埃利斯接过球棒。可内迪还摸不透卡柳梅特队投手那些叫人发懵的弧线球，对着空气狠狠挥了三棒，出局了。卡塔帕队两人出局，拉里·博因在三垒上干着急。好在没等多久：查利·金——长手长脚、眼力尖利——踏上击球位，卡塔帕的儿子们对他寄予厚望。莫尔斯投来的第一个球，查利觉得有戏，把它送向中外野，安打一个垒，让拉里在看台上卡塔帕一角观众的阵阵掌声中回本垒得分。趁着这一记好球带来的欢呼，查利想盗二垒，却被比利·韦伯传杀出局，卡塔帕的这一腔热乎劲儿也随之凉了下去。

卡柳梅特队第二局一开局就铆足了劲。詹姆斯·肯尼迪——他自以为摸着了点金那些弧线球的门道——面授了几句机宜之后，卡柳梅特队的右野手罗伯特·皮博迪（密歇根大学出身，有名的运动健将）操起球棒，朝卡塔帕队的投手要了一个低球。球投来了，却不在罗布要的地方，他客客气气地不肯挥棒，嘴里对达赖厄斯队长咕哝说：「不到齐膝高的球我不打。」这话让查利·金听了去，便一味把球往皮博迪肩膀附近投，直到裁判喊「六个坏球」。这时候也该给这位芝加哥球员一个好球了，可皮博迪奉队长之命，想靠坏球保送上垒，怎么引诱也不肯挥棒。结果，狡猾的查利·金接连三球，恰恰投在这位不满意的右野手点名要的地方，裁判喊：「一击！」「两击！」「三击！」「击球员出局！」

接着操起白蜡木球棒的是汤姆·肖夫，他把球打向游击手本·伯顿的方向；本接球跌跌撞撞，掉了几回手，活像捧着个烫手的土豆，总算让汤姆安全踏上一垒。跟着，格伦·奥托把球打向海勒姆·波特；波特漂亮地接住传杀，封杀了击球员，却放肖夫上了二垒。轮到詹姆斯·肯尼迪击球时，一记捕手漏接的球让肖夫跑完了回本之路的四分之三。两人出局、三垒有人，波特队长自然着了慌。他叮嘱手下要冷静、要当心，「尤其要冷静」，他补了一句。肯尼迪被判两击之后，破解了查利·金的一记内曲球，让芝加哥的看客们乐开了花：球滚向中外野，肖夫飞奔回本；比分变成一比一。卡柳梅特队这半局以韦伯一记擦棒界外球被「百合」（The Lily）接杀告终——比尔·范奥曼如今得的这个人人都这么叫的诨名。卡塔帕的小伙子们并不气馁；他们彼此信得过，重新上场，打定主意要把丢掉的捞回来。然而第二局他们不顺：哈特·斯特林被詹姆斯·肯尼迪封杀于一垒；接着约翰·布鲁贝克蹈了覆辙，被格伦·奥托传杀于一垒；海勒姆·波特打出一记直上云霄的高飞球，被左外野的詹姆斯·麦克威廉斯接杀，这一局就此收场。

===Original===
There was intense depression in the Catalpa section and among the nine of that famous town; only the face of Larry Boyne still bore any semblance of contentment. Larry smiled with his attempt to infuse a little more hopefulness into the Catalpa bosom. And looking to the box where Mr. Heaton's tall white hat towered conspicuously, he caught an answering smile from the young lady who carried a blue parasol.

The score now stood even at even innings, and the faces of the Chicago players wore a broad smile of complacency in place of the gloomy look that had previously been their characteristic expression. Full of confidence, James McWilliams picked out his favorite bat and faced "Tricky Charlie," as they had already dubbed the pitcher of the visitors. King was determined to retire this particular player, as "Mac" had often expressed a desire to "take the conceit out of that chap from Catalpa." Charlie did some of his fine work for the occasion and his friend McWilliams threw down his bat in disgust, after hearing the third strike called by the umpire; and Captain Darius Ayres, with a look of vengeful determination, took the place vacated by his club mate. He hit a sharp grounder between first and second bases and reached the first bag. At this point of the game, the boys from Catalpa had lost some of the hope that they had cherished at the beginning of the contest; and they were not cheered in the least by a sarcastic smile that adorned the face of their shortstop, Ben Burton, who appeared to be almost glad that the chances of his own club were diminishing, instead of increasing.

Even from her distant point of vantage, Alice Howell, scanning Ben's sour face through her field glass, saw with uneasiness that forbidding look and said, in a tragic whisper to her companion, "Ida, if that scamp could throw the game, I believe he is mean enough to do it."

Sam Morse made a base hit to the right field, and Ayres went safely home to third base, while Morse stole to second base. With second and third bases occupied and but one man out, the Catalpas did not feel in jovial mood, and the deputy sheriff of Dean County looked around upon the bright faces of the local spectators with the air of one who is indignant at an outrage which he is powerless to abate.

The next man to the bat was John Handy, who had the reputation of being "a slugger," and as he called out in a stern voice, "Give me a low ball, and I'll knock it's cover off," some of the excitable players quaked in their shoes; but Hiram Porter quieted his men by saying, in a low tone of voice, "Keep cool, fellows! keep cool and we will double them up yet!" Handy hit the ball, the first that was delivered him, and it went like a rocket to Larry Boyne at third base. That young gentleman was ready to receive it, and by making a difficult one-hand catch, he succeeded in making a double play as Ayres had vacated third base without once dreaming that Larry would be able to capture the ball.

===Chinese===
那个著名小镇的看台一角和九人队里，一片愁云惨雾；只有拉里·博因的脸上还挂着几分自若的神色。拉里微笑着，想给卡塔帕人的胸中多注入一点希望。他朝希顿先生那顶高高的白帽子惹眼地矗立着的包厢望去，正好接住那位执天蓝色阳伞的小姐回过来的微笑。

局数打平，比分也平；芝加哥球员们脸上换上了一副洋洋自得的开阔笑容，一扫先前那种阴沉沉的惯常表情。詹姆斯·麦克威廉斯信心十足地挑出他那只心爱的球棒，面对「狡猾的查利」——他们已经给这位客队投手起了这个诨名。金铁了心要让这个对手下场，因为「麦克」常放话，要「煞一煞那个卡塔帕小子的威风」。查利为这个场合使出了几手绝活，他的朋友麦克威廉斯听完裁判喊出的第三击，厌恶地把球棒一摔；达赖厄斯·艾尔斯队长带着一脸复仇似的决绝，补上了同队队友让出的空位。他打出一记又快又刁的地滚球，穿过一垒二垒之间，占上了第一个垒包。比赛打到这个节骨眼，卡塔帕来的小伙子们开赛时揣着的那点希望已经丢了几分；而他们游击手本·伯顿脸上那丝挂着讥诮的微笑，更让他们心里没得到半点宽慰——本看着自己俱乐部赢球的希望越打越渺茫，非但不急，倒几乎像是高兴。

隔着老远的包厢，爱丽丝·豪厄尔用观剧镜细看本那张阴沉的脸，也忐忑地瞧见了那副不祥的神色；她凑近同伴，用悲壮的耳语说：「艾达，要是那个坏蛋能把这场球卖掉，我相信他真下作得干得出来。」

萨姆·莫尔斯朝右外野打出一记安打，艾尔斯安全回到三垒，莫尔斯又盗上二垒。二垒三垒都有人，只出局了一个，卡塔帕队实在乐不起来；迪安县的副治安官环顾四周本地看客的一张张喜气脸庞，那神情活像一个人对着自己无力制止的暴行义愤填膺。

下一个上场击球的是约翰·汉迪，素有「重炮手」的名声；他粗声粗气地喊了一嗓子：「给我个低球，我把它的皮都打飞！」几个沉不住气的球员腿肚子直转筋；海勒姆·波特低声稳住大家：「沉住气，伙计们！沉住气，咱们早晚双杀他们！」汉迪出手了，投给他的第一个球就被他打中，球像火箭一样直奔三垒的拉里·博因。这位年轻先生早有准备，单手完成一个高难度的接杀，硬是做成了一次双杀——艾尔斯压根儿没想到拉里能接住球，早撇下三垒跑了。

===Original===
Ben Burton came now to the bat for the Catalpas, in this inning; but Ben had not established a very good reputation as a batsman, and his speedy retiring on a foul ball excited no remark. "The Lily" took his place at the bat and at once gave evidence of his prowess by hitting the ball for two bases which he made with neatness and despatch. Larry Boyne followed him and gently tipped the sphere for a single base-hit, without ado, whereat "The Lily" slipped to third base. The spectators eyed Sam Morrison as he swung his bat over his shoulder and strode to the home plate. Sam was a stocky, well-built young fellow, with a well-shaped head and shoulders, and a fine pair of very long arms. He was anxious to do something to send up the score of the Catalpas, but he sent up nothing but a small fly to Morse, and he was at once succeeded by Neddie Ellis, the rather diminutive center fielder of the Catalpa Nine. Neddie owed the club three base hits, as he thought, and was falling behind in his batting record as the season had advanced. He moistened his hands and, with the avowed intention of losing the ball, he made a plunge, and, as Al Heaton from his perch remarked, "hit the ball on the nose" and sent it flying over the center fielder's head. After Larry and "The Lily" had cleared the home plate, Neddie tried his best to make a home run. Tommy Shoff, however, handled the ball in clever fashion, and by fielding it quickly, caught Neddie at the home plate, ending the inning and making the score three to one in favor of the Catalpas.

A murmur of applause, mingled with the little buzz which always follows the close of an inning, like a sigh of relief, went around as the Catalpas went to the field with light hearts. Two or three of the baser sort of the gambling onlookers jeered the visitors with derisive remarks, but this indiscretion was speedily suppressed. "Fair play for the visitors" was the watchword of the day. The Catalpa boys disposed of their opponents at the opening of the fourth inning without allowing them to send a man around the circuit. In fact, not a player of the Calumet club reached first base in safety during this inning. Rob Peabody secured first base on called balls, and was followed at the bat by Shoff who hit a grounder to Hart Stirling, at second base, and who delivered the ball in fine style to his captain on first base, after making a neat pickup. Glenn Otto managed, by great craftiness, to send the ball outside of the diamond with tremendous force, but he lifted it too high and he fell a victim to Sam Morrison's alertness in the left field.

Jamie Kennedy, who succeeded at the bat, also gave the ball a tremendous whack, but he, too, lifted it too high, and Neddie Ellis, in center field, captured it without serious difficulty. The Catalpa club, in this inning, was obliged to be contented with a zero, and Ben Burton's face was a puzzling study to Alice Howell and her friend Ida, who scanned the unconscious Benjamin through their glass, as if his telltale countenance were an indicator of the progress of the game. This time, they could not make out whether the Catalpa shortstop was pleased by the ill fortune of his own club, or dismayed by the advancing prospects of the Chicago boys. They gave up the riddle with disgust.

There was yet no real occasion for dismay, although there was when Charlie King began the work of going out by hitting a slow ball to Darius Ayres at first base, and Hart Stirling followed his example by a foul tip to Charlie Webb. John Brubaker, "Honest John," as he was called, hit the ball with all his might and had covered half the circuit before he realized that the sphere had gone outside of the foul flags. He made a second attempt, however, and was retired without hitting the ball, Sam Morse's out-curves being more intricate than anything that he had yet encountered. Honest John's inglorious withdrawal closed the inning.

===Chinese===
这半局轮到本·伯顿为卡塔帕队执棒；可本作为击球员名声本就不佳，一记界外球很快出局，没人多说什么。「百合」接着上场，立刻亮了一手本事：一记两垒安打，跑得干净利落、毫不含糊。拉里·博因跟在他后面，轻轻一碰，就是一支一垒安打，没有丝毫拖泥带水，「百合」趁机溜上三垒。看客们盯着萨姆·莫里森把球棒抡上肩头、大步走向本垒板。萨姆是个敦实健壮的年轻人，头肩周正，一双手臂出奇地长。他一心想为卡塔帕队的比分添上一笔，却只打出一记小小的高飞球给莫尔斯，随即出局；接替他的是内迪·埃利斯——卡塔帕九人队里个头偏小的中外野手。内迪琢磨着，自己还欠俱乐部三支安打，随着赛季推进，击球成绩越落越后。他润了润手，放出话来要把那球打没了影，猛地一挥——用阿尔·希顿在他高座上的话说，「正正打在球鼻子上」——球飞过中外野手的头顶。拉里和「百合」先后踏过本垒之后，内迪拼了命想再来个本垒打。可惜汤米·肖夫把球处理得干净漂亮，快手一传，在本垒把内迪杀了出去。这一局结束，比分三比一，卡塔帕队领先。

卡塔帕队轻快地跑向各自位置时，一阵低低的喝彩，夹着每局收尾常有的那种小嗡嗡声——像如释重负的一声叹息——绕场响了一圈。三两个赌棍一流的下等看客朝客队讥嘲了几句，不过这股轻率劲儿很快被压了下去。「给客队公平机会」是这一天的口号。第四局一开局，卡塔帕的小伙子们就让对手一分未得、无人绕场。实际上，这一局卡柳梅特俱乐部没有一个球员安全踏上一垒。罗布·皮博迪靠坏球保送上一垒；接着击球的肖夫把地滚球打向二垒的哈特·斯特林，斯特林干净地捡起，漂亮地传给一垒上的队长。格伦·奥托凭着一手十足的狡猾，把球用猛力打出内场界外，可他打得太高，成了左外野萨姆·莫里森警觉性下的牺牲品。

接替击球的詹姆斯·肯尼迪也把球狠狠抡了一记，可他也打得太高，中外野的内迪·埃利斯没费什么劲就接杀了。卡塔帕俱乐部这一局只落得个零蛋；本·伯顿的脸成了爱丽丝·豪厄尔和她朋友艾达琢磨不透的一门学问：她们用观剧镜细细端详这位浑然不觉的本杰明，仿佛他那张藏不住事的脸就是比赛进程的指示器。这一回，她们实在分辨不出：这位卡塔帕的游击手到底是为自己俱乐部的不幸暗自得意，还是为芝加哥小伙子们步步得势而黯然神伤。她们腻烦地丢开了这个谜。

其实还没有真正值得惊慌的理由——虽说查利·金开局便把一记慢球送到一垒的达赖厄斯·艾尔斯手里自寻出局、哈特·斯特林又学他的样、一记擦棒界外球送给查利·韦伯的时候，确实叫人慌了一阵。约翰·布鲁贝克——人称「老实的约翰」——使出全身力气击球，等他明白球早飞出了界外旗之外，已经跑完了大半圈垒。他又试了第二回，这回干脆没碰着球就出局了——萨姆·莫尔斯的外曲球，比他至今领教过的任何球路都更刁钻。老实的约翰灰溜溜地下场，结束了这一局。

===Original===
The Calumets sent Webb first to the bat at the opening of the next inning, but Charlie was not fortunate. He hit the ball several times, and it went high in air, and escaped the vigilance of the Catalpas. But Webb sent up one foul too many and the watchful and agile Larry Boyne captured it, after a hard run. James McWilliams for the second time faced Charlie King's pitching, and as he left his seat, said, "Boys, I'll eat clover for a week if I don't hit him safely this time." Mac had fire in his eye, and his look and his remark did not escape the attention of Charlie King, who, turning to his captain, slyly promised to give the Chicago man an opportunity to make good his promise. King kept his word, and, by cunning pitching, retired McWilliams on strikes after six balls were charged against him.

Captain Darius Ayres hit safely to the left field, but it was too late, as Sam Morse ruined all chances of the scoring of the Calumets by sending a fly which was neatly caught by Hart Stirling at second base. The Catalpas also failed to add any runs to their score in the fifth inning. At this point, Sam Morse was pitching in admirable style and it was with difficulty that the visitors could hit the ball at all. Morse had a very effectual out-curve, and he had made good use of it during the last two innings.

Captain Hiram Porter went to the bat with some of the confidence that he had tried to inspire in the breasts of his comrades, but he failed to accomplish his dearest desire, and went out on the strikes successively called by the umpire. He was followed by Ben Burton, who walked up to the batsman's position with a lazy and indifferent manner, hit the ball in an offhand fashion, and had the pleasure of seeing it fielded by Glenn Otto, and was retired at first base. Here "The Lily" made a desperate attempt to achieve a home run, and he probably would have been successful if he had hit the ball far enough into the outfield, judging from the manner in which he sprinted to first base on a slow ball which was readily fielded by Jamie Kennedy.

===Chinese===
下一局一开局，卡柳梅特队派韦伯第一个击球，可查利运气不佳。他把球打了一次又一次，球高高飞起，从卡塔帕队的眼皮底下逃了过去。可韦伯放出的界外球终究多了一个，警觉敏捷的拉里·博因一阵猛跑，把它接了下来。詹姆斯·麦克威廉斯第二回面对查利·金的投球，离座时说：「伙计们，这回我要是打不出安打，就吃一个礼拜的草料。」麦克眼里冒着火；他的神气和这话都没逃过查利·金的眼睛。金转身朝队长狡黠地一诺，答应给这位芝加哥人一个兑现诺言的机会。金说到做到，凭着刁钻的投球，在六个坏球之后，把麦克威廉斯三振出局。

达赖厄斯·艾尔斯队长打出左外野方向的安打，可惜晚了——萨姆·莫尔斯一记高飞球，被二垒的哈特·斯特林利落接杀，断送了卡柳梅特队得分的一切机会。第五局卡塔帕队也没能往比分上添分。打到这个当口，萨姆·莫尔斯投得漂亮至极，客队想碰到球都难。莫尔斯有一手极有效的外曲球，前两局里使得得心应手。

海勒姆·波特队长带着他曾努力注入伙伴胸中的那几分信心走上击球位，却没能如愿，被裁判接连喊出的三击判罚出局。接着是本·伯顿，他懒洋洋、心不在焉地踱到击球位，随随便便一挥棒，很有幸地看着球被格伦·奥托接住传杀，出局于一垒。这里「百合」豁出去拼一个本垒打；从他冲向一垒的那副架势看——不过一记慢球，詹姆斯·肯尼迪轻巧地接住传杀——要是他把球打得够远、送进外野深处，说不定还真成了。

===Original===
"This is our lucky inning," said Captain Ayres to John Handy, as the latter started to face the pitching of Charlie King in the sixth inning. "Here, take my bat for luck," he added, "and see if you can't use it to advantage." Handy accepted the offer of the captain's club and used it with good effect. He called for a high ball, caught King off his guard as he struck, and so secured a good hit on the very first ball, and made first base. Rob Peabody followed and hit a liner to Neddie Ellis who misjudged the distance, and the ball went over his head and allowed Rob to make two bases, while Handy got safely home. This put the figures three to two in favor of the Catalpas and seemed to inspire the Calumets with new confidence, their captain remarking with glee, "I told you this was our lucky inning."

Right here, however, Tommy Shoff went out on a fly to Larry Boyne, and "The Lily" caught a sharp foul tip from the bat of Glenn Otto, which left Peabody on second base and two men out. The prospects of the home nine were not brightening.

Next to the bat came Jamie Kennedy, who tried his best to make a short right field hit that should send his colleague safely home, as Peabody was a good base runner and needed only "half a chance" to make a home run. Jamie hit the ball in the right direction, but his blow was a trifle too hard and the ball was cleverly caught by John Brubaker at right field, and this left the game still three to two in favor of the Catalpas. The latter did not, however, feel safe with so small a lead, and they thought it prudent to send several more men around the circuit of the bases, if possible. Larry Boyne was the first man to the bat for the Catalpas in the sixth inning, and he secured his base on called balls, but fell before Charlie Webb's throwing, while trying to steal to the second bag. Sam Morrison struck out, and Neddie Ellis ended the inning by sending up a skyscraper which was nicely nipped, just in the nick of time, apparently, by Rob Peabody.

===Chinese===
「这一局是咱们的幸运局，」第六局约翰·汉迪起身去面对查利·金的投球时，艾尔斯队长对他说。「来，拿我的棒子讨个彩头，」他补了一句，「看看你能不能拿它打出个名堂。」汉迪接过队长的球棒，用出了好效果。他喊了要高球，趁金不备挥棒，头一个球就打出一记好安打，占上一垒。罗布·皮博迪跟上来，打出一记平直球给内迪·埃利斯；内迪判断错了距离，球飞过他的头顶，让罗布上了两个垒，汉迪也安全回本。比分变成卡塔帕队三比二领先；这似乎给卡柳梅特队注入了新的信心，他们的队长喜滋滋地说：「我说过这一局是咱们的幸运局吧。」

可就在这儿，汤米·肖夫一记高飞球出局给了拉里·博因，「百合」又把格伦·奥托棒下尖厉的擦棒球接进手套，皮博迪留在了二垒，两人出局。主队九人的前景并未明朗起来。

下一个轮到詹姆斯·肯尼迪，他竭力想打出一记右外野的短打，好把同伴稳稳送回本——皮博迪跑垒是把好手，只需「半个机会」就能完成本垒打。杰米打的方位是对的，可惜劲儿稍大了点，球被右外野的约翰·布鲁贝克机敏地接住；比分仍是卡塔帕队三比二领先。不过，领先这么一分，卡塔帕队觉得并不保险，寻思着最好再送几个人绕场跑一圈，如果办得到的话。第六局卡塔帕队第一个击球的是拉里·博因，他靠坏球保送上垒，可盗二垒时栽在查利·韦伯的传杀之下。萨姆·莫里森三击出局，内迪·埃利斯打出一记直上云霄的高飞球，被罗布·皮博迪接杀——看那情形千钧一发，偏偏掐得正是时候——这一局到此为止。

===Original===
In the seventh inning, both clubs failed to score. Webb hit a ball in the direction of Ben Burton who made an overthrow to first base. McWilliams followed and hit a short one to Hart Stirling at second base, who, with the aid of Hiram Porter, made a very pretty double play. Darius Ayres secured his base on called balls, stole to second base, but was left there, as Sam Morse retired on strikes. Not one of the Catalpa players reached first base. Charlie King and Hart Stirling both went out on flies, the former to Tom Shoff and the latter to Glenn Otto. John Brubaker failed to hit the ball and was consequently called out on strikes.

"The Calumets have everything to gain and nothing to lose," remarked Mr. Heaton, sagely, as he regarded the field from the box from which the little party of interested Catalpans overlooked the beautiful scene below. The yellow sun, now declining westward, tinted the woodwork of the stands and enclosures with a golden hue, and a breeze from the lake flaunted the many-colored flags that adorned the structure. The yellow light only intensified the brilliant greenness of the lawn, on which the Diamond Field was laid, and the brilliant costumes of the players were tricked out with a new and strange luster as the sunshine rained down through veiling mists. But the absorbed spectators, as well as the intensely engrossed players in the field below, had no eyes for the picture. Every eye was fixed on John Handy, as he went to the bat for the Calumets. It was felt that they would take desperate chances. On the next few plays might turn the issue of the game. Silence as complete as if there was not a soul in the vast enclosure reigned as Handy took his place at the bat.

He placed the ball safely in the center field and was followed by Peabody who also gained a single hit, sending the ball into the left field. The next ball was hit to Ben Burton by Shoff. Ben was unable to handle the ball properly, and Hart Stirling came to his rescue and as Ben dropped it out of his hands, Stirling picked it up and sent it to first base in time to head off Shoff. At this point in the game, only one man was out and the second and third bases were occupied. A trifling error would tie the game. A single base hit would give the Calumets the lead. The attention with which the play was now regarded from the seats was something almost painful in its tenseness.

Glenn Otto stood before Charlie King's pitching with a look of resolution and defiance. He had been ordered not to strike at a ball until it was put where he asked for it, and to take the chances of the catcher of the Catalpas having a passed ball charged to him. In this little scheme there was one error. King very well knew the purpose of his opponent, and he managed his own points so well that, before Otto could realize what was about to happen, King had him out on strikes.

Jamie Kennedy was the next man to fall before the destructive tactics now followed by the Catalpas. Jamie hit a sharp ball to Larry Boyne, who, with characteristic skill, retired him at first base. This clever bit of play took a load from the hearts of the Catalpas, and, in the excitement of the moment, Deputy Sheriff Wheeler ejaculated "Gosh all hemlock!" whereupon everybody in that region laughed, as if glad of a pretext to slacken their attention from the play for an instant.

===Chinese===
第七局，双方都没得分。韦伯把球打向本·伯顿方向，本传一垒传过了头。麦克威廉斯接着把一个短打球送给二垒的哈特·斯特林；斯特林在海勒姆·波特配合下，完成了一次非常漂亮的双杀。达赖厄斯·艾尔斯靠坏球保送上垒，盗上二垒，却被晾在了那里——萨姆·莫尔斯三击出局。卡塔帕这边，没有一个人踏上一垒：查利·金和哈特·斯特林双双高飞球出局，前者送给了汤姆·肖夫，后者送给了格伦·奥托。约翰·布鲁贝克没能打着球，被三振出局。

「卡柳梅特队赢得起也输得起，」希顿先生从包厢里眺望球场，老成地评论道；一小群兴致勃勃的卡塔帕人正是从这间包厢俯瞰着下面美丽的景致。西斜的太阳给看台和围栏的木架镀上一层金黄，湖上来的风把装点场子的彩旗吹得猎猎招展。金黄的光反倒把草坪衬得愈发碧绿——棒球内场就铺在这片草坪上；阳光透过薄纱般的雾霭洒落下来，球员们鲜艳的球衣仿佛披上了一层新奇的光泽。可是如痴如醉的看客们，连同场下全神贯注的球员，谁也没心思看这幅画。所有的眼睛都盯着走上击球位的约翰·汉迪——卡柳梅特队这回要孤注一掷了。往后几个球，可能就定掉这场比赛的胜负。汉迪站上击球位时，庞大的场地里静得像空无一人。

他把球稳稳送进中外野；接着皮博迪也安打一支，球落在左外野。下一球，肖夫打向本·伯顿。本接不稳，哈特·斯特林赶去救场；本手一滑球掉了，斯特林捡起来传向一垒，赶在肖夫之前封杀了垒包。打到这个节骨眼，只出局一人，二垒三垒都有人。一个小失误就能扳平比分，一支一垒安打就能让卡柳梅特队反超。看台上人们看球的专注劲儿，紧张得近乎痛苦。

格伦·奥托带着一脸决绝与挑衅站到查利·金面前。他奉了指令：球不投到点名的地方绝不出手，就赌卡塔帕队的捕手吃一个漏接球。这条小算盘有一个漏洞。金对他的对手打什么主意一清二楚，把自己的球路调度得天衣无缝；奥托还没明白过来会发生什么，金已经把他三振出局。

詹姆斯·肯尼迪是下一个倒在卡塔帕队这套杀伤战术面前的人。杰米把一记又快又硬的球打向拉里·博因，拉里以他标志性的身手把他传杀于一垒。这一手妙球卸下了卡塔帕人心头的一块大石；兴头上，惠勒副治安官脱口一声「我的老天爷！」惹得那一带人人哄笑，像是巴不得有个由头，好把注意力从比赛中松开那么一瞬。

===Original===
But the riveted intentness of the spectators was at once resumed as the boys of Catalpa went to the bat in the eighth inning, and succeeded in placing another run to their credit. Hiram Porter hit to Kennedy at second base, and was retired at first base. Ben Burton followed his example and "The Lily" finally secured the home run which he had been looking for ever since he had left Catalpa. "The Lily" had many strong points, but base-running was not one of them. He had two strikes called on the first two balls pitched, and then made ready for the third, and, as the ball curved in, he stepped backwards a few inches and hit it with all his might, which was a great deal, for "The Lily" was a man of brawn and muscle. The ball flew over the center fielder's head like a rifle-shot and Bill covered the entire circuit with ease, winning an irrepressible and resounding burst of applause from the multitudes that crowded the amphitheater.

"Splendid, Bill! perfectly splendid!" cried Alice Howell, wholly oblivious of the fact that there were other people than herself in the circle about her. Mr. Heaton looked around with admiration at the impulsive girl, while the dignified maiden aunt glanced into the next box to see if anybody had caught the words of her erratic ward and niece. While this little byplay went on, Alice's eyes were fixed on Larry Boyne who ended the eighth inning by sending a fly ball to McWilliams and so going out.

The score now stood four to two in favor of the Catalpas. To his infinite chagrin, Captain Ayres saw defeat staring him in the face. Hastily calling his men about him, he held a hurried consultation, as they came in from the field. He said,

"Boys, we must take all the chances this time. They lead us two runs, and, in order at least to tie them, you must trust to errors, and, above all things, do not hug the bases."

Captain Darius was right in this particular, and the men obeyed his instructions to the letter in regard to hugging the bases; but it was impossible for them to show any sign of insubordination, as not a man went beyond the first base. Every member of the Calumet club was retired as fast as he went to the bat. Charlie Webb gayly faced "tricky Charlie," and hit the first ball pitched. It went sailing out of the Diamond and into the hands of Sam Morrison. The second victim was McWilliams who failed to take down the pride of King, as he had promised himself that he would; and Charlie felt prouder than ever as he sent his formidable antagonist to the players' bench, put out on strikes.

===Chinese===
可卡塔帕的小伙子们第八局一上场，看客们凝成一团的注意力立刻又钉了回去；他们又往账上添了一分。海勒姆·波特把球打向二垒的肯尼迪，被传杀于一垒。本·伯顿照样出局，随后「百合」终于拿到了他自离开卡塔帕以来朝思暮想的那个本垒打。「百合」优点很多，跑垒却不在其列。头两个球被判了两击，他摆好架势等第三个；球一拐进来，他朝后挪了几英寸，用尽全身力气迎球一击——那可不是小数，「百合」是个筋骨结实的汉子。球像步枪子弹一样飞过中外野手的头顶，比尔轻松跑完全程，赢得了挤满圆形剧场的人群一阵抑制不住、震耳欲聋的欢呼。

「太棒了，比尔！棒极了！」爱丽丝·豪厄尔喊道，浑然忘了身边这一圈还有别人。希顿先生又惊又赞地环顾这位率性的姑娘；端庄的姑妈却朝隔壁包厢里瞟，看看有没有人听见她这位行事没谱的受监护人兼侄女说的话。这段小小的插曲过去时，爱丽丝的眼睛一直盯着拉里·博因；他把一记高飞球送给麦克威廉斯，就此出局，第八局结束。

现在比分四比二，卡塔帕队领先。眼睁睁看着失败逼近，艾尔斯队长懊丧到了极点。他赶紧把队员叫到身边，趁他们从场上下来，匆匆合计了一番。他说：

「伙计们，这回咱们得把所有机会都押上了。他们领先咱们两分；要想至少扳平，你们就得指望对手失误，还有，最要紧的一条——千万别死抱着垒不放。」

达赖厄斯队长这话说到了点子上；说到抱垒，手下也一字不差地照办了——只可惜他们连违令的机会都没有，因为没有一个人跑过了一垒。卡柳梅特俱乐部的每一名队员，上来一个杀一个。查利·韦伯轻快地面对「狡猾的查利」，把头一个投来的球打了出去。球飞出内场，落进萨姆·莫里森手里。第二个牺牲者是麦克威廉斯，他没能像自己暗许的那样压下金的傲气；查利把这位可怕的对手三振回球员席，比先前更得意了。

===Original===
Darius Ayres made several ineffectual attempts to hit the sphere, and at last struck the ball fairly, but Larry Boyne was prepared for its coming his way. Running backwards, with his eye fixed on the little black speck that dropped out of the clouds with lightning-like swiftness, Larry moved over the turf without seeming to move. Ida Boardman so far forgot herself as to cry out, at this critical juncture, "Catch it! catch it!" The sphere fell into Larry's hardened hand with a resounding thud, and with a fervent "Heaven bless you!" the young lady sunk back into her seat, while a prodigious cheer, frightening to flight the sparrows that twittered on the edges of the structure, and faintly heard far out by sailors on the lake, proclaimed the contest ended with a famous victory for the Catalpa Nine.

The band broke forth into a paean of triumph, and while the majority of the spectators began to shuffle out with eager haste, a few, other than the delighted visitors from Catalpa, remained to gaze with undisguised admiration on the stalwart and handsome young fellows who had so unexpectedly won the day.

The two captains, as the game was concluded, advanced towards each other with outstretched hands.

"Your men are capital players," said Hiram Porter, a glow suffusing his cheek, "and I consider it a great honor to have defeated them."

"Aye, aye," said Captain Ayres, not without a wince. "It is a little hard for our boys to be defeated after playing a game without errors; but your victory was due to lucky batting, and it does not signify that your men are the better players. We will try and turn the tables tomorrow."

The visitors gave three cheers and a tiger for their opponents, and then retired from the field. It would be useless to attempt to describe the thrill and the suppressed exultation with which they read on the bulletin boards of the city newspaper offices, as they went to their lodgings, the following score:—

===Chinese===
达赖厄斯·艾尔斯几次挥棒都落了空，最后一记打了个正着，可拉里·博因早防着他这一路。眼睛盯着那粒从云端闪电般坠落的小黑点，拉里倒退着跑，人贴着草皮滑行，仿佛没动似的。在这千钧一发之际，艾达·博德曼忘乎所以地喊了出来：「接住它！接住它！」球砰的一声落进拉里那只磨出茧子的手掌；姑娘热切地念了句「上天保佑你！」便瘫回座位里。山呼海啸般的欢呼，吓得在场子边沿啁啾的麻雀四散惊飞，远得连湖上的水手都隐约听见——它宣告：比赛结束，卡塔帕九人队赢下了一场著名的胜利。

乐队奏起凯歌；多数看客急着起身离场，但也有几个人——不止是喜不自胜的卡塔帕来客——留了下来，毫不掩饰地欣赏这班出人意料旗开得胜的健美少年。

比赛结束，两位队长朝对方伸出手，迎面走去。

「你们队员都是一流的球员，」海勒姆·波特说，双颊泛起红光，「能战胜他们，我视作莫大的荣幸。」

「是啊，是啊，」艾尔斯队长说，不无痛楚。「我们的孩子们打了一场零失误的球却输了，是有点难堪；不过你们的胜利靠的是击球的手气，并不说明你们的人球技更高。明天咱们想法子翻盘。」

客队朝对手三呼万岁，又加一声虎吼，然后退场。他们回住处的路上，在市内各报馆的公告板上读到下面这份比分时，那种激动和强压着的欢腾，就不必多费笔墨去描摹了：
