## IV Reorganization Begins / 第四章 重组开始了

===Original===
“Where now, Larry?” asked ’Squire Mead, meeting Larry Boyne, on Stone River bridge, one wintry day in November. Cold weather had set in early, and huge cakes of ice had already formed on the edge of the dam, and a light fall of snow gave promise of sleighing for Thanksgiving week, then not far off. Larry was mounted on a sorry-looking nag, borrowed from a Sugar Grove neighbor, and he carried behind him a big bundle of knitted mittens, the handiwork of his mother and sisters, to be exchanged for goods at one of the stores in town.

“Oh, I’m just going to town to trade a bit, and I have a message from Al Heaton that he and his father want to see me about joining a new baseball club to be gotten up here. Know anything about it, ’Squire?”

“Well, yes,” replied the ’Squire, “I’m told that there is something of a stir in town about the matter.” The crafty old lawyer did not say how much the stir was indebted to him for its existence. “Quite a stir, Larry, and they do say that they will get up a new nine; even if they have to hire players to go into it.”

===Chinese===
“上哪儿去呀，拉里？”十一月一个冬日，米德老爷（'Squire Mead）在斯通河桥上遇见拉里·博因，问道。寒冬来得早，坝边已经结出大块大块的冰，一场小雪预示着感恩节那个星期有雪橇可坐——感恩节已经不远了。拉里骑着一匹向舒格格罗夫的邻居借来的、模样寒碜的老马，马背上驮着一大包织好的连指手套，是他母亲和姐妹们的手艺，要拿到镇上一家铺子去换货。

“哦，我就是进城做点小买卖。阿尔·希顿捎了话，说他和他的父亲想见见我，商量加入一个要在这儿组建的新棒球俱乐部的事。这事您听说什么了吗，老爷？”

“嗯，听说了点，”老爷答道，“我听说镇上为这事闹腾得不轻。”这位精明的老律师没说这场热闹有多少是他一手操办出来的。“闹腾得不轻啊，拉里，人们都说了，他们要组建一支新九人队，哪怕得雇球员进来也在所不惜。”

===Original===
Larry’s cheeks flushed even deeper red as he replied, “There is no disgrace in hiring players to help out, I suppose, ’Squire? I was paid a share of the gate money while I was with the Jonesville Nine, and they have offered me a regular salary if I go with them next season. But I wouldn’t touch a penny of it if I thought it was the least bit off-color for a fellow to take pay for his services.”

“No, no,” said the ’Squire, warmly, “there is nothing in that that an honorable and high-toned young fellow like you are could object to; and if I were you, I would make the very best terms I could for next year. You have been obliged to give up studying law, I hear, on account of the death of your father. If you do well in the ball-field, next summer, you might save up enough to set you right next year, so far as studying is concerned. And, between you and me and the gatepost, Al Heaton and his father are bound to have you in the new nine. So make as good a bargain for yourself as you can. Al can’t play next season.”

“Why, what is the matter with Al? Why can’t he play any more?”

===Chinese===
拉里的脸颊涨得更红了，答道：“雇球员帮忙，我想这不算什么丢人的事吧，老爷？我在琼斯维尔九人队那阵子就拿过一份门票收入，他们还提出，下个赛季我要是跟着他们干，就给我一份正式薪水。可我要是觉得小伙子凭力气换钱有半点儿不体面，我一个子儿也不会碰。”

“不丢人，不丢人，”老爷热情地说，“像你这样正派体面的年轻人，对这事没什么可挑的；我要是你，就给明年争个最好的价钱。听说你父亲去世，你只好放弃了读法律。要是明年夏天你在球场上干得漂亮，攒下的钱没准够你明年把学业续上。还有一句，天知地知你知我知：阿尔·希顿爷儿俩铁了心要你进新九人队。所以，你能争多好的条件就争多好的条件。阿尔下个赛季打不成了。”

“咦，阿尔怎么了？他为什么打不成了？”

===Original===
“It’s mighty cold standing here talking on the bridge, Larry, and I don’t know that I have any right to give Al’s reasons, but I have a notion that his mother objects to his going around the country playing baseball. She’s got high and mighty airs since her Uncle George was elected to Congress from the Sangamon District, and I reckon that that is what is the matter with Al’s baseball business. Pity ’tis, too, for Al is a first-rate catcher. Nobody like him, unless it is Larry Boyne,” he added with a kindly smile.

Larry thanked the ’Squire, and, with a hearty “goodbye,” went thoughtfully on his way across the bridge. As his steed climbed Bridge Street, Larry was conscious that he had several new ideas in his head. And when, his little errands done, he found his way to Mr. Heaton’s counting-room in the mills near the dam, he had made up his mind that Jonesville had no claim on him and that he belonged no more to Jonesville than he did to Catalpa. In other words, he was in the market for employment. The mortgage on the farm must be paid off; his sisters and the little brother must be kept at school, and he had his own way to make in the world. To take one season’s compensation as a baseball player would help matters at home very much. It was a gleam of hope in an otherwise gloomy outlook for the young man.

“Glad to see you, Larry,” said Mr. Heaton, heartily. “Al’s been waiting for you this some time, and we may as well go right to business. The boys are talking of getting up a first-class nine, and as my son cannot very well go into it, next year, he has coaxed me to turn in and help the others. And so I will, for I want to see old Catalpa come out ahead at the end of the season.”

===Chinese===
“站在桥上说话可真够冷的，拉里。阿尔的缘由，我未必有资格往外说，不过我琢磨着，是他母亲反对他满乡跑着打棒球。自打她那位乔治伯伯从桑加蒙选区（Sangamon District）当选了国会议员，她就摆起高门大户的谱儿来了，我估摸着，阿尔棒球这档子事坏就坏在这儿。可惜呀，阿尔可是个一流的捕手。除了拉里·博因，没人比得上他。”他补了一句，慈祥地笑着。

拉里谢过老爷，道了一声热诚的“再见”，一路思忖着过了桥。他的坐骑爬上桥头街时，拉里自觉脑子里多了好几个新念头。等他把几件小事办完，寻到坝边磨坊里希顿先生的账房时，他已经打定了主意：琼斯维尔对他没有任何权利，他不属于琼斯维尔，正如他不属于卡塔帕一样。换句话说，他正待价而沽。农场上的抵押借款必须还清；姐妹们和小弟弟必须留在学校念书；他也得自己闯出一条路。当一季职业棒球手，拿一季报酬，家里的日子就会好过许多。对这个年轻人来说，这是一片灰暗前景中的一线希望。

“见到你真高兴，拉里，”希顿先生热诚地说。“阿尔等你有些时候了，咱们不妨直接谈正事。小伙子们正商量组建一支一流的九人队；我儿子明年不便入队，便磨着我出手帮帮大伙儿。我愿意帮，因为我想看着老卡塔帕在赛季结束时打个头名。”

===Original===
Young Heaton, with evident regret, told Larry that he would be unable to play in the Catalpa nine, but that it was his dearest wish that the club should be the champion club of the state. “So,” said he, “with my father’s consent, I have agreed to give my monthly allowance for the benefit of the club, and that will help make up a pool to pay expenses. We can’t get good players (I mean players to compete with Chicago and Springfield, and other large cities), without paying them something﻿—gate-money anyhow, and perhaps more.”

Larry said not a word. It was yet a new proposition, this of earning money as a professional ball player. Somehow it did not strike him pleasantly. But he listened respectfully while Mr. Heaton unfolded the plans that had been slowly matured since the signal defeat of the Catalpas, last October. They must organize a new nine. Some of the old players must be dropped, and two, Al and Lewis Morris, had already declined to play any longer. New men must be found to take their places. Would Larry join the new nine? Did he recommend any other players in the vicinity?

Larry’s ruddy face glowed as he walked up and down the little counting-room, thinking over the situation. Mr. Heaton watched the young man’s well-knit and graceful figure with admiration, and winked at Albert, as if to say, “That is your man. Get him if you can.”

===Chinese===
小希顿带着明显的遗憾告诉拉里，他打不成卡塔帕九人队了，但他最大的心愿，就是让这支俱乐部成为全州的冠军俱乐部。“所以，”他说，“我征得父亲同意，已经答应把我每月的零花钱都捐给俱乐部，好凑起一笔付开销的款子。不掏点钱——至少分点门票收入，兴许还要更多——是请不来好球员的（我是说，能跟芝加哥、斯普林菲尔德（Springfield）这些大城市较量的球员）。”

拉里一言不发。当职业棒球手挣钱，这对他还是个新课题，不知怎么的，他觉得不是滋味。但他恭恭敬敬地听着希顿先生铺陈那些计划——自打去年十月卡塔帕队惨败之后，这些计划便在慢慢酝酿成熟。他们必须重组一支新九人队。有些老队员必须裁掉，而阿尔和刘易斯·莫里斯两位已经宣布不再打了。必须找新人补他们的缺。拉里愿意加入新九人队吗？他可知道附近还有什么好手可以举荐？

拉里红润的脸放着光，在小账房里踱来踱去，把眼下的处境掂量来掂量去。希顿先生钦慕地打量着这年轻人结实的漂亮身板，朝阿尔伯特眨眨眼，仿佛在说：“这就是你要的人。能弄到手就弄到手。”

===Original===
“I’ll consider any offer that you make in behalf of the new nine, Mr. Heaton,” said Larry, “and if I were to suggest any other players from the Jonesvilles, I should like to say a good word for Sam Morrison and Neddie Ellis. Morrison is our first baseman, and Neddie is as good a pitcher as there is in the country, unless it is Charlie King. I hope your men don’t think of letting out Charlie?”

“Oh, no,” replied young Heaton, “they want him to stay, and he says that he’ll not only stay but will give in his share of the gate-money for the use of the club. Oh, Charlie’s clear grit, he is, and he’ll stand by the club,” said the young man, with friendly warmth, dashed with a little regret, perhaps, that family complications forbade him a similar sacrifice.

The details of the bargain could not be settled at once. Mr. Heaton and his son were the representatives of a company of public-spirited citizens who were bent on getting up a good baseball club. They could only secure Larry’s promise to wait for terms from them before accepting any other engagement, and to give them some hint as to what compensation he should expect. This last, however, Larry resolutely declined to do; and, after some debate, young Heaton exclaimed, “Well, hang it all, Larry! What’s the use beating round the bush! I think our folks have made up their minds that they will give you a share of the gate-money, say one eighth, and a salary of a thousand dollars for the season. Does that strike you favorably?”

===Chinese===
“你们代表新九人队提出的任何条件，我都愿意考虑，希顿先生，”拉里说，“要我从琼斯维尔队里举荐球员的话，我想替萨姆·莫里森和内迪·埃利斯说句好话。莫里森是我们的一垒手，内迪是全国数得着的投手，除了查利·金。你们的人没打算放走查利吧？”

“哦，没有，”小希顿答道，“大伙儿都想留他，他自己也说了，不但留下，还把自己那份门票收入也让给俱乐部用。哦，查利是条真汉子，他会跟俱乐部共进退的。”年轻人说着，语气友好而热切，或许又掺着一丝遗憾——家门里的牵绊，让他做不出同样的牺牲。

条件的细节不是马上能敲定的。希顿先生父子是一群热心公益、一心要办起一支好棒球俱乐部的镇民的代表。他们只求拉里答应：在接下别的聘约之前，先等他们的条件；再给他透个口风，说他想要多少报酬。这后一条，拉里断然回绝了；推让了一阵，小希顿嚷道：“咳，见鬼，拉里！拐弯抹角有什么用！我想我们那几位已经打定主意，要分给你一份门票收入，就说是八分之一吧，外加一赛季一千美元的薪水。这个你觉得怎么样？”

===Original===
Larry’s eyes shone as he said, “It strikes me as being more than I am worth.”

“Well, this is all informal and entirely between us, you know,” said Mr. Heaton. “You will keep the matter to yourself until we have reported to the rest of the committee, for there is a committee,” he added with a smile. And so the matter was concluded, and Larry, mounting his horse, with a cheery salutation to father and son standing in the mill-door, rode across the bridge into the November twilight, with a light heart.

The next day, Lewis Morris rode over to Sugar Grove to expostulate with Larry. He had heard that the Heatons had offered Larry one thousand dollars and one-eighth of the gate-money. “Now,” said he to Larry, “I cannot play with the nine, next season, neither can Al Heaton, and the chances are that Will Sprague will drop out, too. Charlie King does not need any pay or any income from the playing to induce him to go. So he will not want any gate-money. Geo. Buckner says he will go along as an extra man, and he will take neither salary nor gate-money. If we get Sam Morrison and Neddie Ellis, we shall have to pay them gate-money at least. But there will be, according to my figuring, only seven out of ten to draw on the gate-money, for Hiram Porter, I am sure, will decline to take anything for his services.”

===Chinese===
拉里的眼睛亮了起来，说：“我觉得这超出我的身价了。”

“咳，这都还不算数，天知地知你知我知，你明白的，”希顿先生说。“等我们向委员会其余的人汇报之前，这事你自己先搁在心里——是有一个委员会的。”他笑着补了一句。事情就这样定了。拉里翻身上马，向站在磨坊门口的父子俩欢快地道了别，轻快地打桥上骑进十一月的暮色里去。

第二天，刘易斯·莫里斯骑马到舒格格罗夫来劝拉里。他听说希顿父子给拉里开了一千美元外加八分之一门票收入的价码。“你听我说，”他对拉里说，“下个赛季我打不成球，阿尔·希顿也打不成，威尔·斯普拉格多半也要退出。查利·金用不着什么报酬或打球收入来劝他入伙，所以他不会要门票分成。乔治·巴克纳说，他愿意当个替补跟着走，薪水门票一概不要。咱们要是把萨姆·莫里森和内迪·埃利斯弄来，至少得分他们门票收入。可按我的算法，十个人里头顶多七个人分门票，因为海勒姆·波特，我敢肯定，会分文不取。”

===Original===
Larry expressed his entire satisfaction with the terms offered him by Mr. Heaton, on behalf of the new club. He was willing to do what he could, short of any great sacrifice, to make up a strong nine. He would take less salary, or less of the income of the club, if that were necessary to induce the best men to join it.

“That’s very good of you, Larry, old boy,” said Morris, heartily, “but you can’t afford to waste your summer playing baseball for nothing. I want them to take Bill Van Orman from the Dean County boys. How do you think he would do?”

“First-rate! First-rate!” cried Larry, with enthusiasm. “I do not think of another fellow on the river as good as he is as catcher, unless it is Al Heaton, and he is out of the question.”

===Chinese===
拉里表示，希顿先生代表新俱乐部开的条件，他完全满意。只要不做出大牺牲，他愿意尽其所能帮着凑成一支强硬的九人队。若非如此不能请来最好的人手，他情愿少拿些薪水，或少分些俱乐部的收入。

“你真够意思，拉里，老伙计，”莫里斯衷心地说，“可你不能把一个夏天白白赔在打棒球上。我想让他们从迪安县的小伙子里把比尔·范奥曼要过来。你看他行不行？”

“一流！一流！”拉里起劲地喊道。“这条河上下，除了阿尔·希顿，我想不出还有谁的捕手功夫赶得上他，而阿尔已经不可能了。”

===Original===
“Unless it is Larry Boyne,” said Morris, reproachfully. “You are a great sight better catcher than Bill Van Orman, and I should hope you would take that place if you were to go into the new Catalpa Nine.”

Larry protested that he had watched Van Orman’s catching for two seasons, and had made up his mind that he was the best man in that position that could be got, now that Al Heaton was out of the field. Would Van Orman serve at all?

“Oh, yes,” replied Morris. “All of the Dean County boys are just wild to get into the new nine. They are willing to play for Catalpa, and they don’t care whether they are in their own nine or in a new one. They drop all thoughts of rivalry, so far as the future is concerned.”

===Chinese===
“除非还有个拉里·博因，”莫里斯嗔怪道。“你当捕手比比尔·范奥曼强出一大截，你要是进新的卡塔帕九人队，我还指望你占那个位置呢。”

拉里坚持说，范奥曼的捕球他看了两个赛季，早已有数：既然阿尔·希顿退出了球场，他就是能请到的那个位置上最好的人。范奥曼到底肯不肯来？

“哦，肯，”莫里斯答道。“迪安县的小伙子们一个个都发疯似的想进新九人队。他们愿意为卡塔帕打球，不在乎是待在自家队里还是新队里。往后的日子长着呢，什么争强斗胜的心思，他们全撂下了。”

===Original===
As Lewis Morris cantered back from his visit to Sugar Grove, he met Cyrus Ayres, driving homeward from town, his lumber-wagon making a great din as it rattled and rumbled over the rough, frozen road. The two young men exchanged greetings as they passed, and Cyrus call out to Lewis something which the noise of the wagon drowned; so, turning back, he said, “What was that you were saying about Bill Van Orman?”

“Oh, I only said that Bill is to be catcher in the new nine. I was in Jase Elderkin’s store, just now, and he allowed that Bill would take anything the boys had a mind to give him. But Charlie King and Ben Burton said that Larry Boyne wouldn’t want to serve as catcher, if he did go into the new nine, and that Bill would be the next best man, and Larry would go on one of the bases. Say first base. How’s that, think ye?”

“I don’t like it,” said Lewis, “but we’ll see what we shall see. I am willing, so far as I am concerned, to leave it all to Larry. He has got a level head, and don’t you forget it.”

===Chinese===
刘易斯·莫里斯从舒格格罗夫回来，小跑着马，路上遇见赛勒斯·艾尔斯（Cyrus Ayres）赶着车从镇上回家，运木料的大车在那条冻得坑坑洼洼的路上咣当咣当，响成一片。两个年轻人错车时互相打了招呼，赛勒斯冲刘易斯喊了句什么，全被大车的响动淹了；于是他回过头喊道：“你刚才说比尔·范奥曼什么来着？”

“哦，我就是说，比尔要在新九人队里当捕手。方才我在贾森·埃尔德金（Jase Elderkin）的铺子里，他说比尔这人，小伙子们想给什么他就拿什么。可查利·金和本·伯顿说，拉里·博因就算进了新九人队，也不会想干捕手，比尔就是下一号最好的人选，拉里去守个垒——比方说一垒。你看这样着怎么样？”

“我不喜欢这样，”刘易斯说，“不过咱们走着瞧。就我而言，我愿意把这事全交给拉里。他脑袋清醒得很，你可别忘了这一点。”

===Original===
“Right you are,” responded Cyrus, as, giving the reins to his impatient team, he rattled noisily down the river road.

As he passed Judge Howell’s handsome house, Lewis looked up and caught the glance of Miss Alice, who was sitting in the window-seat, curled up on a big cushion, and scribbling something that seemed to puzzle her very much. The girl wrote, rewrote, erased and wrote again. Finally she held her work, somewhat blurred and scratchy as it was, at arm’s length, and said in soliloquy,

“I really think that is the very best thing that could be done! But I wonder what I put that young Irishman’s name at the head of the list for?”

===Chinese===
“说得对，”赛勒斯应道，把缰绳交给等得不耐烦的马队，一路轰隆隆地顺河边的路去了。

路过豪厄尔法官那座漂亮的宅子时，刘易斯抬头一望，正碰上爱丽丝小姐的目光。她坐在窗座上，蜷在一个大靠垫里，正涂写着什么把她难住了的东西。姑娘写了又写，改了又改，擦了再写。最后，她把那张已经涂得模糊潦草的纸拿到一臂远，自言自语道：

“我真觉得这是再好不过的安排了！可我干吗把那个爱尔兰小伙子的名字放在单子头一名呢？”

===Original===
With a faint pink tint suffusing her cheek, she drew a line through the name at the top of the page, wrote it at the bottom, and then laughed softly to herself. Just then Lewis Morris rode by, gallantly taking off his cap as he passed the house. If Mr. Lewis could have looked over Alice’s shoulder, he would have read this list of names:

- **S. Morrison**, L.F.

- **Neddie Ellis**, C.F.

- **Charlie King**, P.

- **Hart Stirling**, 2nd B.

- **John Brubaker**, R.F.

- **Hiram Porter**, 1st B.

- **Ben Burton**, S.S.

- **Wm. Van Orman**, 3rd B.

- **Lawrence Boyne**, Catcher

===Chinese===
一抹浅红浮上她的脸颊。她把页首的名字划掉，写到了最底下，然后自己轻轻笑了。恰在此时，刘易斯·莫里斯骑马经过，风度翩翩地摘帽向这座宅子致意。要是刘易斯先生能从爱丽丝肩后望上一眼，他就会看到这样一张名单：

- **萨姆·莫里森**，左野手

- **内迪·埃利斯**，中外野手

- **查利·金**，投手

- **哈特·斯特林**，二垒手

- **约翰·布鲁贝克**，右野手

- **海勒姆·波特**，一垒手

- **本·伯顿**，游击手

- **威廉·范奥曼**，三垒手

- **劳伦斯·博因**，捕手

===Original===
Alice concealed the paper in her pocket, as she saw her father drive up the road from the bridge. Then she took it out again with a pretty little air of determination, saying to herself. “My papa knows that I am so much interested in the new nine scheme, why shouldn’t I tell him that this is what I think about the reorganization?”

So, when the Judge, that night, drew his motherless child to his knee, she brought to him the list of players which she had made out.

“Perhaps you will think it mannish in me, papa,” she said, “but I have made out a list of the players in the new Catalpa nine. I have a whim that this is about the way they will be placed.”

===Chinese===
看见父亲的车从桥那头沿路驶来，爱丽丝把纸藏进了口袋。随即她又把它掏了出来，带着一副可爱的小小的决断神气，对自己说：“爸爸知道我对新九人队的事这么上心，我为什么不告诉他，这就是我对重组的想法呢？”

于是，当晚法官把没了娘的孩子搂到膝头时，她便把拟好的球员名单拿给了他。

“也许您会觉得我这份心思像个男孩子，爸爸，”她说，“可我把新卡塔帕九人队的球员名单拟出来了。我有个直觉，他们的位置差不多就会这么排。”

===Original===
The Judge took the crumpled and blurred paper, and running his eyes over it, said, “That is a good cast, as they say in the theaters, Alice; but don’t you think you are a little premature? The new nine is not yet formed, and until they begin to practice they can hardly tell where each player should be placed. I don’t pretend to know much about the game; not so much as my little daughter does, for example, but isn’t that about the way it strikes you?”

Alice admitted that her father was right. But she had given a great deal of thought to the matter. Everybody in the town was discussing this absorbing topic. And, out of all that she had heard, she had evolved this cast of characters, so to speak. Anticipating the story of the Catalpa nine a little, it may be said that Alice Howell’s list, although its features were known only to herself and her father, was adopted with two exceptions, Larry Boyne was chosen to the third base and Bill Van Orman took the position of catcher. But this was not done until far later in the winter, when the new nine was finally organized for the summer campaign.

===Chinese===
法官接过那张皱巴巴、涂得模糊的纸，目光扫了一遍，说：“阵容排得好，爱丽丝——用戏园子里的话说；不过你不觉得你有点操之过急吗？新九人队还没组建起来，不开始练球，谁也说不准每个球员该守哪个位置。我并不装作多懂这项运动；比方说，懂不过我的小女儿——可你不就是这么个看法吗？”

爱丽丝承认父亲说得对。可这件事她已琢磨了很久。镇上人人都在议论这个抓人的话题。可以说，从她听来的一切当中，她排出了这套出场人物。这里不妨把卡塔帕九人队的故事稍稍往前透一点：爱丽丝·豪厄尔的这份名单，尽管只有她自己和父亲知道内容，后来几乎全被采纳，只有两处例外——拉里·博因被安排到了三垒，捕手的位置给了比尔·范奥曼。不过那是那年冬天晚得多的时候才定下的，那时新九人队为夏季赛季完成了最后的组建。

## XVI The Conspiracy Laid Open / 第十六章 阴谋大白

===Original===
The Selbys kept their own counsel, although Tom burned to tell everybody whom he met not to bet with Hank Jackson on the baseball match; but, after pondering the matter in his mind, he came to the conclusion that if people would bet on a baseball game, they must run their own risks and chances. It would serve them right, he thought, if they did lose their money in this foolish fashion. The League, he knew, had enacted severe rules against gaming, and the influence of that example should be strengthened even if by the misfortunes of those who laid wagers.

So there would have been no suspicion of Hank’s complicity in any plot, if Mike had been able to keep a secret, but Mike adored “the lady operator” secretly and from afar. He submitted in silence and uncomplainingly to her rebuffs and scoldings for the sake of winning her regard. In a moment of confidence, he imparted to the object of his dumb worship the information that the cipher message which she had received for Jackson was “crooked.” The young lady was shocked. She had heard that Hank was going about town offering to bet against the Catalpa nine, and now she instantly divined what was going on, and was indignant accordingly. The fact that she had been the unconscious channel of communicating with the culprit did not lessen her wrath. Unhappily for Henry, he came to the office in the course of the afternoon, and the operator, as soon as she saw him, gave him a piece of her mind, to his great discomfiture. Hank, unlike his co-conspirator, did not attempt to deny anything, but tacitly admitted all that was charged against him by the irate young lady.

===Chinese===
塞尔比父子守口如瓶，虽然汤姆憋得难受，见谁都想劝一句：别跟汉克·杰克逊赌这场棒球赛；可他把这事在心里掂量了一番，得出的结论是：人们要肯在棒球赛上下注，那就得自担风险。要是他们真用这种蠢法子把钱输掉，那是活该，他想。他知道，联盟已经订了严厉的禁赌规则，这一先例的威慑就该加强，哪怕靠下注的人们的倒霉来加强也罢。

本来，汉克跟什么阴谋有牵连的事，本不会起任何疑心——只要迈克能守住秘密。可迈克偷偷地、远远地爱慕着那位“女报务员”。为了赢得她的青睐，她的冷脸和训斥他都默默忍受，毫无怨言。一时交心的当口，他把自己这份无言的倾慕的对象拉进了知情圈：她收下转给杰克逊的那封密码电报“有鬼”。年轻小姐大吃一惊。她早听说汉克满镇跑着开盘赌卡塔帕九人队输，这下立刻把事情猜了个透，也就义愤填膺。自己一直不知不觉地当着跟这桩罪案的传话管道，这一点也没让她少生气半分。亨利倒霉就倒霉在，当天下午他来了电报局，报务员一见到他，劈头盖脸就数落了他一顿，数落得他狼狈不堪。汉克不像他那位同谋，什么都不想抵赖，而是默不作声地认下了怒气冲冲的年轻小姐指控的全部罪名。

===Original===
After turning over in his mind the circumstances of the scrape into which he had been drawn, Master Jackson coolly sat down and wrote the following despatch to Ben Burton:

> The thing is blown. Look out for yourself.

> Henry J. Jackson.

===Chinese===
杰克逊少爷把自己被拖下水这桩祸事的来龙去脉在心里翻来覆去盘算了一阵，随即冷静地坐下，给本·伯顿写了下面这封电报：

> 事情败露。你自己当心。

> 亨利·J·杰克逊。

===Original===
It was this warning, received by Burton after the game was over, that put him on his guard when he was confronted with the despatch sent to Hiram Porter. Next day, when the town was alive with enthusiasm over the reception to the returning baseball club, Henry Jackson did not appear in any of the excited groups that accompanied the players from the depot to their clubrooms.

The hilarity of the day was somewhat dampened by the fact that one of the nine was a traitor, and that he must be disciplined, if the charge were proven against him. The evidence shown to the boys on their arrival was tolerably conclusive, but it was needful, as they thought, to secure an admission from either Ben or Henry that there had been collusion between them. Burton’s father, a worthy and honest miller, sought out Captain Hiram, and, with much grief, told him that Ben had written to him from Chicago, saying that he was going to Indiana on unexpected business, and that he would not be in Catalpa for some weeks to come. This, to the old gentleman, who had heard the flying reports to his son’s discredit, was a suspicious circumstance. He did not like to believe that Benjamin had done anything wrong, he said, but he was “afeard,” yes, he was “afeard.”

Judge Howell sent for Hank Jackson, and that young man, although at first disposed to be stubborn, finally broke down before the majesterial bearing of the Judge and told all that was needful to convict himself and Ben of having combined to make money by betting on the game between the Calumets and the Catalpas. Ben, he said, had suggested the trick, agreeing to throw the game, if Hank, and any other confederate whom he might select, would get the bets secured in Catalpa. Henry also thought that Ben had arranged to have a similar scheme at the same time played in Sandy Key, where he had a boon companion.

===Chinese===
正是这封赛后才送达伯顿手里的警告，让他在面对发给了海勒姆·波特的那封电报时存了戒心。第二天，全镇欢腾，热热闹闹迎接凯旋的棒球俱乐部，亨利·杰克逊却没有在任何一支兴奋的人堆里露面——那些人簇拥着球员们从车站一直送到俱乐部会所。

可是这一天的欢腾多少被浇了点凉水：九人队里出了一个叛徒，罪名若坐实，他就得受罚。球员们抵达时看到的证据已相当确凿，但他们觉得，还必须让本或亨利亲口承认两人之间有过勾结。伯顿的父亲是个正直老实的磨坊主，专程找到海勒姆队长，十分悲痛地告诉他，本从芝加哥给他来了信，说他因意外事务要去印第安纳，几个星期内不会回卡塔帕。对这位已经听到些有损儿子名声的流言的老先生来说，这是个可疑的迹象。他说，他不愿相信本杰明做了什么错事，可他“心里头发怵”，可不是嘛，他“心里头发怵”。

豪厄尔法官派人传唤汉克·杰克逊。那年轻人起初还想硬扛，最后在法官威严的气派面前垮了下来，把足以给他和本定罪的话都交代了：两人合谋，在卡柳梅特队和卡塔帕队的比赛上赌钱牟利。他说，这鬼主意是本出的：他负责打假球输掉比赛，条件是汉克和他挑中的任何同伙负责在卡塔帕把赌注都揽到手。亨利还认为，本同时安排了在桑迪基（Sandy Key）如法炮制一套同样的把戏——他在那儿有个酒肉知己。

===Original===
The story of the despatches was now clearly unravelled. Ben had sent a despatch to Henry Jackson directly after leaving the Chicago lodgings of the club, on the morning of the second day; subsequently, he had remembered that his friend in Sandy Key might be utilized as a fellow conspirator, and, just before the game was called, he had hurried off a despatch to him, also. Inquiries subsequently developed the fact that this was exactly what had been done.

While Henry was undergoing an examination in Judge Howell’s private office, the nine were in consultation. Presently, the door opened and the Judge and his unwilling prisoner appeared.

“Henry has decided to make a clean breast of this unhappy business, Captain Porter,” said the Judge. “Speak up like a man, Henry, and tell the gentlemen what you have told me.”

===Chinese===
电报的事如今水落石出。第二天一早，本刚离开俱乐部在芝加哥的住处，就给亨利·杰克逊发了封电报；随后他又想起桑迪基那位朋友可以拉来当同谋，于是在开赛前，又匆匆给他发了一封。事后查明，事实分毫不差正是如此。

亨利在豪厄尔法官的私人办公室里受审的时候，九人队正在隔壁合议。不一会儿，门开了，法官和他那位一百个不情愿的“囚徒”走了出来。

“亨利决定把这桩不幸的事一五一十全说出来，波特队长，”法官说。“像个男子汉那样大声说，亨利，把你跟我说的都告诉这几位先生。”

===Original===
With downcast eyes and a sullen manner, Hank fumbled with his cap, and mumbled his story, but without omitting anything relevant to the case. He was heard in silence, although “The Lily,” whose eyes glared vengefully at the culprit, with difficulty restrained himself. And when the door closed behind the Judge and the criminal, the ungentle William gave a roar of rage that astonished first, and then set the club off into fits of laughter, in spite of the solemnity of the occasion.

“Well, what is the result of your deliberations, Mr. Boyne?” asked a brisk and somewhat seedy young man, as the boys came down from their clubroom. Pulling out a note book and moistening a pencil at his lip, as he spoke, he continued, “Shocking case of depravity on the part of young Burton. Quite a small sensation, on my word. Small, small for a big city, but really sensational for Catalpa, you know. Ha! ha!” and the young gentleman laughed at his little sally.

“Great powers!” was Larry’s exclamation. “You are not going to print anything about this disgraceful business in *The Leaf*, are you?”

===Chinese===
汉克垂着眼皮，一脸不服，手指摆弄着帽子，把事情咕哝了一遍，倒也没漏掉任何与案情相关的细节。大家静静听着，只有“百合”双眼喷着复仇的火，好不容易才按捺住自己。等法官和罪犯的身影消失在门外，这位不温柔的威廉发出一声怒吼，先吓了众人一跳，随即惹得全队哄堂大笑，尽管这个场合本来何等严肃。

“怎么样，博因先生，你们商议出什么结果了？”小伙子们从俱乐部会所下来时，一个麻利却有点寒酸的年轻人问道。他一边说话，一边掏出笔记本，舔了舔铅笔头，接着说：“小伯顿这桩堕落案，骇人听闻啊。说真的，也算一场不大不小的轰动。小，对大城市来说是小，可对卡塔帕来说，实在是轰动了，你明白。哈！哈！”这位年轻先生为自己这句妙语笑了起来。

“我的老天！”拉里失声喊道。“你们不会要把这件丢人现眼的事登到《叶报》上吧，会吗？”

===Original===
“Why, certainly, Mr. Boyne. I have a lovely article written up. We only want the action of the club to round it off, give it completeness as it were, and there you are.”

“Oh, that would be very bad!” cried Larry. “I don’t mind your saying in the paper that Mr. Burton has been obliged to leave the club, and that we have supplied his place by placing Mr. Albert Heaton at shortstop, Mr. William Sprague being unable to play, on account of having sprained his thumb while practicing with the club. But don’t let us disgrace the town and the club by making public Ben Burton’s treachery!”

A new light seemed to dawn on the reporter’s mind, and he sucked his pencil reflectively. Finally, he brightened up and said, “Well, you must go and see Mr. Downey. He was reckoning that we would have a first-class story out of this. I have no authority in the premises. I am only an humble scribbler, a mere local-items, so to speak. But a word from you to the editor-in-chief, Mr. Boyne, will have its effect. Yes, it will have its effect. But that is a lovely story spoiled, Mr. Boyne.”

===Chinese===
“嗨，当然登，博因先生。一篇漂亮文章我都写好了。只差俱乐部的处理决定来收个尾，好让文章十全十美，就这么回事。”

“哦，那可就糟透了！”拉里喊道。“报纸上说伯顿先生不得不离开了俱乐部，说他空出的位置已由阿尔伯特·希顿补上、改打游击手——因为威廉·斯普拉格先生随队练球时扭伤了拇指，无法出战——这些我都不介意。可别让我们把本·伯顿的背叛公之于众，丢了全镇和俱乐部的脸！”

记者脑子里似乎亮起了一盏新的灯，他若有所思地咂着铅笔。末了，他爽朗起来，说：“唉，那你们得去见见唐尼先生。他还指望靠这个弄一篇一流报道呢。这事上我做不了主。我不过是个卑微的涂鸦匠，说白了就是个跑本地简讯的。可您跟主编说一句话，博因先生，管保有用。是的，管保有用。只是，一篇漂亮报道就这么泡汤了，博因先生。”

===Original===
Mr. Downey, when sought in the office of *The Leaf*, was deeply chagrined to learn that the members of the baseball club were unwilling that anything should appear in next morning’s paper regarding the unfortunate affair in which Ben Burton was involved. News was news, he said, and, what was more, news was very scarce at this season of the year. Harvesting was not wholly completed. No shooting matches had been yet arranged, and there was a frightful dullness throughout the county. His hated rival, *The Dean County Banner*, would be almost certain to get hold of the affair, and, as *The Banner* was a semiweekly, instead of a daily, like *The Leaf*, he would have time to work it up into that dime novel sensation to which *The Banner* was so addicted. And the editor of *The Leaf* curled his lip with fine contempt for his rival.

But the arguments of the young men overwhelmed the generous mind of the editor, who, on condition that similar persuasion should be brought to bear on the editor of *The Banner*, consigned to the wastebasket, but with a pang, the highly-seasoned narrative which his reporter had prepared.

The substitution of Albert Heaton for the derelict Ben Burton was not effected without a struggle. His mother, firm in her conviction that baseball was not an aristocratic game, held out against the arguments of her husband and her son, until Judge Howell, accidentally meeting her on the street, one day, craftily won her over by informing her that he wished that he had a son big enough to play baseball. He was sure that the honor and the glory of defeating the crack baseball club of the State would now fall to the Catalpa nine. It would be a great day for Catalpa when this happened.

The good lady surrendered. What Judge Howell thought and said seemed to her like law and gospel, social and moral. Albert joyfully received consent to play with the nine﻿—“just for this once.”

===Chinese===
在《叶报》编辑部找到唐尼先生时，他一听说棒球俱乐部的会员们不愿让明早的报上出现任何关于本·伯顿那桩不幸事件的报道，懊恼得不行。他说，新闻就是新闻，何况眼下这个时节，新闻实在稀罕。收割还没收完，射击比赛一场也没排上，全县闷得吓人。他那可恨的对头《迪安县旗帜报》（The Dean County Banner）十有八九会抓住这件事不放，而且《旗帜报》不像《叶报》是日报，是份半周报，有的是工夫把它炮制成那种《旗帜报》最上瘾的廉价小说式轰动新闻。《叶报》的主编对这位同行撇了撇嘴，一脸精致的轻蔑。

但年轻人们的一番道理压倒了主编那颗宽宏的心；他提了一个条件——得有人去把同样的说辞同样地说动《旗帜报》的主编——然后，忍着一阵心痛，把他那位记者写好的这篇重口味的纪实丢进了字纸篓。

用阿尔伯特·希顿顶替渎职的本·伯顿，也不是没经过一番争斗。他母亲坚信棒球不是贵族运动，任凭丈夫和儿子怎么劝都不松口；直到有一天，豪厄尔法官在街上偶然遇见她，耍了个心眼儿对她说，他真希望自己也有个够个子打棒球的儿子，这才把她争取了过来。法官确信，打败全州王牌棒球俱乐部的荣誉与光彩，这回非卡塔帕九人队莫属。到那一天，将是卡塔帕盛大的日子。

这位好心的太太投降了。豪厄尔法官所想所说，在她听来就像社会与道德层面的法律和福音。阿尔伯特欢天喜地地得到了随九人队打球的许可——“就这一次。”
